## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. Slush﻿—forged Bank of England notes. ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. Slush（黑话：伪钞）——伪造的英格兰银行钞票。↩︎

## Sheer Melodrama / 纯粹的情节剧

===Original===
It was Mr. Reeder who planned the raid on Tommy Fenalow’s snide shop and worked out all the details except the composition of the raiding force. Tommy had a depot at Golders Green whither trusted agents came, purchasing Treasury notes for £7 10s. per hundred, or £70 a thousand. Only experts could tell the difference between Tommy’s currency and that authorised by and printed for H.M. Treasury. They were the right shades of brown and green, the numbers were of issued series, the paper was exact. They were printed in Germany at £3 a thousand, and Tommy made thousands percent profit.

Mr. Reeder discovered all about Tommy’s depot in his spare time, and reported the matter to his chief, the Director of Public Prosecutions. From Whitehall to Scotland Yard is two minutes’ walk, and in just that time the information got across.

“Take Inspector Greyash with you and superintend the raid,” were his instructions.

He left the inspector to make all the arrangements, and amongst those who learnt of the projected coup was a certain detective officer who made more money from questionable associations than he did from Government. This officer “blew” the raid to Tommy, and when Mr. Reeder and his bold men arrived at Golders Green, there was Tommy and three friends playing a quiet game of auction bridge, and the only Treasury notes discoverable were veritable old masters.

===Chinese===
策划突袭汤米·芬纳洛（Tommy Fenalow）伪钞铺子、并把除行动队人员组成之外的一切细节都打理周详的，正是里德先生。汤米在戈尔德斯格林（Golders Green）设着一处仓库，受信任的主顾从四方前来，每一百英镑国库券收 7 英镑 10 先令，每一千英镑收 70 英镑。只有专家才分辨得出汤米的钞票同财政部授权印制的国库券有什么两样：棕、绿两色深浅无误，编号属于已发行序列，纸张也分毫不差。这些钞票在德国印制，每千张成本3英镑，汤米坐享的是百分之几千的利润。

里德先生利用闲暇把汤米那处仓库的底细查了个通透，将此事呈报给他的上司——检察总长（Director of Public Prosecutions）。从白厅到苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）不过两分钟脚程，这消息传递得也恰恰只用了这点工夫。

“带上格雷亚什（Greyash）探长，亲自督办这次突袭。”——这便是上司的指示。

具体安排他都交给了探长去办，而在风闻这次筹划中行动的人里，有一名警探，他从不清白的交际中捞到的钱比政府发给他的薪水还多。这位警探把突袭行动“漏”给了汤米。待里德先生带着他那些勇猛的部下赶到戈尔德斯格林，只见汤米正同三位朋友安安静静打着一局竞叫桥牌，而能搜出来的国库券，张张都是不折不扣的真旧票。

===Original===
“It is a pity,” sighed J. G. when they reached the street; “a great pity. Of course I hadn’t the least idea that Detective-Constable Wilshore was in our party. He is﻿—er﻿—not quite loyal.”

“Wilshore?” asked the officer, aghast. “Do you mean he ‘blew’ the raid to Tommy?”

Mr. Reeder scratched his nose and said gently, that he thought so.

“He has quite a big income from various sources﻿—by the way, he banks with the Midland and Derbyshire, and his account is in his wife’s maiden name. I tell you this in case﻿—er﻿—it may be useful.”

===Chinese===
“真可惜，”回到街上，J. G. 叹了口气，“太可惜了。我当然万万没有料到，侦缉警士威尔肖尔（Wilshore）也编在我们这一队里。他这个人——呃——忠心有点欠奉。”

“威尔肖尔？”警官骇然问，“您是说他把行动‘漏’给了汤米？”

里德先生挠了挠鼻子，温和地说，他正是这么想的。

“他的进项相当可观，来路五花八门——顺便说一句，他把钱存在米德兰与德比郡银行（Midland and Derbyshire），户头开的是他妻子的娘家姓。我告诉您这些，以防——呃——往后用得着。”

===Original===
It was useful enough to secure the summary ejection of the unfaithful Wilshore from the force, but it was not sufficiently useful to catch Tommy, whose parting words were:

“You’re clever, Reeder; but you’ve got to be lucky to catch me!”

Tommy was in the habit of repeating this scrap of conversation to such as were interested. It was an encounter of which he was justifiably proud, for few dealers in “slush” and “snide” have ever come up against Mr. J. G. and got away with it.

“It’s worth a thousand pounds to me﻿—ten thousand! I’d pay that money to make J. G. look sick, anyway, the old dog! I guess the Yard will think twice before it tries to shop me again, and that’s the real kick in the raid. J. G.’s name is Jonah at headquarters, and if I can do anything to help, it will be mud!”

To a certain Ras Lal Punjabi, an honoured (and paying) guest, Mr. Fenalow told this story, with curious results.

===Chinese===
这条消息足以让不忠的威尔肖尔被当场逐出警队，却不足以逮住汤米。汤米临别撂下的话是：

“你是聪明，里德；可要抓我，光聪明不行——你还得走运！”

汤米惯于把这段对话的只言片语讲给感兴趣的人听。这次交锋他引以为豪，也确实有资本自豪，因为在“伪钞”（slush）、“假票”（snide）这一行里，撞上 J. G. 本人还能全身而退的，没有几个。

“这一下对我价值一千镑——一万镑！只要能让 J. G. 当众出丑，这钱我出得起，反正那只老滑头！我看苏格兰场下回再想出卖我之前，可得三思，这才是这次突袭里最痛快的地方。J. G. 在场总部就是个‘扫帚星’，我要是能搭上一把手，准叫他抹一身泥！”

芬纳洛先生把这段故事讲给某位拉斯·拉尔·旁遮普（Ras Lal Punjabi）听——此人是他一位受礼遇的（而且是付钱的）客人——结果非同小可。

===Original===
A good wine tastes best in its own country, and a man may drink sherry by the cask in Jerez de la Frontena and take no ill, whereas if he attempted so much as a bottle in Fleet Street, he would suffer cruelly. So also does the cigarette of Egypt preserve its finest bouquet for such as smoke it in the lounge of a Cairo hotel.

Crime is yet another quantity which does not bear transplanting. The American safe-blower may flourish in France just so long as he acquires by diligent study, and confines himself to, the Continental method. It is possible for the European thief to gain a fair livelihood in oriental countries, but there is no more tragic sight in the world than the Eastern mind endeavouring to adapt itself to the complexities of European roguery.

===Chinese===
好酒在本土才最见风味。在赫雷斯-德拉弗龙特拉（Jerez de la Frontena），一个人论桶喝雪利酒也安然无恙；可若到了舰队街（Fleet Street），哪怕只喝一瓶，也要狠狠受一场罪。埃及的香烟也是如此——顶好的香气，专留给在开罗哪家旅馆休息室里抽它的人。

犯罪也是一样经不起移栽的东西。美国撬保险箱的窃贼到了法国，只要肯下苦功学会、并且严格恪守欧陆手法，照样能混得风生水起。欧洲的贼到东方国家谋一份体面生计，也不是不可能；可世上再没有比东方头脑硬要适应欧洲骗术的千头万绪更可悲的景象了。

===Original===
Ras Lal Punjabi enjoyed a reputation in Indian police circles as the cleverest native criminal India had ever produced. Beyond a short term in Poona Jail, Ras Lal had never seen the interior of a prison, and such was his fame in native circles that, during this short period of incarceration, prayers for his deliverance were offered at certain temples, and it was agreed that he would never have been convicted at all but for some pretty hard swearing on the part of the police commissioner sahib﻿—and anyway, all sahibs hang together, and it was a European judge who sent him down.

He was a general practitioner of crime, with a leaning towards specialisation in jewel thefts. A man of excellent and even gentlemanly appearance, with black and shiny hair parted at the side and curling up over one brow in an inky wave, he spoke English, Hindustani and Tamil very well indeed, had a sketchy knowledge of the law (on his visiting cards was the inscription “Failed LL.B.”) and a very full acquaintance with the science of precious stones.

During Mr. Ras Las Punjabi’s brief rest in Poona, the police commissioner sahib, whose unromantic name was Smith, married a not very good-looking girl with a lot of money. Smith Sahib knew that beauty was only skin deep and that she had a kind heart, which is notoriously preferable to the garniture of coronets. It was honestly a love match. Her father owned jute mills in Calcutta, and on festive occasions, such as the Governor-General’s ball, she carried several lakhs of rupees on her person; but even rich people are loved for themselves alone.

===Chinese===
拉斯·拉尔·旁遮普在印度警界享有盛名，公认为印度有史以来所出产的最聪明的土著罪犯。除了在浦那（Poona）监狱短期服刑一回，拉斯·拉尔从未见识过监狱的内部。他在土人圈子里的名望大到这般地步：那段短短的囹圄岁月里，有几座庙宇专门为他的早日脱难上香祈祷；众人一口咬定，若非警察总监萨希布（sahib，对欧洲老爷的尊称）狠狠作了些厉害的伪证，他根本不会被定罪——何况萨希布们本就官官相护，判他入狱的还是一位欧洲法官。

他是犯罪行当的全科通才，又偏重珠宝盗窃这一门专科。此人仪表堂皇，甚至堪称绅士气派：乌黑发亮的头发从侧边分开，在一侧眉梢卷起一道墨色的波浪。他的英语、印度斯坦语和泰米尔语都说得极好，法律知识只得皮毛（名片上印着一行字：“法学士考试落第”（Failed LL.B.）），对宝石这门学问却精通得很。

拉斯·拉尔·旁遮普在浦那小住的当口，那位警察总监萨希布——他的名字毫不浪漫，姓史密斯（Smith）——娶了一位其貌不扬却坐拥巨资的姑娘。史密斯萨希布深知美貌不过一层皮，而她心地仁厚——世人都晓得，这可比冠冕上的珠玉金贵得多。这诚然是一桩以爱情为基础的婚姻。她父亲在加尔各答（Calcutta）开着黄麻纺织厂，逢上喜庆日子——比如总督舞会——她一身上下就披挂着几十万卢比；不过，就连有钱人，也有人是单单为了他们本人而爱他们的。

===Original===
Ras Lal owed his imprisonment to an unsuccessful attempt he had made upon two strings of pearls the property of the lady in question, and when he learnt, on his return to freedom, that Smith Sahib had married the resplendent girl and had gone to England, he very naturally attributed the hatred and bitterness of Smith Sahib to purely personal causes, and swore vengeance.

Now in India the business of every man is the business of his servants. The preliminary inquiries, over which an English or American jewel thief would spend a small fortune, can be made at the cost of a few annas. When Ras Lal came to England he found that he had overlooked this very important fact.

Smith, sahib and memsahib, were out of town; they were, in fact, on the high seas en route for New York when Ras Lal was arrested on the conventional charge of “being a suspected person.” Ras had shadowed the Smiths’ butler, and, having induced him to drink, had offered him immense sums to reveal the place, receptacle, drawer, safe, box or casket wherein Mrs. Commissioner Smith’s jewels were kept. His excuse for asking, namely, that he had had a wager with his brother that the jewels were kept under the Memsahib’s bed, showed a lamentable lack of inventive power. The butler, an honest man, though a drinker of beer, informed the police. Ras Lal and his friend and assistant Ram were arrested, brought before a magistrate, and would have been discharged but for the fact that Mr. J. G. Reeder saw the record of the case and was able to supply from his own files very important particulars of the dark man’s past. Therefore Mr. Ras Lal was sent down to hard labour for six months, but, what was more maddening, the story of his ignominious failure was, he guessed, broadcast throughout India.

===Chinese===
拉斯·拉尔那场牢狱之灾，起因于他对前述那位女士的两串珍珠下过一回手，没有得逞。待他重获自由，听说史密斯萨希布娶了那位珠光宝气的姑娘并已赴英，便顺理成章地认定史密斯萨希布对他的仇视怨毒纯属私人挟怨，于是发誓报复。

须知在印度，每个人的事务都是他仆人们的事务。英国或美国的珠宝窃贼要花一小笔家财去做的初步打探，在印度只消几个安那（anna，旧时印度铜币）便能办妥。拉斯·拉尔到了英国才发现，自己恰恰漏算了这一条至关紧要的道理。

史密斯夫妇——萨希布和梅姆萨希布（memsahib，太太）——双双出门在外；事实上，拉斯·拉尔以“身为可疑分子”这条惯例罪名被捕时，他们正在横渡大洋赴纽约的途中。拉斯盯过史密斯家的管家，把他灌醉之后许以重金，要他说出史密斯夫人的珠宝究竟存放在哪个地方、哪种容器、抽屉、保险柜、匣子或首饰盒里。他搭话的由头——说是同兄弟打赌，赌珠宝就藏在梅姆萨希布的床底下——足见其编造才能可怜得很。管家虽嗜啤酒，却是条诚实的汉子，报了警。拉斯·拉尔连同他的朋友兼助手拉姆（Ram）一起被捕，被押到治安官面前，本可当庭开释，偏偏 J. G. 里德先生见到了本案的卷宗，还能从自己档库里调出这位黑肤绅士过往的若干重要细节。于是拉斯·拉尔先生被判六个月苦役；更叫他发疯的是，他猜度，这桩丢尽脸面的失败已然传遍全印度。

===Original===
This was the thought which distracted him in his lonely cell at Wormwood Scrubbs. What would India think of him?﻿—he would be the scorn of the bazaars, “the mocking point of third-rate mediocrities,” to use his own expression. And automatically he switched his hate from Smith Sahib to one Mr. J. G. Reeder. And his hate was very real, more real because of the insignificance and unimportance of this Reeder Sahib, whom he likened to an ancient cow, a sneaking weasel, and other things less translatable. And in the six months of his durance he planned desperate and earnest acts of reprisal.

Released from prison, he decided that the moment was not ripe for a return to India. He wished to make a close study of Mr. J. G. Reeder and his habits, and, being a man with plenty of money, he could afford the time, and, as it happened, could mix business with pleasure.

Mr. Tommy Fenalow found means of getting in touch with the gentleman from the Orient whilst he was in Wormwood Scrubbs, and the handsome limousine that met Ras Lal at the gates of the Scrubbs when he came out of jail was both hired and occupied by Tommy, a keen business man, who had been offered by his German printer a new line of one-hundred-rupee notes that might easily develop into a most profitable sideline.

===Chinese===
在沃姆伍德·斯克拉布斯（Wormwood Scrubbs）监狱的单人牢房里，搅得他神魂不宁的正是这个念头。印度会怎么看他？——他要沦为集市的笑柄，“三流庸才们的笑柄”——这是他自己的说法。于是他的仇恨自动转了向，从史密斯萨希布转到了 J. G. 里德先生身上。这仇恨真实得很，而且正因为这位里德萨希布其貌不扬、无足轻重，反倒恨得愈发真切——他把此人比作一头老母牛、一只鬼祟的黄鼠狼，还有种种更难翻译的东西。在六个月的拘押里，他筹划下一桩桩狠辣而认真的报复行动。

出狱之后，他断定回印度的时机尚未成熟。他要细细研究 J. G. 里德先生其人及其习性。好在他有的是钱，耗得起工夫，而且机缘凑巧，还能公私兼顾。

汤米·芬纳洛先生趁这位东方绅士还蹲在沃姆伍德·斯克拉布斯的时候，就设法搭上了线。拉斯·拉尔出狱那天，在监狱大门口迎候他的那辆漂亮豪华轿车，正是汤米雇下并亲自乘坐的——这位精明的生意人刚从他的德国印制商手里接过一批新出的百元卢比假钞，不难发展成一门极为赚钱的副业。

===Original===
“You come along and lodge at my expense, boy,” said the sympathetic Tommy, who was very short, very stout, and had eyes that bulged like a pug dog’s. “You’ve been badly treated by old Reeder, and I’m going to tell you a way of getting back on him, with no risk and a ninety percent profit. Listen, a friend of mine﻿—”

It was never Tommy who had snide for sale: invariably the hawker of forged notes was a mysterious “friend.”

So Ras was lodged in a service flat which formed part of a block owned by Mr. Fenalow, who was a very rich man indeed. Some weeks after this, Tommy crossed St. James’s Street to intercept his old enemy.

===Chinese===
“跟我来吧，吃住全记在我的账上，小伙子。”富于同情心的汤米说。他个子极矮，身材极胖，一双眼睛鼓得像哈巴狗。“老里德把你坑苦了，我这就教你一条报复他的路子，毫无风险，还有九成利。听着，我有个朋友——”

兜售假票的从来不是汤米本人：沿街叫卖伪钞的，永远是一位神秘的“朋友”。

就这样，拉斯住进了一套服务式公寓——那是芬纳洛先生名下一片楼群中的一套；芬纳洛先生委实是个非常有钱的人。此后过了几个星期，汤米穿过圣詹姆斯街（St. James’s Street），去截他的老对头。

===Original===
“Good morning, Mr. Reeder.”

Mr. J. G. Reeder stopped and turned back.

“Good morning, Mr. Fenalow,” he said, with that benevolent solicitude which goes so well with a frock coat and square-toed shoes. “I am glad to see that you are out again, and I do trust that you will now find a more﻿—er﻿—legitimate outlet for your undoubted talents.”

Tommy went angrily red.

===Chinese===
“早上好，里德先生。”

J. G. 里德先生止步回身。

“早上好，芬纳洛先生。”他说，带着那种慈眉善目的关切——这副神气同长礼服、方头鞋正相得益彰。“很高兴看到您又出来走动了，我也确实希望，您从今往后能为您的过人才干找一条更——呃——更正当的出路。”

汤米气得涨红了脸。

===Original===
“I haven’t been in ‘stir’ and you know it, Reeder! It wasn’t for want of trying on your part. But you’ve got to be something more than clever to catch me﻿—you’ve got to be lucky! Not that there’s anything to catch me over﻿—I’ve never done a crook thing in my life, as you well know.”

He was so annoyed that the lighter exchanges of humour he had planned slipped from his memory.

He had an appointment with Ras Lal, and the interview was entirely satisfactory. Mr. Ras Lal made his way that night to an uncomfortably situated rendezvous and there met his new friend.

===Chinese===
“我没蹲过号子，这你清楚，里德！倒不是你没往这上头使劲。可要逮我，光聪明不够——你还得走运！再说压根儿没什么可逮的——我这辈子没干过一件邪门歪道的事，这你心里有数。”

他气成这样，原先盘算好的那些轻松诙谐的寒暄全忘到了脑后。

他还同拉斯·拉尔有个约会，会面圆满之至。当天夜里，拉斯·拉尔先生摸到一处位置蹊跷、令人浑身不自在的碰头地点，见到了他的新朋友。

===Original===
“This is the last place in the world old man Reeder would dream of searching,” said Tommy enthusiastically, “and if he did he would find nothing. Before he could get into the building, the stuff would be put out of sight.”

“It is a habitation of extreme convenience,” said Ras Lal.

“It is yours, boy,” replied Tommy magnificently. “I only keep this place to get-in and put-out. The stuff’s not here for an hour and the rest of the time the store’s empty. As I say, old man Reeder has gotta be something more than clever﻿—he’s gotta be lucky!”

===Chinese===
“这是全世界里德老头做梦也想不到要搜的最后一个地方，”汤米兴冲冲地说，“就算他梦到了，也搜不出什么。他人还没进楼，货早藏得没影儿了。”

“这是一处便利至极的居所。”拉斯·拉尔说。

“归你了，小伙子。”汤米答得豪爽，“我留这地方只为收货发货。货在这儿从不搁上一个钟头，其余时候库房空空荡荡。还是那句老话：里德老头光聪明不够——他还得走运！”

===Original===
At parting he handed his client a key, and with that necessary instrument tendered a few words of advice and warning.

“Never come here till late. The police patrol passes the end of the road at ten, one o’clock and four. When are you leaving for India?”

“On the twenty-third,” said Ras, “by which time I shall have uttered a few reprisals on that cad Reeder.”

“I shouldn’t like to be in his shoes,” said Tommy, who could afford to be sycophantic, for he had in his pocket two hundred pounds’ worth of real money which Ras had paid in advance for a vaster quantity of money which was not so real.

===Chinese===
临别时他递给客户一把钥匙，随同这件必需的物件，还奉上几句忠告与警诫。

“千万等天黑透了再来。警察巡逻队十点、一点、四点路过路口。你几时动身回印度？”

“二十三号，”拉斯说，“到那天，我对里德那卑鄙小人的几桩报复也该做完了。”

“换了我是他，可有的受了。”汤米说。他有本钱这么谄媚——他口袋里正揣着两百英镑货真价实的票子，是拉斯预付的定金，换来的是一大批不怎么真实的钞票。

===Original===
It was a few days after this that Ras Lal went to the Orpheum Theatre, and it was no coincidence that he went there on the same night that Mr. Reeder escorted a pretty lady to the same place of amusement.

When Mr. J. G. Reeder went to the theatre (and his going at all was contingent upon his receiving a complimentary ticket) he invariably chose a melodrama, and preferably a Drury Lane melodrama, where to the thrill of the actors’ speeches was added the amazing action of wrecked railway trains, hair-raising shipwrecks and terrific horse-races in which the favourite won by a nose. Such things may seem wildly improbable to blasé dramatic critics﻿—especially favourites winning﻿—but Mr. Reeder saw actuality in all such presentations.

Once he was inveigled into sitting through a roaring farce, and was the only man in the house who did not laugh. He was, indeed, such a depressing influence that the leading lady sent a passionate request to the manager that “the miserable-looking old man in the middle of the front row” should have his money returned and be requested to leave the theatre. Which, as Mr. Reeder had come in on a free ticket, placed the manager in a very awkward predicament.

===Chinese===
此后没几天，拉斯·拉尔去了奥菲姆剧院（Orpheum Theatre）；他与护送一位俏丽女士光临同一处消遣场所的里德先生同夜而至，绝非巧合。

J. G. 里德先生上剧院（他上不上剧院，全看有没有人奉送赠票）必定挑情节剧，最好还是德鲁里巷（Drury Lane）式的情节剧——在那儿，演员道白的惊险之外，还添着火车出轨的惊人场面、令人毛发倒竖的海难，以及热门马以一鼻之差夺魁的火爆赛马。这类事情在腻味的剧评家看来未免荒诞不经——尤其是热门马夺魁——可里德先生在这一切演出里看见的全是真实。

有一回他被诓去从头坐到尾看完一出轰闹的滑稽戏，成了全场唯一没笑的观众。他实在是一股叫人泄气的气场，害得女主角激愤地上书经理，请求给“前排正中那个一脸苦相的老头”退票，并请他离场。而里德先生本是持赠票入场，这一来就把经理搁进了一个非常难堪的窘境。

===Original===
Invariably he went unaccompanied, for he had no friends, and fifty-two years had come and gone without bringing to his life romance or the melting tenderness begot of dreams. In some manner Mr. Reeder had become acquainted with a girl who was like no other girl with whom he had been brought into contact. Her name was Belman, Margaret Belman, and he had saved her life, though this fact did not occur to him as frequently as the recollection that he had imperilled that life before he had saved it. And he had a haunting sense of guilt for quite another reason.

He was thinking of her one day﻿—he spent his life thinking about people, though the majority of these were less respectable than Miss Margaret Belman. He supposed that she would marry the very good-looking young man who met her street car at the corner of the Embankment every morning and returned with her to the Lewisham High Road every night. It would be a very nice wedding, with hired motorcars, and the vicar himself performing the ceremony, and a wedding breakfast provided by the local caterer, following which bride and bridegroom would be photographed on the lawn surrounded by their jovial but unprepossessing relatives. And after this, one specially hired car would take them to Eastbourne for an expensive honeymoon. And after that all the humdrum and scrapings of life, rising through villadom to a little car of their own and Saturday afternoon tennis parties.

===Chinese===
他向来独往独来，因为他没有朋友；五十二年岁月来了又去，不曾给他的生命带来罗曼史，也不曾带来由梦孕育的那种令人心软的柔情。也不知怎的，里德先生结识了一位姑娘，此女同他打过交道的任何姑娘都不一样。她姓贝尔曼（Belman），玛格丽特·贝尔曼（Margaret Belman）。他救过她的命，不过这一事实在他心头浮现的频次，远不及另一段记忆——在救她之前，他曾置她于险境。而且出于全然另外的缘由，他心里还萦绕着一种挥之不去的负罪感。

一天他正想着她——他一辈子都在琢磨人，尽管这些人大多不如玛格丽特·贝尔曼小姐体面。他料想她终会嫁给那个非常英俊的年轻人：此人每天早晨在泰晤士河堤街角接她下电车，每晚又陪她回到刘易舍姆大街（Lewisham High Road）。那将是一场很美满的婚礼：租来的汽车，教区牧师亲自主礼，喜宴由本地酒席铺承办；礼成之后，新郎新娘在草坪上留影，四周围着快快活活却其貌不扬的亲戚。再往后，一辆专门雇来的车载他们去伊斯特本（Eastbourne）度一趟花销不菲的蜜月。再往后，便是生活的平淡与营营汲汲，从小别墅人家一步步爬到一辆自家的小汽车、一场场周六下午的网球会。

===Original===
Mr. Reeder sighed deeply. How much more satisfactory was the stage drama, where all the trouble begins in the first act and is satisfactorily settled in the last. He fingered absently the two slips of green paper that had come to him that morning. Row A, seats 17 and 18. They had been sent by a manager who was under some obligation to him. The theatre was the Orpheum, home of transpontine drama, and the play was *The Fires of Vengeance*. It looked like being a pleasant evening.

He took an envelope from the rack, addressed it to the box office, and had begun to write the accompanying letter returning the surplus voucher, when an idea occurred to him. He owed Miss Margaret Belman something, and the debt was on his conscience. He had once, for reasons of expediency, described her as his wife. This preposterous claim had been made to appease a mad woman, it is true, but it had been made. She was now holding a good position﻿—a secretaryship at one of the political headquarters, for which post she had to thank Mr. J. G. Reeder, if she only knew it.

===Chinese===
里德先生深深叹了口气。舞台上的戏多么叫人满意啊——一切祸端起于第一幕，又在末一幕得个圆满收场。他心不在焉地摩挲着那天早上寄到的两张绿纸条。A排，17、18号。票是一位受过他恩惠的经理送的。剧院是奥菲姆，泰晤士河南岸式情节剧的大本营，戏码是《复仇之火》（*The Fires of Vengeance*）。看来会是一个愉快的夜晚。

他从信架上取过一只信封，写上售票处的地址，已动手写随函，要把多余的那张票退回去，忽然起了一个念头。他欠玛格丽特·贝尔曼小姐一点情，这笔债压在他良心上。他曾出于权宜，把她说成是自己的妻子。这个荒谬的说法固然是为了安抚一个疯女人才出口的，可毕竟说出口了。她如今占着一个好职位——某政党总部的秘书，而这个职位，她若知道内情，是得感谢 J. G. 里德先生的。

===Original===
He took up the phone and called her number, and, after the normal delay, heard her voice.

“Er﻿—Miss Belman,” Mr. Reeder coughed, “I have﻿—er﻿—two tickets for a theatre tonight. I wonder if you would care to go?”

Her astonishment was almost audible.

“That is very nice of you, Mr. Reeder. I should love to come with you.”

Mr. J. G. Reeder turned pale.

===Chinese===
他拿起电话，拨了她的号码，照例耽搁片刻之后，听到了她的声音。

“呃——贝尔曼小姐，”里德先生咳了一声，“我有——呃——两张今晚的戏票。不知您可愿赏光同去？”

她的惊愕几乎听得见。

“您真好，里德先生。我很乐意同您一起去。”

J. G. 里德先生脸都白了。

===Original===
“What I mean is, I have two tickets﻿—I thought perhaps that your﻿—er﻿—your﻿—er﻿—that somebody else would like to go﻿—what I mean was﻿—”

He heard a gentle laugh at the other end of the phone.

“What you mean is that you don’t wish to take me,” she said, and for a man of his experience he blundered badly.

“I should esteem it an honour to take you,” he said, in terror that he should offend her, “but the truth is, I thought﻿—”

“I will meet you at the theatre﻿—which is it? Orpheum﻿—how lovely! At eight o’clock.”

===Chinese===
“我的意思是，我有两张票——我想也许您的——呃——您的——呃——也许有别的什么人愿意去——我是说——”

他听见电话那头传来一声轻笑。

“您的意思是，您并不想带我去。”她说。以他这样阅历的人而论，这一下失言失得实在厉害。

“能带您去，我引以为荣。”他说，唯恐开罪了她，“可实情是，我原以为——”

“我到剧院同您碰头——是哪家？奥菲姆——太好了！八点见。”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder put down the instrument, feeling limp and moist. It is the truth that he had never taken a lady to any kind of social function in his life, and as there grew upon him the tremendous character of this adventure he was overwhelmed and breathless. A murderer waking from dreams of revelry to find himself in the condemned cell suffered no more poignant emotions than Mr. Reeder, torn from the smooth if treacherous currents of life and drawing nearer and nearer to the horrid vortex of unusualness.

“Bless me,” said Mr. Reeder, employing a strictly private expression which was reserved for his own crises.

He employed in his private office a young woman who combined a meticulous exactness in the filing of documents with a complete absence of those attractions which turn men into gods, and in other days set the armies of Perseus moving towards the walls of Troy. She was invariably addressed by Mr. Reeder as “Miss.” He believed her name to be “Oliver.” She was in truth a married lady with two children, but her nuptials had been celebrated without his knowledge.

===Chinese===
里德先生放下听筒，只觉浑身发软、手心冒汗。实情是，他平生从未陪任何女士出席过任何性质的社交场合；随着这场冒险的重大性质在心头越压越沉，他简直透不过气来。一个杀人犯正梦见纵酒狂欢，醒来却发现自己躺在死囚牢里，其情绪之煎熬也不比里德先生更甚——他正被从生活那平顺（虽说暗流四伏）的水流里生生扯出来，朝“破格出格”的可怖漩涡越漂越近。

“老天爷。”里德先生说，用的是一句严格自用的口头禅，专为应付他本人的危机而设。

他的私人办公室里雇着一位年轻女子：她归档文件一丝不苟到严苛的地步，同时全然不具备那种能把男人变成神明、当年驱使珀尔修斯的大军直扑特洛伊城下的魅力。里德先生对她的称呼一成不变，是“小姐”。他相信她的名字叫“奥利弗”（Oliver）。实际上她是一位已婚太太，膝下有两个孩子，只是她那场婚礼办得悄无声息，他一无所知。

===Original===
To the top floor of a building in Regent Street Mr. Reeder repaired for instruction and guidance.

“It is not﻿—er﻿—a practice of mine to﻿—er﻿—accompany ladies to the theatre, and I am rather at a loss to know what is expected of me, the more so since the young lady is﻿—er﻿—a stranger to me.”

His frosty-visaged assistant sneered secretly. At Mr. Reeder’s time of life, when such natural affections as were not atrophied should in decency be fossilised!

He jotted down her suggestions.

===Chinese===
里德先生专程去了摄政街（Regent Street）一幢大楼的顶层，寻求指点与教导。

“陪——呃——陪女士上剧院，不是——呃——不是我的惯例。人家对我有何指望，我颇无所适从，更何况这位小姐于我——呃——是个陌生人。”

他那位冷面助手暗自嗤笑。都到里德先生这把年纪了，那些不曾萎缩的天性柔情，按体面也早该化成化石了！

他把她的建议一一记下。

===Original===
“Chocolates indeed? Where can one procure﻿—? Oh, yes, I remember seeing the attendants sell them. Thank you so much, Miss﻿—er﻿—”

And as he went out, closing the door carefully behind him, she sneered openly.

“They all go wrong at seventy,” she said insultingly.

===Chinese===
“原来还要巧克力？上哪儿能弄到——哦，对了，我记得见引座员卖过。多谢，小——呃——”

他出去时仔细掩上了门，她这才公然嗤笑出声。

“人一到七十，没一个不出岔子的。”她出言讥诮。

===Original===
Margaret hardly knew what to expect when she came into the flamboyant foyer of the Orpheum. What was the evening equivalent to the square-topped derby and the tightly-buttoned frock coat of ancient design which he favoured in the hours of business? She would have passed the somewhat elegantly dressed gentleman in the correct pique waistcoat and the perfectly tied butterfly bow, only he claimed her attention.

“Mr. Reeder!” she gasped.

It was indeed Mr. Reeder: with not so much as a shirt-stud wrong; with a suit of the latest mode, and shoes glossy and V-toed. For Mr. Reeder, like many other men, dressed according to his inclination in business hours, but accepted blindly the instructions of his tailor in the matter of fancy raiment. Mr. J. G. Reeder was never conscious of his clothing, good or bad﻿—he was, however, very conscious of his strange responsibility.

===Chinese===
走进奥菲姆剧院那华丽俗艳的门厅时，玛格丽特简直不知该期待什么。他办公时间偏爱的平顶圆礼帽和扣得严严实实的旧式长礼服，到了晚间该换成什么相当的行头？若非那位绅士主动招呼，她险些同他擦肩而过——他衣着颇为讲究，穿着得体的凹凸纹布马甲，蝴蝶结打得一丝不苟。

“里德先生！”她倒吸一口气。

千真万确是里德先生：连一颗衬衫饰扣都没出差错；一身最新式样的套装，皮鞋锃亮，鞋尖呈 V 形。原来里德先生同许多男人一样，办公时间的衣着听凭自家的心意，可一到花哨衣裳，就对裁缝的指示照单全收。J. G. 里德先生从不留意自己的衣着，好也罢坏也罢——不过，对肩上这桩奇特的担子，他倒是意识得清清楚楚。

===Original===
He took her cloak (he had previously purchased programmes and a large box of chocolates, which he carried by its satin ribbon). There was a quarter of an hour to wait before the curtain went up, and Margaret felt it incumbent upon her to offer an explanation.

“You spoke about ‘somebody’ else; do you mean Roy﻿—the man who sometimes meets me at Westminster?”

Mr. Reeder had meant that young man. “He and I were good friends,” she said, “no more than that﻿—we aren’t very good friends anymore.”

===Chinese===
他接过她的披风（此前他已买好节目单和一大盒巧克力，正用缎带提着）。离开幕还有一刻钟，玛格丽特觉得有义务解释几句。

“您说过‘别的什么人’；您是指罗伊（Roy）吗——就是有时到威斯敏斯特接我的那个人？”

里德先生指的正是那位年轻人。“他和我本是好朋友，”她说，“仅此而已——如今连好朋友都算不上了。”

===Original===
She did not say why. She might have explained in a sentence if she had said that Roy’s mother held an exalted opinion of her only son’s qualities, physical and mental, and that Roy thoroughly endorsed his mother’s judgment, but she did not.

“Ah!” said Mr. Reeder unhappily. Soon after this the orchestra drowned further conversation, for they were sitting in the first row near to the noisiest of the brass and not far removed from the shrillest of the woodwind. In odd moments, through the thrilling first act, she stole a glance at her companion. She expected to find this man mildly amused or slightly bored by the absurd contrast between the realities which he knew and the theatricalities which were presented on the stage. But whenever she looked, he was absorbed in the action of the play; she could almost feel him tremble when the hero was strapped to a log and thrown into the boiling mountain stream, and when the stage Jove was rescued on the fall of the curtain, she heard, with something like stupefaction, Mr. Reeder’s quivering sigh of relief.

===Chinese===
她没说缘故。她本可一句话说清的——只消说，罗伊的母亲对她独子的身心才具怀着极高的评价，而罗伊对他母亲的判断又全盘赞同。可她没有。

“啊！”里德先生闷闷地说。此后不久，乐队便淹没了进一步的交谈，因为他们坐在第一排，紧挨着铜管声部里最吵的那几件，离木管声部里最尖利的也不远。在惊心动魄的第一幕的间隙里，她偷偷瞥看她的同伴。她原以为此君会觉着有趣，或对他所熟知的真实与舞台上的做派之间那荒唐的反差略感无聊。可无论她何时望过去，他都全神贯注于剧中的动作；主角被捆在圆木上、抛进翻滚沸腾的山涧时，她几乎能感到他在发抖；待到剧中那位朱庇特般的老者在落幕之际获救，她听到了里德先生一声颤抖的、如释重负的长叹——她几乎惊呆了。

===Original===
“But surely, Mr. Reeder, this bores you?” she protested, when the lights in the auditorium went up.

“This﻿—you mean the play﻿—bore me? Good gracious, no! I think it is very fine, remarkably fine.”

“But it isn’t life, surely? The story is so wildly improbable, and the incidents﻿—oh, yes, I’m enjoying it all; please don’t look so worried! Only I thought that you, who knew so much about criminology﻿—is that the word?﻿—would be rather amused.”

Mr. Reeder was looking very anxiously at her.

===Chinese===
“可是说真的，里德先生，这还不叫您腻烦吗？”场内灯光亮起时，她抗议道。

“这个——您是说这出戏——叫我腻烦？天哪，才不呢！我觉得它好得很，好得出奇。”

“可这毕竟不是生活，对吧？故事离奇得没了边，还有那些情节——哦，对，我看得挺开心；请别一脸愁苦嘛！我只是想，您对犯罪学——是叫这个词吧？——懂得那么多，该会觉着好笑。”

里德先生正焦急万分地望着她。

===Original===
“I’m afraid it is not the kind of play﻿—”

“Oh, but it is﻿—I love melodrama. But doesn’t it strike you as being﻿—far-fetched? For instance, that man being chained to a log, and the mother agreeing to her son’s death?”

Mr. Reeder rubbed his nose thoughtfully.

“The Bermondsey gang chained Harry Salter to a plank, turned it over and let him down, just opposite Billingsgate Market. I was at the execution of Tod Rowe, and he admitted it on the scaffold. And it was ‘Lee’ Pearson’s mother who poisoned him at Teddington to get his insurance money so that she could marry again. I was at the trial and she took her sentence laughing﻿—now what else was there in that act? Oh, yes, I remember: the proprietor of the sawmill tried to get the young lady to marry him by threatening to send her father to prison. That has been done hundreds of times﻿—only in a worse way. There is really nothing very extravagant about a melodrama except the prices of the seats, and I usually get my tickets free!”

===Chinese===
“恐怕这不是那种戏——”

“哦，可它正是——我爱看情节剧。不过您不觉得它未免——未免牵强吗？比方说，那个人被链子锁在圆木上，还有那母亲竟肯认下自己儿子的死？”

里德先生若有所思地揉了揉鼻子。

“伯蒙德赛帮就把哈里·索尔特（Harry Salter）绑在一块木板上，翻过来吊下去，地点就在比林斯盖特市场（Billingsgate Market）正对面。托德·罗（Tod Rowe）受刑那天我在场，他在绞架上也认了这一条。还有，在特丁顿（Teddington）毒死‘李’·皮尔逊（‘Lee’ Pearson）的，正是他母亲，为的是拿他的保险金好改嫁。那场审判我也在场，她听完判决还笑呢——那一幕里还有什么来着？哦，对了，我想起来了：锯木厂厂主想娶那位小姐，就威胁要送她父亲进监狱。这种事发生过几百回了——只做得更狠而已。情节剧里实在没什么太夸张的东西，除了座位的价钱——而我的票通常是白来的！”

===Original===
She listened, at first dumbfounded and then with a gurgle of amusement.

“How queer﻿—and yet﻿—well, frankly, I have only met melodrama once in life, and even now I cannot believe it. What happens in the next act?”

Mr. Reeder consulted his programme.

“I rather believe that the young woman in the white dress is captured and removed to the harem of an Eastern potentate,” he said precisely, and this time the girl laughed aloud.

===Chinese===
她听着，先是目瞪口呆，随即咯咯地笑出了声。

“多古怪呀——不过——这么说吧，坦白讲，我这辈子只在生活里碰到过一回情节剧，到如今都不敢相信。下一幕演什么？”

里德先生翻了翻节目单。

“我揣测，穿白衣裳的那位姑娘会被掳走，送进某位东方君王的后宫。”他一字一板地说。这一回，姑娘放声笑了。

===Original===
“Have you a parallel for that?” she asked triumphantly, and Mr. Reeder was compelled to admit that he knew no exact parallel, but﻿—

“It is rather a remarkable coincidence,” he said, “a very remarkable coincidence!”

She looked at her programme, wondering if she had overlooked anything so very remarkable.

===Chinese===
“这个您也有对应案例吗？”她得意地问。里德先生只好承认，他不知道有分毫不差的对应案例，不过——

“这算得上一桩非同寻常的巧合，”他说，“一桩非常非同寻常的巧合！”

她低头看节目单，纳闷自己是不是漏看了什么如此了不得的东西。

===Original===
“There is at this moment, watching me from the front row of the dress circle﻿—I beg you not to turn your head﻿—one who, if he is not a potentate, is undoubtedly Eastern; there are, in fact, two dark-complexioned gentlemen, but only one may be described as important.”

“But why are they watching you?” she asked in surprise.

“Possibly,” said Mr. Reeder solemnly, “because I look so remarkable in evening dress.”

===Chinese===
“此时此刻，正从楼座前排盯着我瞧——求您千万别转头——的那一位，纵然算不上君王，无疑是个东方人；确切地说，有两位肤色黝黑的绅士，但只有一位称得上要紧。”

“可他们为什么盯着您瞧？”她诧异地问。

“也许，”里德先生一本正经地说，“是因为我穿晚礼服的样子太不寻常了。”

===Original===
One of the dark-complexioned gentlemen turned to his companion at this moment.

“It is the woman he travels with every day; she lives in the same street, and is doubtless more to him than anybody in the world, Ram. See how she laughs in his face and how the old so-and-so looks at her! When men come to his great age they grow silly about women. This thing can be done tonight. I would sooner die than go back to Bombay without accomplishing my design upon this such-and-such and so-forth.”

Ram, his chauffeur, confederate and fellow jailbird, who was cast in a less heroic mould, and had, moreover, no personal vendetta, suggested in haste that the matter should be thought over.

===Chinese===
就在这时，两位黑肤绅士中的一位转向他的同伴。

“就是那个天天同他乘一车的女人；她住在同一条街上，对他来说无疑比世上任何人都亲，拉姆。瞧她冲着他的脸笑得多欢，瞧那老东西看她的眼神！男人一到他这把年纪，见了女人就犯傻。这事今晚就能办。不把这个、那个、如此这般统统办成，我宁可死，也不回孟买。”

拉姆——他的司机、同党兼牢友——天生少了副英雄坯子，何况又没有私仇，赶忙提议此事还须从长计议。

===Original===
“I have cogitated every hypothesis to its logical conclusions,” said Ras Lal in English.

“But, master,” said his companion urgently, “would it not be wise to leave this country and make a fortune with the new money which the fat little man can sell to us?”

“Vengeance is mine,” said Ras Lal in English.

===Chinese===
“我已经把每一个假想都推演到它的逻辑终点了。”拉斯·拉尔用英语说。

“可是，主人，”同伴急切地说，“离开这个国家，用那矮胖子能卖给我们的新钞票发一笔财，岂不更明智？”

“复仇在我。”拉斯·拉尔用英语说。

===Original===
He sat through the next act which, as Mr. Reeder had truly said, depicted the luring of an innocent girl into the hateful clutches of a Turkish pasha and, watching the development of the plot, his own scheme underwent revision. He did not wait to see what happened in the third and fourth acts﻿—there were certain preparations to be made.

“I still think that, whilst the story is awfully thrilling, it is awfully impossible,” said Margaret, as they moved slowly through the crowded vestibule. “In real life﻿—in civilised countries, I mean﻿—masked men do not suddenly appear from nowhere with pistols and say ‘Hands up!’﻿—not really, do they, Mr. Reeder?” she coaxed.

===Chinese===
他坐着看完了下一幕——正如里德先生所言，这一幕演的是无辜少女被诱入土耳其帕夏（pasha）可憎的魔掌——看着剧情层层推进，他自己的计划也随之修订。他没有等着看第三幕、第四幕演什么——有些准备工作要做。

“我还是觉得，这故事虽然刺激得要命，也不可能得要命，”两人随着拥挤的人流缓缓挪过前厅时，玛格丽特说，“现实生活里——我是说，在文明国家——蒙面人不会不知打哪儿突然冒出来，举着手枪喊‘举起手来’——真的不会，对吧，里德先生？”她哄劝道。

===Original===
Mr. Reeder murmured a reluctant agreement.

“But I have enjoyed it tremendously!” she said with enthusiasm, and looking down into the pink face Mr. Reeder felt a curious sensation which was not entirely pleasure and not wholly pain.

“I am very glad,” he said.

===Chinese===
里德先生含含糊糊地勉强表示了同意。

“可我看得痛快极了！”她兴高采烈地说。里德先生低头望着那张红扑扑的脸，感到一种奇异的滋味，不全是快乐，也不全是痛楚。

“我很高兴。”他说。

===Original===
Both the dress-circle and the stalls disgorged into the foyer, and he was looking round for a face he had seen when he arrived. But neither Ras Lal nor his companion in misfortune was visible. Rain was falling dismally, and it was some time before he found a cab.

“Luxury upon luxury,” smiled Margaret, when he took his place by her side. “You may smoke if you wish.”

Mr. Reeder took a paper packet of cigarettes from his waistcoat pocket, selected a limp cylinder, and lit it.

===Chinese===
楼座和正厅的看客一齐涌进门厅，他四下张望，寻找进场时瞥见的一张面孔。可拉斯·拉尔和他那位难兄难弟都不见了踪影。凄迷的雨下个不停，他好一会儿才寻到一辆马车。

“奢华上加奢华。”他在她身边落座时，玛格丽特笑道，“您想抽烟就请便。”

里德先生从马甲口袋里掏出一个纸包香烟，拣出一支软塌塌的烟卷，点着了。

===Original===
“No plays are quite like life, my dear young lady,” he said, as he carefully pushed the match through the space between the top of the window and the frame. “Melodramas appeal most to me because of their idealism.”

She turned and stared at him.

“Idealism?” she repeated incredulously.

He nodded.

===Chinese===
“没有哪出戏完全像生活，亲爱的小姐，”他一边小心翼翼地把火柴从车窗上框与窗玻璃的缝隙里塞出去，一边说，“情节剧最打动我的，是它们的理想主义。”

她转过头盯着他瞧。

“理想主义？”她难以置信地重复道。

他点了点头。

===Original===
“Have you ever noticed that there is nothing sordid about a melodrama? I once saw a classical drama﻿—*Oedipus*﻿—and it made me feel sick. In melodrama even the villains are heroic and the inevitable and unvarying moral is ‘Truth crushed to earth will rise again’﻿—isn’t that idealism? And they are wholesome. There are no sex problems; unpleasant things are never shown in an attractive light﻿—you come away uplifted.”

“If you are young enough,” she smiled.

“One should always be young enough to rejoice in the triumph of virtue,” said Mr. Reeder soberly.

===Chinese===
“您可曾留意，情节剧里没有一丝腌臜？我有一次看了出古典戏——《俄狄浦斯》（*Oedipus*）——看得我直想吐。情节剧里连恶棍都气概堂堂，而且那必然不变的主旨总是‘真理纵被践踏在地，终将重新站起’——这难道不是理想主义吗？而且它们有益身心。没有性爱纠葛；丑恶的事物从不见得诱人——你散场时只觉灵魂得到了升华。”

“只要你还够年轻。”她微笑道。

“人无论何时都该年轻得足以为美德的凯旋而欢欣。”里德先生正色道。

===Original===
They crossed Westminster Bridge and bore left to the New Kent Road. Through the rain-blurred windows J. G. picked up the familiar landmarks and offered a running commentary upon them in the manner of a guide. Margaret had not realised before that history was made in South London.

“There used to be a gibbet here﻿—this ugly-looking goods station was the London terminus of the first railways﻿—Queen Alexandra drove from there when she came to be married﻿—the thoroughfare on the right after we pass the Canal bridge is curiously named Bird-in-Bush Road﻿—”

A big car had drawn level with the cab, and the driver was shouting something to the cabman. Even the suspicious Mr. Reeder suspected no more than an exchange of offensiveness, till the cab suddenly turned into the road he had been speaking about. The car had fallen behind, but now drew abreast.

===Chinese===
马车过了威斯敏斯特桥（Westminster Bridge），向左拐上新肯特路（New Kent Road）。透过被雨水糊住的玻璃，J. G. 认出一个个熟悉的地标，像导游似的沿途逐一解说。玛格丽特从前竟不知道，南伦敦也是缔造历史的地方。

“这里从前立着一架绞刑架——这座其貌不扬的货场，是头几条铁路的伦敦终点站——亚历山德拉王后当年进京完婚，御驾正是打那儿出发——过了运河桥，右手边那条大道名字怪得很，叫‘鸟入灌木路’（Bird-in-Bush Road）——”

一辆大轿车已同马车并了行，司机正冲着马车夫嚷嚷什么。连多疑的里德先生也只当是一场寻常的对骂，直到马车忽然拐进他方才说到的那条路。轿车落在了后面，这会儿又赶了上来。

===Original===
“Probably the main road is up,” said J. G., and at that moment the cab slowed and stopped.

He was reaching out for the handle when the door was pulled open violently, and in the uncertain light Mr. Reeder saw a broad-shouldered man standing in the road.

“Alight quickly!”

In the man’s hand was a long, black Colt, and his face was covered from chin to forehead by a mask.

===Chinese===
“大概是大路在翻修吧。”J. G. 说。话音未落，马车慢了下来，停住了。

他正伸手去够车门把手，车门猛地被人拉开。昏暗的光线里，里德先生看见一个宽肩膀的男人站在路上。

“快下车！”

那人手里握着一支黑色的长筒柯尔特（Colt），脸上从下巴到额头蒙着面罩。

===Original===
“Quickly﻿—and keep your hands erect!”

Mr. Reeder stepped out into the rain and reached to close the door.

“The female also﻿—come, miss!”

“Here﻿—what’s the game﻿—you told me the New Cross Road was blocked.” It was the cabman talking.

===Chinese===
“快点——双手举直了别放！”

里德先生跨进雨里，伸手要关门。

“那位女士也下来——来吧，小姐！”

“喂——这是演的哪一出——不是说新克罗斯路（New Cross Road）封路了吗。”说话的是马车夫。

===Original===
“Here is a five﻿—keep your mouth shut.”

The masked man thrust a note at the driver.

“I don’t want your money﻿—”

“You require my bullet in your bosom perchance, my good fellow?” asked Ras Lal sardonically.

===Chinese===
“这儿是五镑——闭紧你的嘴。”

蒙面人把一张钞票塞给车夫。

“我不要你的钱——”

“那么您或许是想让一颗子弹进胸膛了，我的好伙计？”拉斯·拉尔讥诮地问。

===Original===
Margaret had followed her escort into the road by this time. The car had stopped just behind the cab. With the muzzle of the pistol stuck into his back, Mr. Reeder walked to the open door and entered. The girl followed, and the masked man jumped after them and closed the door. Instantly the interior was flooded with light.

“This is a considerable surprise to a clever and intelligent police detective?”

===Chinese===
这时玛格丽特也已跟着她的护送人下了车。轿车就停在马车后面。枪口顶着后背，里德先生走向敞开的车门，钻了进去。姑娘跟着进去，蒙面人也跳上车，带上了门。车厢里霎时灯火通明。

“对一位聪明睿智的警探来说，这份意外够分量吧？”

===Original===
Their captor sat on the opposite seat, his pistol on his knees. Through the holes of the black mask a pair of brown eyes gleamed malevolently. But Mr. Reeder’s interest was in the girl. The shock had struck the colour from her face, but he observed with thankfulness that her chief emotion was not fear. She was numb with amazement, and was stricken speechless.

The car had circled and was moving swiftly back the way they had come. He felt the rise of the Canal bridge, and then the machine turned abruptly to the right and began the descent of a steep hill. They were running towards Rotherhithe﻿—he had an extraordinary knowledge of London’s topography.

The journey was a short one. He felt the car wheels bump over an uneven roadway for a hundred yards, the body rocking uncomfortably, and then with a jar of brakes the machine stopped suddenly.

===Chinese===
劫持他们的人坐在对面的座位上，手枪搁在膝头。黑面罩的两个孔洞后面，一双棕色的眼睛闪着恶毒的光。可里德先生的心思在姑娘身上。惊变夺走了她脸上的血色，但他欣慰地注意到，她的头号情绪并非恐惧。她是惊得麻木了，一句话也说不出。

轿车已调了头，顺着来路疾驰。他觉出车轮碾过运河桥的拱坡，随后车身猛地向右一拐，开始下一段陡坡。他们正朝罗瑟希德（Rotherhithe）而去——他对伦敦的地形熟稔得惊人。

路程不长。他感到车轮在一段坎坷的路面上颠了百来码，车身晃得人难受，随后刹车一震，车猛地停住。

===Original===
They were on a narrow muddy lane. On one side rose the arches of a railway aqueduct, on the other an open space bounded by a high fence. Evidently the driver had pulled up short of their destination, for they had to squelch and slide through the thick mud for another fifty yards before they came to a narrow gateway in the fence. Through this they struck a cinder-path leading to a square building, which Mr. Reeder guessed was a small factory of some kind. Their conductor flashed a lamp on the door, and in weatherworn letters the detective read:

“The Storn-Filton Leather Company.”

“Now!” said the man, as he turned a switch. “Now, my false-swearing and corrupt police official, I have a slight bill to settle with you.”

===Chinese===
这是一条狭窄的泥泞小巷。一侧立着铁路旱桥的桥拱，另一侧是一片以高栅栏围起的空地。司机显然把车停在了离目的地差一截的地方，因为他们不得不在厚泥里连滑带蹭再跋涉五十码，才到栅栏一道窄门前。穿过窄门是一条煤渣小径，通向一座四四方方的建筑——里德先生猜是哪家小工厂。押送人拿手电照了照门，侦探借着风霜剥蚀的字母读出：

“斯托恩-菲尔顿皮革公司（The Storn-Filton Leather Company）。”

“好了！”那人拨动开关，说道，“好了，我那位作伪证的腐败警官，我有一笔小小的账单，要同您结算。”

===Original===
They were in a dusty lobby, enclosed on three sides by matchboard walls.

“ ‘Account’ is the word you want, Ras Lal,” murmured Mr. Reeder.

For a moment the man was taken aback, and then, snatching the mask from his face:

“I am Ras Lal! And you shall repent it! For you and for your young missus this is indeed a cruel night of anxiety!”

===Chinese===
他们置身一间积灰的门厅，三面围着企口板墙。

“您想用的那个词是‘账目’，拉斯·拉尔。”里德先生低声说。

那人愣了片刻，随即一把扯下面罩：

“我正是拉斯·拉尔！你要后悔的！对你和你家年轻太太来说，这确确实实是个焦灼难熬的残酷之夜！”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder did not smile at the quaint English. The gun in the man’s hand spoke all languages without error, and could be as fatal in the hands of an unconscious humorist as if it were handled by the most savage of purists.

And he was worried about the girl: she had not spoken a word since their capture. The colour had come back to her cheeks, and that was a good sign. There was, too, a light in her eyes which Reeder could not associate with fear.

===Chinese===
对这古雅别扭的英语，里德先生没有笑。那人手里的枪说起任何语言都不带差错，落在一个浑然不觉的幽默家手里，同攥在最凶悍的纯语主义者手里一样致命。

再说，他也替姑娘担心：被劫以来她一句话没说。双颊已回了血色，这是好兆头。而且她眼里有一种光，里德没法把它同恐惧联在一起。

===Original===
Ras Lal, taking down a long cord that hung on a nail in the wooden partition, hesitated.

“It is not necessary,” he said, with an elaborate shrug of shoulder; “the room is sufficiently reconnoitred﻿—you will be innocuous there.”

Flinging open a door, he motioned them to pass through and mount the bare stairs which faced them. At the top was a landing and a large steel door set in the solid brickwork.

===Chinese===
拉斯·拉尔从木隔板的钉子上取下一条长绳，又犹豫了。

“不必了。”他说，煞有介事地耸了耸肩，“那房间我已侦察周全——你们在那儿翻不了天。”

他推开一扇门，示意他们穿过去，登上面前的光板楼梯。顶头是一个平台，还有一扇嵌在实体砖墙里的大铁门。

===Original===
Pulling back the iron bolt, he pushed at the door, and it opened with a squeak. It was a large room, and had evidently been used for the storage of something inflammable, for the walls and floor were of rough-faced concrete and above a dusty desk an inscription was painted, “Danger. Don’t smoke in this store.” There were no windows except one some eighteen inches square, the top of which was near the ceiling. In one corner of the room was a heap of grimy paper files, and on the desk a dozen small wooden boxes, one of which had been opened, for the nail-bristling lid was canted up at an angle.

“Make yourself content for half an hour or probably forty minutes,” said Ras Lal, standing in the doorway with his ostentatious revolver. “At that time I shall come for your female; tomorrow she will be on a ship with me, bound for﻿—ah, who knows where?”

“Shut the door as you go out,” said Mr. J. G. Reeder; “there is an unpleasant draught.”

===Chinese===
他拉开铁闩，推门，门吱呀一声开了。这是个大房间，显然存放过什么易燃之物，因为四壁和地面都是毛面混凝土，积灰的书桌上方刷着一行字：“危险。库房内严禁吸烟。”窗户只有一扇，约十八英寸见方，窗顶快贴着天花板。屋角堆着一摞乌黑的纸板文件夹，桌上摆着十来只小木箱，其中一只开着——钉子密布的箱盖翘起一个角度。

“且安心待上半个钟头，或者四十分钟。”拉斯·拉尔拎着他那把招摇的左轮手枪，站在门口说，“到时候我来领你家女伴；明天她就在船上了，同我一道，开往——啊，天知道哪儿？”

“出去时请随手关门，”J. G. 里德先生说，“过堂风难受。”

===Original===
Mr. Tommy Fenalow came on foot at two o’clock in the morning and, passing down the muddy lane, his electric torch suddenly revealed car marks. Tommy stopped like a man shot. His knees trembled beneath him and his heart entered his throat at the narrowest end. For a while he was undecided whether it would be better to run or walk away. He had no intention of going forward. And then he heard a voice. It was Ras Lal’s assistant, and he nearly swooned with joy. Stumbling forward, he came up to the shivering man.

“Did that fool boss of yours bring the car along here?” he asked in a whisper.

“Yas﻿—Mr. Ras Lal,” said Ram with whom the English language was not a strong point.

===Chinese===
凌晨两点钟，汤米·芬纳洛先生步行而来，沿泥巷走到半路，手电筒忽然照出车辙印。汤米像挨了枪子似的站住了。两膝在他身子底下直打颤，一颗心堵到了嗓子眼最窄处。有那么一会儿，他拿不定是撒腿跑还是缓步走。他本来无意再往前去。接着他听见一个声音——是拉斯·拉尔的助手。他险些欢喜得晕过去，跌跌撞撞奔到那个哆嗦着的人跟前。

“你那位蠢老板把车开到这儿来了？”他压着嗓子问。

“是——拉斯·拉尔老爷。”拉姆答道。英语于他并非强项。

===Original===
“Then he’s a fool!” growled Tommy. “Gosh! he put my heart in my mouth!”

Whilst Ram was getting together sufficient English to explain what had happened, Tommy passed on. He found his client sitting in the lobby, a black cheroot between his teeth, a smile of satisfaction on his dark face.

“Welcome!” he said, as Tommy closed the door. “We have trapped the weasel.”

“Never mind about the weasel,” said the other impatiently. “Did you find the rupees?”

===Chinese===
“那他就是个蠢货！”汤米低吼，“天哪！差点把我魂吓出来！”

趁拉姆还在攒措辞、攒够讲清原委的英语，汤米自顾走了进去。他看见客户坐在门厅里，齿间咬着一支黑色方头雪茄，黝黑的脸上挂着满意的笑。

“欢迎！”汤米关门时他说，“黄鼠狼落网了。”

“黄鼠狼先别提了，”对方不耐烦地说，“卢比找到了吗？”

===Original===
Ras Lal shook his head.

“But I left them in the store﻿—ten thousand notes. I thought you’d have got them and skipped before this,” said Mr. Fenalow anxiously.

“I have something more important in the store﻿—come and see my friend.”

He preceded the bewildered Tommy up the stairs, turned on the landing light and threw open the door.

“Behold﻿—” he said, and said no more.

===Chinese===
拉斯·拉尔摇了摇头。

“可我把它们留在库房了——整整一万张。我还当你早取了货跑路了呢。”芬纳洛先生焦急地说。

“我库房里放着更要紧的东西——来见见我的朋友吧。”

他领着满头雾水的汤米上了楼，打开楼梯平台的灯，一把推开门。

“看哪——”他说，再说不出下文。

===Original===
“Why, it is Mr. Fenalow!” said Mr. J. G.

One hand held a packet of almost lifelike rupee notes; as for the other hand﻿—

“You oughter known he carried a gun, you dam’ black baboon,” hissed Tommy. “An’ to put him in a room where the stuff was, and a telephone!”

He was being driven to the local police station, and for the moment was attached to his companion by links of steel.

“It was a mere jest or a piece of practical joking, as I shall explain to the judge in the morning,” said Ras airily.

Tommy Fenalow’s reply was unprintable.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，这不是芬纳洛先生嘛！”J. G. 说。

他一只手托着一包几可乱真的卢比假钞；至于另一只手嘛——

“你本该晓得他身上带枪，你这该死的黑狒狒，”汤米嘶声骂道，“还偏偏把他关在那间屋里——屋里搁着货，还有电话！”

他正被押往当地警察局，一时还同他的同伴以钢链相连。

“这不过是句玩笑，一场恶作剧，明早我自会向法官大人解释清楚。”拉斯轻描淡写地说。

汤米·芬纳洛的回答无法付印。

===Original===
Three o’clock boomed out from St. John’s Church as Mr. Reeder accompanied an excited girl to the front door of her boardinghouse.

“I can’t tell you how I﻿—I’ve enjoyed tonight,” she said.

Mr. Reeder glanced uneasily at the dark face of the house.

“I hope﻿—er﻿—your friends will not think it remarkable that you should return at such an hour﻿—”

Despite her assurance, he went slowly home with an uneasy feeling that her name had in some way been compromised. And in melodrama, when a heroine’s name is compromised, somebody has to marry her.

That was the disturbing thought that kept Mr. Reeder awake all night.

===Chinese===
圣约翰教堂的钟声报出三点，里德先生陪着一个兴奋难抑的姑娘，走到她寄宿舍的大门前。

“我说不清我今晚——我今晚有多开心。”她说。

里德先生不安地瞥了一眼房子黑沉沉的正面。

“我希望——呃——您的朋友们不会觉得您这个钟点回来有什么稀奇——”

尽管她再三担保，他回家的一路仍怀着不安，觉得她的名声多少受了牵累。而在情节剧里，女主角的名声一旦受了牵累，总得有人娶她。

正是这个扰人的念头，害得里德先生一夜未曾合眼。

## The Green Mamba / 绿曼巴

===Original===
The spirit of exploration has ruined more promising careers than drink, gambling or the smiles of women. Generally speaking, the beaten tracks of life are the safest, and few men have adventured into the uncharted spaces in search of easy money who have not regarded the discovery of the old hard road whence they strayed as the greatest of their achievements.

Mo Liski held an assured position in his world, and one acquired by the strenuous and even violent exercise of his many qualities. He might have gone on until the end of the chapter, only he fell for an outside proposition, and, moreover, handicapped himself with a private feud, which had its beginning in an affair wholly remote from his normal operations.

===Chinese===
断送大好前程的，探险精神比美酒、赌博或女人的嫣然一笑还要多。大体而论，人生走得熟的旧路最是安稳；为觅轻松钱财而闯入地图空白处的男人们，事后多半会把重新觅得自己走岔的那条又老又硬的路，当作平生最大的成就。

莫·利斯基（Mo Liski）在他的天地里占着一个稳固的位置，而这位置是他凭自己诸多才干的勤勉乃至激烈的施展挣来的。他本可以照这样一直走到这一章的末尾，偏偏相中了一桩外来的买卖，更要命的是，还让自己背上了一场私人仇怨——这仇怨起于一件同他的正常营生毫不相干的事。

===Original===
There was a Moorish grafter named El Rahbut, who had made several visits to England, travelling by the banana boats which make the round trip from London River to Funchal Bay, Las Palmas, Tangier and Oporto. He was a very ordinary, yellow-faced Moor, pockmarked and undersized, and he spoke English, having in his youth fallen into the hands of a well-meaning American missionary. This man Rahbut was useful to Mo because quite a lot of German drugs are shipped via Trieste to the Levant, and many a crate of oranges has been landed in the Pool that had, squeezed in their golden interiors, little metal cylinders containing smuggled saccharine, heroin, cocaine, hydrochlorate and divers other noxious medicaments.

Rahbut brought such things from time to time, was paid fairly and was satisfied. One day, in the saloon bar of The Four Jolly Seamen, he told Mo of a great steal. It had been carried out by a group of Anghera thieves working in Fez, and the loot was no less than the Emeralds of Suliman, the most treasured possession of Morocco. Not even Abdul Aziz in his most impecunious days had dared to remove them from the Mosque of Omar; the Anghera men being what they were, broke into the holy house, killed two guardians of the treasure, and had got away with the nine green stones of the great king. Thereafter arose an outcry which was heard from the bazaars of Calcutta to the mean streets of Marsi-Karsi. But the men of Anghera were superior to the voice of public opinion and they did no more than seek a buyer. El Rahbut, being a notorious bad character, came into the matter, and this was the tale he told to Mo Liski at The Four Jolly Seamen one foggy October night.

===Chinese===
有一个摩尔族骗子，名叫埃尔·拉布特（El Rahbut），来过英国好几趟，坐的是跑伦敦河至丰沙尔湾（Funchal Bay）、拉斯帕尔马斯（Las Palmas）、丹吉尔（Tangier）与波尔图（Oporto）环形航线的香蕉货船。他是个再普通不过的黄脸摩尔人，麻面，个头矮小，年轻时落到过一位好心美国传教士手里，因此会说英语。这个拉布特对莫有用，因为相当多的德国药品经的里雅斯特（Trieste）船运去黎凡特（Levant），而一箱箱橙子在伦敦池码头卸货时，金黄的果肉里往往窝藏着小金属筒，装着走私的糖精、海洛因、可卡因、盐酸盐和种种别的害人药物。

拉布特不时捎来这类货色，报酬公道，他也知足。有一天，在“四个快乐水手”酒馆（The Four Jolly Seamen）的雅座酒吧里，他给莫讲了一桩天大的窃案。案子是一伙在非斯（Fez）作案的安格拉（Anghera）窃贼干的，赃物不是别的，正是苏莱曼绿宝石（Emeralds of Suliman）——摩洛哥最珍贵的镇国之宝。就连阿卜杜勒·阿齐兹（Abdul Aziz）在手头最拮据的日子里，也不敢把它们挪出奥马尔清真寺（Mosque of Omar）；安格拉人却不管这一套，闯进圣殿，杀了宝藏的两名守卫，带着大国王的九颗绿宝石扬长而去。事后舆论哗然，从加尔各答（Calcutta）的集市一直嚷到马尔西-卡尔西（Marsi-Karsi）的陋巷。可安格拉人压根不把舆论放在眼里，只管物色买主。埃尔·拉布特既是个出了名的坏坯，便掺和进了这桩事。十月里一个雾夜，他在“四个快乐水手”讲给莫·利斯基听的，就是这个故事。

===Original===
“There is a million pesetas profit in this for you and me, Mr. Good Man,” said Rahbut (all Europeans who paid on the nail were “Mr. Good Man” to El Rahbut). “There is also death for me if this thing becomes known.”

Mo listened, smoothing his chin with a hand that sparkled and flashed dazzlingly. He was keen on ornamentation. It was a little outside his line, but the newspapers had stated the bald value of the stolen property, and his blood was on fire at the prospect of earning half a million so easily. That Scotland Yard and every police headquarters in the world were on the lookout for the nine stones of Suliman did not greatly disturb him. He knew the subterranean way down which a polished stone might slide; and if the worst came to the worst, there was a reward of £5,000 for the recovery of the jewels.

“I’ll think it over; where is the stuff?”

“Here,” said Rahbut, to the other’s surprise. “In ten﻿—twenty minutes I could lay them on your hands, Mr. Good Man.”

===Chinese===
“这票买卖，你我有一百万比塞塔（pesetas）的赚头，好先生。”拉布特说（凡当场付现的欧洲人，在埃尔·拉布特嘴里一律是“好先生”）。“不过这事要是漏了风，我的死期也就到了。”

莫听着，一只手摩挲下巴——那只手珠光宝气，亮得晃眼。他素来偏爱饰物。这桩买卖稍稍出了他的本行，可报纸上白纸黑字登过失窃财物的总值，一想到能这样轻松赚进五十万，他的血就烧起来了。苏格兰场和全世界每一家警察总部都在搜找苏莱曼的九颗宝石，这也不怎么叫他犯愁。一颗磨光的宝石能顺着哪条地下暗道滑下去，他心里有数；退一万步说，寻回这批珠宝还有 5,000 英镑的悬赏呢。

“我考虑考虑；货在哪儿？”

“就在这儿。”拉布特说，倒叫对方吃了一惊。“十分钟——二十分钟之内，我就能把它们搁到您手心里，好先生。”

===Original===
Here seemed a straightforward piece of negotiation; it was doubly unfortunate that at that very period he should find himself mixed up in an affair which promised no profit whatever﻿—the feud of Marylou Plessy, which was to become his because of his high regard for the lady.

When a woman is bad, she is usually very bad indeed, and Marylou Plessy was an extremely malignant woman. She was rather tall and handsome, with black sleek hair, boyishly shingled, and a heavy black fringe that covered a forehead of some distinction.

Mr. Reeder saw her once: he was at the Central Criminal Court giving evidence against Bartholomew Xavier Plessy, an ingenious Frenchman who discovered a new way of making old money. His forgeries were well-nigh undetectable, but Mr. Reeder was no ordinary man. He not only detected them, but he traced the printer, and that was why Bartholomew Xavier faced an unimpassioned judge, who told him in a hushed voice how very wrong it was to debase the currency; how it struck at the very roots of our commercial and industrial life. This the debonair man in the dock did not resent. He knew all about it. It was the judge’s curt postscript which made him wince.

===Chinese===
看来这是一笔干脆利落的买卖；偏偏就在这个当口，他发觉自己卷进了一件毫无赚头的恩怨里，实在倒霉加倒霉——玛丽卢·普莱西（Marylou Plessy）的仇怨；又因为他对这位太太敬慕得深，这仇怨就要变成他自己的了。

女人一旦坏，往往就坏得透顶，玛丽卢·普莱西正是个歹毒至极的女人。她个子颇高，容貌标致，一头黑发剪成男孩式短发，厚重的黑色刘海覆着一片气度不凡的额头。

里德先生见过她一回：他正在中央刑事法院（Central Criminal Court）出庭作证，指控巴塞洛缪·泽维尔·普莱西（Bartholomew Xavier Plessy）——一个别出心裁的法国人，琢磨出了伪造旧钞的新法子。他的伪钞几乎无法鉴别，可里德先生不是寻常人物。他不但鉴别出来了，还循线追到了印制者；于是巴塞洛缪·泽维尔站到了一位不动声色的法官面前，法官压低嗓音告诉他，败坏币制是何等罪过，如何直击我们商业与工业生活的根基。被告席上这位风流人物对这套说辞并无怨怼，桩桩件件他心里有数。叫他咧嘴的，是法官那句简短的后话。

===Original===
“You will be kept in penal servitude for twenty years.”

That Marylou loved the man is open to question. The probabilities are that she did not; but she hated Mr. Reeder, and she hated him not because he had brought her man to his undoing, but because, in the course of his evidence, he had used the phrase “the woman with whom the prisoner is associated.” And Mr. John Reeder could have put her beside Plessy in the dock had he so wished: she knew this too and loathed him for his mercifulness.

===Chinese===
“你将被处以二十年苦役监禁。”

玛丽卢爱不爱那个男人，大可存疑。多半是不爱的；可她恨里德先生，恨他倒不是因为断送了她的男人，而是因为他在证词里用了“与囚犯有牵连的女人”这个说法。而约翰·里德先生（John Reeder）本可以把她也送上被告席、让她同普莱西并肩受审的——这一点她同样心知肚明，于是因他的手下留情而愈发恨他。

===Original===
Mrs. Plessy had a large flat in Portland Street. It was in a block which was the joint property of herself and her husband, for their graft had been on the grand scale, and Mr. Plessy owned racehorses before he owned a number in Parkhurst Convict Establishment. And here Marylou entertained lavishly.

A few months after her husband went to prison, she dined tête-à-tête with Mo Liski, the biggest of the gang leaders and an uncrowned emperor of the underworld. He was a small, dapper man who wore pince-nez and looked rather like a member of one of the learned professions. Yet he ruled the Strafas and the Sullivans and the Birklows, and his word was law on a dozen racetracks, in a score of spieling clubs and innumerable establishments less liable to police supervision. People opposing him were incontinently “coshed”﻿—rival leaders more or less paid tribute and walked warily at that. He levied toll upon bookmakers and was immune from police interference by reason of their two failures to convict him.

===Chinese===
普莱西太太在波特兰街（Portland Street）有一套宽敞的公寓，所在的那幢楼是她同丈夫的共有产业——他们的非法买卖做得规模宏大，普莱西先生早在领到帕克赫斯特监狱（Parkhurst Convict Establishment）的囚号之前，就拥有赛马了。就在这儿，玛丽卢挥金如土地款待宾客。

丈夫入狱几个月后，她同莫·利斯基两人对坐共进晚餐——此人是帮派头目里最大的一位，黑道上的无冕之王。他身材矮小，衣着利落，架一副夹鼻眼镜，模样颇像受过高等教育的专业人士。然而斯特拉法们、沙利文们、伯克洛们统统归他辖制；在十几处赛马场、二十来家赌场和数不清的不易受警察监管的场子里，他的话就是法律。同他作对的人，转眼便挨“闷棍”——对立的头目们多多少少都得纳贡，行走起来也格外小心。他向赛马赌注经纪人抽头收税，又因警方两次定罪未遂，得以不受警察搅扰。

===Original===
Since there are white specks on the blackest coat, he had this redeeming feature, that Marylou Plessy was his ideal woman, and it is creditable in a thief to possess ideals, however unworthily they may be disposed.

He listened intently to Marylou’s views, playing with his thin watchguard, his eyes on the embroidery of the tablecloth. But though he loved her, his native caution held him to reason.

“That’s all right, Marylou,” he said. “I dare say I could get Reeder, but what is going to happen then? There will be a squeak louder than a bus brake! And he’s dangerous. I never worry about the regular busies, but this old feller is in the Public Prosecutor’s office, and he wasn’t put there because he’s silly. And just now I’ve got one of the biggest deals on that I’ve ever touched. Can’t you ‘do’ him yourself? You’re a clever woman: I don’t know a cleverer.”

===Chinese===
不过话说回来，最黑的罩衫上也还有白点，他也有这么一条可取之处：玛丽卢·普莱西是他心中的理想女人。贼而有理想，总算难得，哪怕这理想安置得如何不三不四。

他专心听玛丽卢发牢骚，手指绕着细细的表链，眼睛盯着桌布上的绣花。他虽爱她，天生的谨慎却拉着他讲理性。

“行了，玛丽卢，”他说，“要说我能不能收拾里德，或许能。可收拾完了呢？到时候闹出的吱哇声，比公共汽车的刹车还响！而且这人危险。寻常条子我从不含糊，可这位老兄在检察总长公署（Public Prosecutor’s office）当差，他坐那把交椅，总不是因为他傻。眼下我手上还压着一桩这辈子数一数二的大买卖。你自己就不能‘办’了他？你是个聪明女人：再聪明的我没见过。”

===Original===
“Of course, if you’re scared of Reeder﻿—!” she said contemptuously, and a tolerant smile twisted his thin lips.

“Me? Don’t be silly, dearie! Show him a point yourself. If you can’t get him, let me know. Scared of him! Listen! That old bird would lose his feathers and be skinned for the pot before you could say ‘Mo Liski’ if I wanted!”

===Chinese===
“当然啦，你要是怕了里德——！”她不屑地说。他薄薄的嘴唇一扯，露出一抹宽容的笑。

“我？别犯傻了，亲爱的！你自己也给他点厉害瞧瞧。你办不了他，言语一声。怕他！听着！只要我乐意，不等你说完‘莫·利斯基’四个字，那只老鸟就得拔光毛、剥了皮下锅！”

===Original===
In the Public Prosecutor’s office they had no doubt about Mr. Reeder’s ability to take care of himself, and when Chief Inspector Pyne came over from the Yard to report that Marylou had been in conference with the most dangerous man in London, the Assistant Prosecutor grinned his amusement.

“No﻿—Reeder wants no protection. I’ll tell him if you like, but he probably knows all about it. What are you people doing about the Liski crowd?”

Pyne pulled a long face.

“We’ve had Liski twice, but well organised perjury has saved him. The Assistant Commissioner doesn’t want him again till we get him with the blood on his hands, so to speak. He’s dangerous.”

===Chinese===
检察总长公署里，对里德先生自保的本事倒无人怀疑。总探长派恩（Pyne）从苏格兰场过来报告，说玛丽卢同伦敦最危险的人物碰过头，副检察总长听了咧嘴直乐。

“不必——里德用不着保护。你们愿意，我可以转告他，不过这事他八成一清二楚。你们打算怎么处置利斯基那伙人？”

派恩拉长了脸。

“利斯基我们抓过两回，可组织严密的伪证把他救了。副总监的意思是，不逮他个现行——好比说，血还沾在手上的当口——就先别逮第三回。这人危险。”

===Original===
The Assistant Prosecutor nodded.

“So is Reeder,” he said ominously. “That man is a genial mamba! Never seen a mamba? He’s a nice black snake, and you’re dead two seconds after he strikes!”

The chief inspector’s smile was one of incredulity.

“He never impressed me that way﻿—rabbit, yes, but snake, no!”

===Chinese===
副检察总长点点头。

“里德也一样。”他说，语气不祥，“那人是条和气的曼巴！没见过曼巴？一种漂亮的黑蛇，咬你一口，两秒钟后你就死透了！”

总探长的笑容满脸狐疑。

“他从没给过我这种印象——像兔子，是；像蛇，不像！”

===Original===
Later in the morning a messenger brought Mr. Reeder to the chief’s office, and he arrived with that ineffable air of apology and diffidence which gave the uninitiated such an altogether wrong idea of his calibre. He listened with closed eyes whilst his superior told him of the meeting between Liski and Marylou.

“Yes, sir,” he sighed, when the narrative came to an end. “I have heard rumours. Liski? He is the person who associates with unlawful characters? In other days and under more favourable conditions he would have been the leader of a Florentine faction. An interesting man. With interesting friends.”

“I hope your interest remains impersonal,” warned the lawyer, and Mr. Reeder sighed again, opened his mouth to speak, hesitated, and then: “Doesn’t the continued freedom of Mr. Liski cast﻿—um﻿—a reflection upon our department, sir?” he asked.

===Chinese===
上午稍晚，一名信差把里德先生请到上司的办公室。他到时带着那副难以言传的赔罪兼怯场的神气——正是这副神气，让不明底细的人把他的斤两估得一塌糊涂。上司告诉他利斯基同玛丽卢会面一事时，他闭目聆听。

“是，长官。”叙述完毕，他叹道，“我听到过些风声。利斯基？就是同不法之徒为伍的那一位？换一个时代、换一种顺遂的境遇，他本可以当上佛罗伦萨的党魁。有意思的人。交的也有意思的朋友。”

“但愿你的兴趣始终止于公事。”那位法律家告诫道。里德先生又叹了口气，张口欲言，迟疑片刻，然后问：“利斯基先生至今逍遥法外，岂不——嗯——有损敝署的清誉么，长官？”

===Original===
His chief looked up: it was an inspiration which made him say:

“Get him!”

Mr. Reeder nodded very slowly.

“I have often thought that it would be a good idea,” he said. His gaze deepened in melancholy. “Liski has many acquaintances of a curious character,” he said at last. “Dutchmen, Russians, Jewish persons﻿—he knows a Moor.”

===Chinese===
上司抬起头来：灵光一闪，他说：

“办了他！”

里德先生极缓极缓地点头。

“我常常想，这未尝不是个好主意。”他说。他的目光沉入忧郁。“利斯基有许多来路古怪的相识，”他终于说，“荷兰人、俄国人、犹太人——他还认识一个摩尔人。”

===Original===
The chief looked up quickly.

“A Moor﻿—you’re thinking of the Nine Emeralds? My dear man, there are hundreds of Moors in London and thousands in Paris.”

“And millions in Morocco,” murmured Mr. Reeder. “I only mention the Moor in passing, sir. As regards my friend Mrs. Plessy﻿—I hope only for the best.”

And he melted from the room.

===Chinese===
上司霍然抬眼。

“摩尔人——你想到九颗绿宝石了？我亲爱的伙计，伦敦的摩尔人数以百计，巴黎的数以千计。”

“摩洛哥的数以百万计。”里德先生喃喃道，“我只是顺带一提那位摩尔人，长官。至于我的朋友普莱西太太——但愿一切向好。”

说罢，他从屋里悄然隐去。

===Original===
The greater part of a month passed before he showed any apparent interest in the case. He spent odd hours wandering in the neighbourhood of Lambeth, and on one occasion he was seen in the members’ enclosure at Hurst Park racetrack﻿—but he spoke to nobody, and nobody spoke to him.

One night Mr. Reeder came dreamily back to his well-ordered house in Brockley Road, and found waiting on his table a small flat box which had arrived, his housekeeper told him, by post that afternoon. The label was addressed in typewritten characters “John Reeder, Esq.” and the postmark was Central London.

===Chinese===
大半个月过去，他才显出对本案的任何明显兴趣。他把零零碎碎的闲暇都花在兰贝斯（Lambeth）一带游荡，有一回还叫人瞧见在赫斯特公园（Hurst Park）赛马场的会员围场里——可他不理人，人也不理他。

一天夜里，里德先生恍恍惚惚地回到布罗克利路（Brockley Road）上他那井井有条的宅子，发现桌上正候着一只扁扁的小盒——女管家告诉他，是当天下午寄到的。标签上是打字机打的收件人：“约翰·里德先生台启”，邮戳是伦敦中区。

===Original===
He cut the thin ribbon which tied it, stripped first the brown paper and then the silver tissue, and exposed a satiny lid, which he lifted daintily. There, under a layer of paper shavings, were roll upon roll of luscious confectionery. Chocolate, with or without dainty extras, had an appeal for Mr. Reeder, and he took up a small globule garnished with crystallised violets and examined it admiringly.

His housekeeper came in at that moment with his tea-tray and set it down on the table. Mr. Reeder looked over his large glasses.

“Do you like chocolates, Mrs. Kerrel?” he asked plaintively.

“Why, yes, sir,” the elderly lady beamed.

===Chinese===
他剪断捆盒的细缎带，先剥去牛皮纸，再揭开银色薄绵纸，露出一只缎面盒盖，他优雅地掀了开来。纸刨花下，一卷又一卷甘美诱人的糖果卧在其中。巧克力——带不带精致附加物的——于里德先生自有魅力，他拈起一颗缀着水晶紫罗兰的小圆球，满心赏玩地端详着。

恰在此时，女管家端着茶盘进来，把它搁在桌上。里德先生从大眼镜上方望过去。

“您喜欢巧克力吗，克雷尔太太（Mrs. Kerrel）？”他可怜巴巴地问。

“哟，喜欢呀，先生。”上了年纪的太太眉开眼笑。

===Original===
“So do I,” said Mr. Reeder. “So do I!” and he shook his head regretfully, as he replaced the chocolate carefully in the box. “Unfortunately,” he went on, “my doctor﻿—a very excellent man﻿—has forbidden me all sorts of confectionery until they have been submitted to the rigorous test of the public analyst.”

Mrs. Kerrel was a slow thinker, but a study of current advertisement columns in the daily newspaper had enlarged to a very considerable extent her scientific knowledge.

“To see if there is any vitamines in them, sir?” she suggested.

Mr. Reeder shook his head.

“No, I hardly think so,” he said gently. “Vitamines are my sole diet. I can spend a whole evening with no other company than a pair of these interesting little fellows, and take no ill from them. Thank you, Mrs. Kerrel.”

===Chinese===
“我也喜欢，”里德先生说，“我也喜欢！”他把那颗巧克力小心放回盒中，遗憾地摇着头。“不巧，”他接着说，“我的医生——一位非常高明的人士——禁止我沾一切糖果，除非它们先送交公共化验师，通过严格检验。”

克雷尔太太脑子转得慢，但研读日报上时兴的广告栏，已使她的科学知识大为扩充。

“看里头有没有维他命（vitamines），是吧，先生？”她献策道。

里德先生摇了摇头。

“不，我看不见得。”他温和地说，“维他命是我唯一的饮食。整晚只消有这一对有趣的小家伙作伴，我也安然无恙。谢谢您，克雷尔太太。”

===Original===
When she had gone, he replaced the layer of shavings with punctilious care, closed down the lid, and as carefully re-wrapped the parcel. When it was finished he addressed the package to a department at Scotland Yard, took from a small box a label printed redly “Poison.” When this was done, he scribbled a note to the gentleman affected, and addressed himself to his muffins and his large teacup.

It was a quarter-past six in the evening when he had unwrapped the chocolates. It was exactly a quarter-past eleven, as he turned out the lights preparatory to going to bed, that he said aloud:

“Marylou Plessy﻿—dear me!”

Here began the war.

===Chinese===
她走后，他一丝不苟地铺回那层纸刨花，扣上盒盖，又同样仔细地把包裹重新包好。包罢，他在包裹上写了苏格兰场某部门的地址，又从一只小盒里取出一张红字印刷的标签：“毒物”。这也办完，他给那位受牵连的绅士草草写了张便条，然后专心对付他的松饼和他的大茶杯。

他拆开巧克力是在晚上六点一刻。待他熄灯预备就寝，恰好十一点一刻，他出声说道：

“玛丽卢·普莱西——哎呀呀！”

战争由此开场。

===Original===
This was Wednesday evening; on Friday morning the toilet of Marylou Plessy was interrupted by the arrival of two men who were waiting for her when she came into the sitting-room in her negligee. They talked about fingerprints found on chocolates and other such matters.

Half an hour later a dazed woman sat in the cells at Harlboro Street and listened to an inspector’s recital of her offence. At the following sessions she went down for two years on a charge of “conveying by post to John Reeder a poisonous substance, to wit aconite, with intent to murder.”

To the last Mo Liski sat in court, his drawn, haggard face testifying to the strength of his affection for the woman in the dock. After she disappeared from the dock he went outside into the big, windy hall, and there and then made his first mistake.

===Chinese===
这是星期三晚上。星期五早晨，玛丽卢·普莱西的梳妆被人打断——她穿着晨衣走进客厅时，来了两个人正等着她。他们谈的是巧克力上发现的指纹，以及其他诸如此类的事项。

半小时后，一个晕头转向的女人坐在哈尔伯勒街（Harlboro Street）的牢房里，听一位探长宣读她的罪状。接下来的季审法庭上，她因“经邮路向约翰·里德递送毒物，即乌头碱，意图谋杀”的罪名被判两年。

莫自始至终坐在法庭里，憔悴枯槁的脸，见证着他对被告席上那个女人的深情厚爱。她从被告席消失后，他走到外面空旷风大的大厅里，就在彼时彼地，犯下了他的第一个错误。

===Original===
Mr. Reeder was putting on his woollen gloves when the dapper man strode up to him.

“Name of Reeder?”

“That is my name, sir.”

Mr. Reeder surveyed him benevolently over his glasses. He had the expectant air of one who has steeled himself to receive congratulations.

“Mine is Mo Liski. You’ve sent down a friend of mine﻿—”

===Chinese===
里德先生正往手上戴毛线手套，那个衣着利落的男人大步逼了过来。

“你是叫里德吧？”

“那正是我的名字，先生。”

里德先生从眼镜上方和蔼地打量着他。他本人则带着一副期待的神气，活像一个已横下心来、准备硬着头皮领受贺辞的人。

“我叫莫·利斯基。你判了我一位朋友——”

===Original===
“Mrs. Plessy?”

“Yes﻿—you know! Reeder, I’m going to get you for that!”

Instantly somebody behind him caught his arm in a vice and swung him round. It was a City detective.

“Take a walk with me,” he said.

===Chinese===
“普莱西太太？”

“对——你清楚！里德，这笔账我要跟你算！”

说时迟那时快，他身后有人铁钳般抓住他的胳膊，把他猛地拧了过来。那是伦敦市的一名警探。

“跟我走一趟。”他说。

===Original===
Mo went white. Remember that he owed the strength of his position to the fact that never once had he been convicted: the register did not bear his name.

“What’s the charge?” he asked huskily.

“Intimidation of a Crown witness and using threatening language,” said the officer.

Mo came up before the Aldermen at the Guildhall the next morning and was sent to prison for three weeks, and Mr. Reeder, who knew the threat would come and was ready to counter with the traditional swiftness of the mamba, felt that he had scored a point. The gang leader was, in the parlance of the law, “a convicted person.”

===Chinese===
莫的脸刷地白了。要知道，他地位的全部根基，就在于他从未被定过一次罪：犯人名册上找不着他的名字。

“什么罪名？”他嗓音发哑地问。

“恐吓王室证人并出言威胁。”警官说。

第二天上午，莫被押到市政厅（Guildhall）受高级市政官们审问，被判三周监禁。里德先生早料到那威胁必来，也备好了以曼巴世代相传的迅捷回击，自觉先赢了一分。这位帮派首领，用法律的行话说，成了“已定罪之人”。

===Original===
“I don’t think anything will happen until he comes out,” he said to Pyne, when he was offered police protection. “He will find a great deal of satisfaction in arranging the details of my﻿—um﻿—‘bashing,’ and I feel sure that he will postpone action until he is free. I had better have that protection until he comes out﻿—”

“After he comes out, you mean?”

“Until he comes out,” insisted Mr. Reeder carefully. “After﻿—well﻿—um﻿—I’d rather like to be unhampered by﻿—um﻿—police protection.”

===Chinese===
“我想，他放出来之前不会有什么动静。”有人提出给他警方保护，他对派恩说，“安排怎么——嗯——‘痛揍’我的种种细节，会给他莫大的满足，我敢肯定他会把行动推迟到自己恢复自由之后。那我不如趁他还没放出来享受这保护——”

“你是说，等他放出来之后？”

“趁他还没放出来。”里德先生字斟句酌地坚持，“之后——呃——嗯——我倒宁可不受——嗯——警方保护的掣肘。”

===Original===
Mo Liski came to his liberty with all his senses alert. The cat-caution which had, with only one break, kept him clear of trouble, dominated his every plan. Cold-bloodedly he cursed himself for jeopardising his emerald deal, and his first step was to get into touch with El Rahbut.

But there was a maddening new factor in his life: the bitter consciousness of his fallibility and the fear that the men he had ruled so completely might, in consequence, attempt to break away from their allegiance. There was something more than sentiment behind this fear. Mo drew close on fifteen thousand a year from his racecourse and clubhouse victims alone. There were pickings on the side: his “crowd” largely controlled a continental drug traffic worth thousands a year. Which may read romantic and imaginative, but was true. Not all the “bunce” came to Mo and his men. There were pickings for the carrion fowl as well as for the wolves.

===Chinese===
莫·利斯基重获自由时，感官全部张开。那份猫一般的谨慎——中间只断过一回——一向护得他远离祸事，如今主宰着他的每一项计划。他冷冷地咒骂自己害绿宝石买卖节外生枝，第一步便是重新联络埃尔·拉布特。

可他的生活里添了一个恼人的新因素：苦涩地意识到自己也会失手，随之又生怕那些被他辖制得服服帖帖的手下会因此生叛离之心。这恐惧背后，不止是感情上的因素。单是赛马场和俱乐部两处的受害客，一年就给莫进账将近一万五千镑。外快还有：他的“帮派”在很大程度上掌控着一条欧洲大陆的毒品通道，年值数千镑。这话读着像浪漫传奇，却是实情。也不是所有“横财”都进了莫和他弟兄们的腰包——狼有狼的食，食腐的鸢乌也有鸢乌的一口。

===Original===
He must fix Reeder. That was the first move. And fix him so that there was no recoil. To beat him up one night would be an easy matter, but that would look too much like carrying into execution the threat which had put him behind bars. Obviously some ingenuity was called for; some exquisite punishment more poignant than the shock of clubs.

Men of Mr. Liski’s peculiar calling do not meet their lieutenants in dark cellars, nor do they wear cloaks or masks to disguise their identities. The big six who controlled the interests serving Mo Liski came together on the night of his release, and the gathering was at a Soho restaurant, where a private dining-room was engaged in the ordinary way.

===Chinese===
他必须收拾里德。这是头一步棋。而且要收拾得毫无后患。哪天夜里把他痛揍一顿本是易事，可那未免太像把害他坐牢的那句威胁付诸实行了。显然，这需要几分匠心；需要某种比闷棍的重击更钻心的精巧惩罚。

利斯基先生这类特殊行当的人物，并不在阴暗的地窖里会见副手，也不穿斗篷、蒙面罩来遮掩身份。他放出来的当晚，掌控着为莫·利斯基效力的各项利益的六大巨头聚到一处，地点是苏荷区（Soho）一家餐馆的私人餐室，照常例订下。

===Original===
“I’m glad nobody touched him whilst I was away,” said Mo with a little smile. “I’d like to manage this game myself. I’ve been doing some thinking whilst I was in bird, and there’s a good way to deal with him.”

“He had two coppers with him all the time, or I’d have coshed him for you, Mo,” said Teddy Alfield, his chief of staff.

“And I’d have coshed you, Teddy,” said Mr. Liski ominously. “I left orders that he wasn’t to be touched, didn’t I? What do you mean by ‘you’d have coshed him’?”

===Chinese===
“我不在的时候没人动他，我很高兴。”莫微微笑道，“这一局我要亲自来办。蹲号子这阵子我想了不少，治他有个好法子。”

“他身边老跟着两个条子，不然我早替你闷了他，莫。”他的参谋长泰迪·奥尔菲尔德（Teddy Alfield）说。

“那我就闷了你，泰迪。”利斯基先生声音阴沉，“我留过话不许碰他，是不是？你那句‘早替你闷了他’是什么意思？”

===Original===
Alfield, a big-shouldered man whose speciality was the “knocking-off” of unattended motorcars, grew incoherent.

“You stick to your job,” snarled Mo. “I’ll fix Reeder. He’s got a girl in Brockley; a young woman who is always going about with him﻿—Belman’s her name and she lives nearly opposite his house. We don’t want to beat him up﻿—yet. What we want to do is to get him out of his job, and that’s easy. They fired a man in the Home Office last week because he was found at the ‘95’ Club after drinking hours.”

===Chinese===
奥尔菲尔德——一个宽肩膀的大汉，专长是“顺”走无人看管的汽车——顿时语无伦次。

“你干好你自己的活。”莫咆哮道，“里德由我来收拾。他在布罗克利挂着一个姑娘；一个总同他出双入对的年轻女人——姓贝尔曼，住处几乎正对着他的房子。我们还不想揍他——还不到时候。我们要的是把他撵出他的差事，这个容易。上礼拜内政部刚开掉一个人，因为禁酒时辰过后他被撞见还在‘95’俱乐部里。”

===Original===
He outlined a simple plan.

Margaret Belman left her office one evening and, walking to the corner of Westminster Bridge and the Embankment, looked around for Mr. Reeder. Usually, if his business permitted, he was to be found hereabouts, though of late the meetings had been very few, and when she had seen him he was usually in the company of two glum men who seated themselves on either side of him.

===Chinese===
他粗粗讲了一个简单的计划。

一天傍晚，玛格丽特·贝尔曼下班出来，走到威斯敏斯特桥与泰晤士河堤的街角，四下张望里德先生。往常只要公务脱得开身，他总在这一带；虽说近来会面寥寥，而且见到他时，他身边多半坐着两个愁眉苦脸的男人，一左一右把他夹在当中。

===Original===
She let one car pass, and had decided to catch the second which was coming slowly along the Embankment, when a parcel dropped at her feet. She looked round to see a pretty, well-dressed woman swaying with closed eyes, and had just time to catch her by the arm before she half collapsed. With her arm round the woman’s waist she assisted her to a seat providentially placed hereabouts.

“I’m so sorry﻿—thank you ever so much. I wonder if you would call me a taxi?” gasped the fainting lady.

===Chinese===
她放过了一辆车，正要搭那第二辆——它正沿着泰晤士河堤缓缓驶来——一只包裹忽然落在她脚边。她回头看见一位衣着讲究的漂亮太太，闭着眼摇摇欲坠，赶忙抢上去扶住她的胳膊，才没让她瘫倒。她揽着那女人的腰，把她搀到幸而设在近旁的一张座椅上。

“真对不起——太谢谢您了。可否劳驾替我叫辆出租汽车？”那位将晕倒的太太喘着气说。

===Original===
She spoke with a slightly foreign accent, and had the indefinable manner of a great lady; so Margaret thought.

Beckoning a cab, she assisted the woman to enter.

“Would you like me to go home with you?” asked the sympathetic girl.

“It would be good of you,” murmured the lady, “but I fear to inconvenience you﻿—it was so silly of me. My address is 105, Great Claridge Street.”

===Chinese===
她说话带一点外国口音，有一种贵妇人难以名状的气度——至少玛格丽特这么觉得。

她招手唤来一辆车，搀那女人上了车。

“要我一直送您回家吗？”好心的姑娘问。

“那真是太难为你了，”太太低声说，“可我怕太麻烦你——瞧我多没出息。我家在大克拉里奇街（Great Claridge Street）105 号。”

===Original===
She recovered sufficiently on the journey to tell Margaret that she was Madame Lemaire, and that she was the widow of a French banker. The beautiful appointments of the big house in the most fashionable part of Mayfair suggested that Madame Lemaire was a woman of some wealth. A butler opened the door, a liveried footman brought in the tea which Madame insisted on the girl taking with her.

“You are too good. I cannot be thankful enough to you, mademoiselle. I must know you better. Will you come one night to dinner? Shall we say Thursday?”

===Chinese===
路上她恢复了不少，告诉玛格丽特，她叫勒梅尔夫人（Madame Lemaire），是一位法国银行家的遗孀。那幢大宅坐落在梅费尔（Mayfair）最时髦的地段，内部陈设精美，处处显示勒梅尔夫人身家不菲。开门的是位男管家；一名穿号衣的听差端上茶来——夫人执意要姑娘陪着同用。

“您真是太好了。我对您感激不尽，小姐。我一定要同您多多亲近。哪天晚上来吃顿便饭可好？就定星期四？”

===Original===
Margaret Belman hesitated. She was human enough to be impressed by the luxury other surroundings, and this dainty lady had the appeal of refinement and charm which is so difficult to resist.

“We will dine tête-à-tête, and after﻿—some people may come for dancing. Perhaps you have a friend you would like to come?”

Margaret smiled and shook her head. Curiously enough, the word “friend” suggested only the rather awkward figure of Mr. Reeder, and somehow she could not imagine Mr. Reeder in this setting.

===Chinese===
玛格丽特·贝尔曼犹豫了。她到底是个凡人，难免被这周遭的奢华镇住；何况这位娇雅的太太自有一种教养与魅力的引力，教人难以抗拒。

“我们就两人对坐小酌，饭后——兴许有些人要来跳舞。你有没有愿意带来的朋友呢？”

玛格丽特笑着摇了摇头。说来也怪，“朋友”二字在她心里唤起的，偏偏只有里德先生那副不大灵便的身形；而且不知怎么，她没法把里德先生安放进这个场合。

===Original===
When she came out into the street and the butler had closed the door behind her, she had the first shock of the day. The object of her thoughts was standing on the opposite side of the road, a furled umbrella hooked to his arm.

“Why, Mr. Reeder!” she greeted him.

“You had seven minutes to spare,” he said, looking at his big-faced watch. “I gave you half an hour﻿—you were exactly twenty-three minutes and a few odd seconds.”

“Did you know I was there?” she asked unnecessarily.

===Chinese===
她走到街上，男管家在她身后关了门，当天的第一记惊吓便来了：她心心念念的那个人正站在马路对面，臂弯里钩着一把收拢的雨伞。

“哎呀，里德先生！”她招呼道。

“你还富余七分钟。”他看看那只大面盘手表，说道，“我给了你半个钟头——你用了二十三分零几秒，一毫不差。”

“您知道我在那儿？”她问了一句多余的话。

===Original===
“Yes﻿—I followed you. I do not like Mrs. Annie Feltham﻿—she calls herself Madame something or other. It is not a nice club.”

“Club!” she gasped.

Mr. Reeder nodded.

“They call it the Muffin Club. Curious name﻿—curious members. It is not nice.”

===Chinese===
“知道——我跟着你。我不喜欢安妮·费尔瑟姆太太（Annie Feltham）——她自称什么什么‘夫人’来着。那不是个体面的俱乐部。”

“俱乐部！”她倒吸一口气。

里德先生点点头。

“他们管它叫松饼俱乐部（Muffin Club）。名字古怪——会员也古怪。那地方不体面。”

===Original===
She asked no further questions, but allowed herself to be escorted to Brockley, wondering just why Madame had picked upon her as a likely recruit to the gaieties of Mayfair.

And now occurred the succession of incidents which at first had so puzzled Mr. Liski. He was a busy man, and almost regretted that he had not postponed putting his plan of operation into movement. That he had failed in one respect he discovered when by accident, as it seemed, he met Mr. Reeder face to face in Piccadilly.

“Good morning, Liski,” said Mr. Reeder, almost apologetically. “I was so sorry for that unfortunate contretemps, but believe me, I bear no malice. And whilst I realise that in all probability you do not share my sentiments, I have no other wish than to live on the friendliest terms with you.”

===Chinese===
她没再多问，任由他送回布罗克利，心里兀自纳闷：那位夫人究竟为什么偏偏挑中她，做梅费尔声色场中的候补客人。

接下来接连发生的一串事，起初让利斯基先生大为摸不着头脑。他是个大忙人，几乎后悔没推迟启动他的行动计划。他在皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）大街上仿佛出于偶然地同里德先生迎面相逢，这才发现自己在一个环节上失了手。

“早上好，利斯基。”里德先生说，语气近乎抱歉，“那次不愉快的龃龉，我很过意不去；但请相信，我毫无芥蒂。我也明白，十之八九你同我不所见略同，可我别无所愿，只盼同你和睦相处。”

===Original===
Liski looked at him sharply. The old man was getting scared, he thought. There was almost a tremble in his anxious voice when he put forward the olive branch.

“That’s all right, Mr. Reeder,” said Mo, with his most charming smile. “I don’t bear any malice either. After all, it was a silly thing to say, and you have your duty to do.”

He went on in this strain, stringing platitude to platitude, and Mr. Reeder listened with evidence of growing relief.

===Chinese===
利斯基锐利地打量他。老头子慌了，他心想。递橄榄枝的时候，那焦切的嗓音里几乎带着颤音。

“这就好，里德先生。”莫说着，脸上挂起他最有魅力的笑，“我也不记仇。说到底，那是句蠢话，你也是职责所在嘛。”

他照这个调门往下说，陈词滥调一句接着一句，里德先生听着，宽慰之色越来越浓。

===Original===
“The world is full of sin and trouble,” he said, shaking his head sadly; “both in high and low places vice is triumphant, and virtue thrust, like the daisies, underfoot. You don’t keep chickens, do you, Mr. Liski?”

Mo Liski shook his head.

“What a pity!” sighed Mr. Reeder. “There is so much one can learn from the domestic fowl! They are an object lesson to the unlawful. I often wonder why the Prison Commissioners do not allow the convicts at Dartmoor to engage in this harmless and instructive hobby. I was saying to Mr. Pyne early this morning, when they raided the Muffin Club﻿—what a quaint title it has﻿—”

===Chinese===
“这世上满是罪恶与苦恼，”他悲哀地摇着头说，“无论庙堂之高、江湖之远，邪恶都大获全胜，美德呢，就像雏菊，被踩在了脚下。你不养鸡吧，利斯基先生？”

莫·利斯基摇了摇头。

“多可惜呀！”里德先生叹道，“家禽身上可学的东西太多了！它们于不法之徒就是一面现成的镜子。我常纳闷，监狱委员会为什么不许达特穆尔（Dartmoor）的囚犯从事这项无害又长见识的消遣。今天一大早我还在同派恩先生说——他们查抄松饼俱乐部的时候——这名字起得多别致——”

===Original===
“Raided the Muffin Club?” said Mo quickly. “What do you mean? I’ve heard nothing about that.”

“You wouldn’t. That kind of institution would hardly appeal to you. Only we thought it was best to raid the place, though in doing so I fear I have incurred the displeasure of a young lady friend of mine who was invited to dinner there tomorrow night. As I say, chickens﻿—”

Now Mo Liski knew that his plan had miscarried. Yet he was puzzled by the man’s attitude.

===Chinese===
“查抄了松饼俱乐部？”莫立刻问，“什么意思？这事我一点风声都没听到。”

“你自然听不到。那种去处本也入不了你的眼。只是我们认为最好还是抄了那地方，虽说这么做，我怕已经开罪了我的一位年轻小姐朋友——她明晚本应邀去那儿赴宴的。还是说鸡——”

至此莫·利斯基明白，自己的计划砸了。可此人的态度又叫他迷惑。

===Original===
“Perhaps you would like to come down and see my Buff Orpingtons, Mr. Liski? I live in Brockley.” Reeder removed his glasses and glared owlishly at his companion. “Say at nine o’clock tonight; there is so much to talk about. At the same time, it would add to the comfort of all concerned if you did not arrive﻿—um﻿—conspicuously: do you understand what I mean? I should not like the people of my office, for example, to know.”
