The light was fading from a grey, blustering sky, and rain was falling fitfully, when Mr. Reeder, with his furled umbrella hooked to his arm, his coat collar turned up, stepped through the dark gateway of Brixton Prison and was led to the cell where a distracted man sat, his head upon his hands, his pale eyes gazing into vacancy.

“It’s true; it’s true! Every word.” Green almost sobbed the words.

A pallid man, inclined to be bald, with a limp yellow moustache, going grey. Reeder, with his extraordinary memory for faces, recognised him the moment he saw him, though it was some time before the recognition was mutual.

===Chinese===
灰色狂风的天空里光线渐暗，雨断断续续地下着。里德先生挽着收拢的雨伞，竖起衣领，跨进布里克斯顿监狱幽暗的大门，被人引进一间囚室——一个心神溃散的人坐在里面，头埋在手里，淡色的眼睛茫然望着虚空。

“是真的，是真的！句句是真的。”格林说这话时几乎带着哭腔。

一个面色苍白的男人，头顶渐秃，柔软的黄胡子已经花白。里德先生对面孔有超凡的记忆力，一眼便认出了他——只是过了一阵，对方才认出他来。

===Original===
“Yes, Mr. Reeder, I remember you now. You were the gentleman who caught me before. But I’ve been as straight as a die. I’ve never taken a farthing that didn’t belong to me. What my poor girl will think—”

“Are you married?” asked Mr. Reeder sympathetically.

“No, but I was going to be—rather late in life. She’s nearly thirty years younger than me, and the best girl that ever—”

Reeder listened to the rhapsody that followed, the melancholy deepening in his face.

===Chinese===
“对了，里德先生，我如今想起您了。您就是从前逮住我的那位先生。可这些年我清清白白，规规矩矩。一个不属于自己的铜子儿我也没拿过。我那可怜的姑娘会怎么想啊——”

“您成家了吗？”里德先生同情地问。

“没有，不过我正打算成家——这辈子是晚了点。她比我年轻将近三十岁，是天底下最好的姑娘——”

里德听着他随后那一长篇热情洋溢的颂赞，脸上的阴郁愈来愈深。

===Original===
“She hasn’t been into the court, thank God, but she knows the truth. A friend of mine told me that she has been absolutely knocked out.”

“Poor soul!” Mr. Reeder shook his head.

“It happened on her birthday, too,” the man went on bitterly.

“Did she know you were going away?”

“Yes, I told her the night before. I’m not going to bring her into the case. If we’d been properly engaged it would be different; but she’s married and is divorcing her husband, but the decree hasn’t been made absolute yet. That’s why I never went about with her or saw much of her. And of course, nobody knew about our engagement, although we lived in the same street.”

“Firling Avenue?” asked Reeder, and the bank manager nodded despondently.

===Chinese===
“谢天谢地，她没上过法庭，可她知道了实情。一位朋友告诉我，她整个人都垮了。”

“可怜的人！”里德先生摇了摇头。

“偏偏事情就出在她生日那天。”男人苦苦地说下去。

“她知道您要走吗？”

“知道，前一晚我跟她说了。我不打算把她牵进案子来。若是我们正式订了婚，那另当别论；可她结过婚，正在跟丈夫闹离婚，判令还没有最终生效。所以我从不带她出门，也很少同她会面。当然喽，我们订婚的事谁也不知道，虽然我们住同一条街。”

“费灵大道？”里德问，银行经理颓然点头。

===Original===
“She was married when she was seventeen to a brute. It was pretty galling for me, having to keep quiet about it—I mean, for nobody to know about our engagement. All sorts of rotten people were making up to her, and I had just to grind my teeth and say nothing. Impossible people! Why, that fool Burnett, who arrested me, he was sweet on her; used to write her poetry—you wouldn’t think it possible in a policeman, would you?”

The outrageous incongruity of a poetical policeman did not seem to shock the detective.

“There is poetry in every soul, Mr. Green,” he said gently, “and a policeman is a man.”

===Chinese===
“她十七岁上嫁给一个畜生。这滋味让我难受——订了婚却得瞒着所有人——我意思是，谁也不知道我们的婚约。各路不堪的下三滥都往她跟前凑，我只能咬碎了牙往肚里咽。没辙！嗐，逮我的那个傻瓜伯内特，就在追她；还给她写诗——一个警察能干出这个，您想得到吗？”

一个写诗的警察——这荒唐的不协调似乎并未让侦探吃惊。

“人人心里都有诗，格林先生。”他温和地说，“警察也是人。”

===Original===
Though he dismissed the eccentricity of the constable so lightly, the poetical policeman filled his mind all the way home to his house in the Brockley Road, and occupied his thoughts for the rest of his waking time.

It was a quarter to eight o’clock in the morning, and the world seemed entirely populated by milkmen and whistling newspaper boys, when Mr. J. G. Reeder came into Firling Avenue.

He stopped only for a second outside the bank, which had long since ceased to be an object of local awe and fearfulness, and pursued his way down the broad avenue. On either side of the thoroughfare ran a row of pretty villas—pretty although they bore a strong family resemblance to one another; each house with its little forecourt, sometimes laid out simply as a grass plot, sometimes decorated with flowerbeds. Green’s house was the eighteenth in the road on the right-hand side. Here he had lived with a cook-housekeeper, and apparently gardening was not his hobby, for the forecourt was covered with grass that had been allowed to grow at its will.

===Chinese===
尽管他这样轻描淡写地打发了这位警员的怪癖，那位诗人警察却一路占满了他的脑子，直到回到布罗克利路的家中，此后醒着的时光里也始终盘踞心头。

早晨七点三刻，全世界仿佛只剩送奶工和吹口哨的报童，约翰·G·里德先生走进了费灵大道。

他在银行门外只停了一秒——那地方早已不再是本地人望而生畏的所在——便继续沿宽阔的大道走去。大道两侧各是一排漂亮的小楼——虽然彼此像一家人似的相像，却确乎漂亮；每座房子都有个小前院，有的简简单单铺成草地，有的装点着花坛。格林的房子在路的右手第十八座。他同一位厨娘兼管家住在这里；看得出园艺不是他的爱好，前院里的野草恣意疯长。

===Original===
Before the twenty-sixth house in the road Mr. Reeder paused and gazed with mild interest at the blue blinds which covered every window. Evidently Miss Magda Grayne was a lover of flowers, for geraniums filled the window-boxes and were set at intervals along the tiny border under the bow window. In the centre of the grass plot was a circular flowerbed with one flowerless rose tree, the leaves of which were drooping and brown.

As he raised his eyes to the upper window, the blind went up slowly, and he was dimly conscious that there was a figure behind the white lace curtains. Mr. Reeder walked hurriedly away, as one caught in an immodest act, and resumed his peregrinations until he came to the big nursery gardener’s which formed the corner lot at the far end of the road.

Here he stood for some time in contemplation, his arm resting on the iron railings, his eyes staring blankly at the vista of greenhouses. He remained in this attitude so long that one of the nurserymen, not unnaturally thinking that a stranger was seeking a way into the gardens, came over with the laborious gait of the man who wrings his living from the soil, and asked if he was wanting anybody.

“Several people,” sighed Mr. Reeder; “several people!”

===Chinese===
到路上第二十六座房子前，里德先生驻了足，饶有兴味地端详着遮满每扇窗子的蓝色百叶帘。玛格达·格雷恩小姐（Magda Grayne）显然爱花：窗箱里栽满天竺葵，凸窗下那条窄窄的花境里也隔三差五种着一丛丛。草地中央有个圆形花坛，坛里一株玫瑰不开花，叶子蔫头耷脑，一片枯黄。

他抬眼望向上层窗户时，百叶帘正缓缓卷起，他隐约觉出白纱帘后头有个人影。里德先生匆匆走开，像做了什么不体面的事被人撞见似的，又接着踱他的方步，一直走到大路尽头拐角处的那家大苗圃花场。

他在这儿伫立良久，胳膊搭在铁栏杆上，目光怔怔地望着那一排排温室。这姿势保持得太久，一个苗圃工人便走了过来——不怪他多心，只当这生人想寻路进园子——迈着从土里刨食的人特有的那种吃力的步子，问他找谁。

“找好几位呢。”里德先生叹道，“好几位！”

===Original===
Leaving the resentful man to puzzle out his impertinence, he slowly retraced his steps. At No. 412 he stopped again, opened the little iron gate and passed up the path to the front door. A small girl answered his knock and ushered him into the parlour.

The room was not well furnished; it was scarcely furnished at all. A strip of almost new linoleum covered the passage; the furniture of the parlour itself was made up of wicker chairs, a square of art carpet and a table. He heard the sound of feet above his head, feet on bare boards, and then presently the door opened and a girl came in.

She was pretty in a heavy way, but on her face he saw the marks of sorrow. It was pale and haggard; the eyes looked as though she had been recently weeping.

===Chinese===
他把满腔不快的工人撂在那里琢磨这句没头没脑的话，自己慢慢折返。走到 412 号门前他又停住，推开小铁门，沿小径走到正门。一个小女孩应门，把他领进客厅。

屋里陈设算不上好；简直说不上有陈设。过道铺着一条几乎全新的油地毡；客厅的家当不过是几把藤椅、一方花哨地毯和一张桌子。他听见头顶传来脚步声，光脚板踩在裸木地板上的声音。不多时门开了，一个年轻女人走了进来。

她有种浓艳的美，可他看得出她脸上的愁容。脸色苍白憔悴；那双眼睛像是才哭过。

===Original===
“Miss Magda Grayne?” he asked, rising as she came in.

She nodded.

“Are you from the police?” she asked quickly.

“Not exactly the police,” he corrected carefully. “I hold an—er—an appointment in the office of the Public Prosecutor, which is analogous, to, but distinct from, a position in the Metropolitan Police Force.”

She frowned, and then:

“I wondered if anybody would come to see me,” she said. “Mr. Green sent you?”

“Mr. Green told me of your existence: he did not send me.”

===Chinese===
“玛格达·格雷恩小姐？”她进来时，他起身问。

她点点头。

“您是警察局来的？”她急急地问。

“不完全是警察。”他小心地更正，“我在——呃——检察总长公署任职，这个职务，与伦敦警察厅的职位类似，却又有区别。”

她皱起眉，随即：

“我还当会不会有人来看我呢。”她说，“是格林先生让您来的？”

“格林先生向我提起过您：他并没有差我来。”

===Original===
There came to her face in that second a look which almost startled him. Only for a fleeting space of time, the expression had dawned and passed almost before the untrained eye could detect its passage.

“I was expecting somebody to come,” she said. Then: “What made him do it?” she asked.

“You think he is guilty?”

“The police think so.” She drew a long sigh. “I wish to God I had never seen this place!”

He did not answer; his eyes were roving round the apartment. On a bamboo table was an old vase which had been clumsily filled with golden chrysanthemums, of a peculiarly beautiful variety. Not all, for amidst them flowered a large Michaelmas daisy that had the forlorn appearance of a parvenu that had strayed by mistake into noble company.

===Chinese===
那一瞬间她脸上掠过一种神情，几乎叫他吃了一惊。那表情一闪即逝，未经训练的眼睛几乎捕捉不到它的来去。

“我就知道会有人来。”她说。接着又问：“他为什么要那么干？”

“您认为他有罪？”

“警察这么认为。”她长叹一声，“老天在上，我真希望从没见过这个地方！”

他没有作声；目光在屋里逡巡。一张竹桌上摆着一只旧花瓶，笨手笨脚地插满了金黄的菊花，品种美得出奇。也不全是菊花——花丛中还开着一朵硕大的紫菀，一副孤苦伶仃的模样，活像个误入贵人堆里的暴发户。

===Original===
“You’re fond of flowers?” he murmured.

She looked at the vase indifferently.

“Yes, I like flowers,” she said. “The girl put them in there.” Then: “Do you think they will hang him?”

The brutality of the question, put without hesitation, pained Reeder.

“It is a very serious charge,” he said. And then: “Have you a photograph of Mr. Green?”

She frowned.

“Yes; do you want it?”

He nodded.

===Chinese===
“您爱花？”他轻声问。

她漠然看了看花瓶。

“是的，我喜欢花。”她说，“是那小丫头插的。”接着又问：“您说，他们会绞死他吗？”

这一问毫不迟疑，问得残忍，里德听了心里发痛。

“这指控非同小可。”他说。随后又道：“您有格林先生的照片吗？”

她皱起眉。

“有；您要吗？”

他点点头。

===Original===
She had hardly left the room before he was at the bamboo table and had lifted out the flowers. As he had seen through the glass, they were roughly tied with a piece of string. He examined the ends, and here again his first observation had been correct: none of these flowers had been cut; they had been plucked bodily from their stalks. Beneath the string was the paper which had been first wrapped about the stalks. It was a page torn from a notebook; he could see the red lines, but the pencilled writing was indecipherable.

As her foot sounded on the stairs, he replaced the flowers in the vase, and when she came in he was looking through the window into the street.

“Thank you,” he said, as he took the photograph from her.

It bore an affectionate inscription on the back.

===Chinese===
她前脚出屋，他后脚便到了竹桌边，把那捧花从瓶里提了出来。正如他隔着玻璃看到的，花用一根细绳草草捆着。他察看花茎末端，他最初的观察又对了一回：这些花都不是剪下的，是连花带茎从植株上生生拔下来的。绳子底下垫着纸，原先就裹在花茎上。那是从笔记本上撕下的一页；他看得见纸上的红线，铅笔字却无从辨认。

楼梯上响起她的脚步声，他把花原样插回瓶中；她进屋时，他正望着窗外的街道。

“谢谢。”他说着，从她手里接过照片。

照片背面题着一句情意绵绵的赠言。

===Original===
“You’re married, he tells me, madam?”

“Yes, I am married, and practically divorced,” she said shortly.

“Have you been living here long?”

“About three months,” she answered. “It was his wish that I should live here.”

He looked at the photograph again.

“Do you know Constable Burnett?”

He saw a dull flush come to her face and die away again.

“Yes, I know the sloppy fool!” she said viciously. And then, realising that she had been surprised into an expression which was not altogether ladylike, she went on, in a softer tone: “Mr. Burnett is rather sentimental, and I don’t like sentimental people, especially—well, you understand, Mr.—”

“Reeder,” murmured that gentleman.

“You understand, Mr. Reeder, that when a girl is engaged and in my position, those kind of attentions are not very welcome.”

===Chinese===
“他告诉我，您已经结婚了，夫人？”

“是，我结了婚，婚也离得差不多了。”她简慢地说。

“您在这儿住得久吗？”

“三个月左右。”她答道，“是他要我住在这儿的。”

他又看了看照片。

“您认识伯内特警员吗？”

他看见一片暗红涌上她的脸，又慢慢退去。

“认识，那个腻腻歪歪的傻瓜！”她恶狠狠地说。随即意识到自己一时失态，说了句不甚淑女的话，便放缓了声调接着说：“伯内特先生太多愁善感，我不喜欢多愁善感的人，尤其——唉，您明白的，先——”

“里德。”那位先生低声补上。

“您明白，里德先生，一个姑娘订了婚、又处在我这种境地，那一套殷勤可不受欢迎。”

===Original===
Reeder was looking at her keenly. Of her sorrow and distress there could be no doubt. On the subject of the human emotions, and the ravages they make upon the human countenance, Mr. Reeder was almost as great an authority as Mantegazza.

“On your birthday,” he said. “How very sad! You were born on the seventeenth of October. You are English, of course?”

“Yes, I’m English,” she said shortly. “I was born in Walworth—in Wallington. I once lived in Walworth.”

“How old are you?”

“Twenty-three,” she answered.

Mr. Reeder took off his glasses and polished them on a large silk handkerchief.

“The whole thing is inexpressibly sad,” he said. “I am glad to have had the opportunity of speaking with you, young lady. I sympathise with you very deeply.”

And in this unsatisfactory way he took his departure.

===Chinese===
里德锐利地端详着她。她的悲戚与苦痛做不得假。论人类情感、以及情感在人脸上留下的创痕这门学问，里德先生几乎与曼特加扎齐名，也算一大权威。

“偏偏在您生日那天。”他说，“多么不幸！您生于十月十七日。您是英国人，想必无疑？”

“是，我是英国人。”她简慢地说，“我生在沃尔沃思——生在沃灵顿。我从前住过沃尔沃思。”

“您多大年纪？”

“二十三。”她答。

里德先生摘下眼镜，用一方大绸帕擦拭着。

“这整桩事情，不幸得难以言表。”他说，“能有机会同您谈谈，我很高兴，小姐。我对您深表同情。”

他就这样不得要领地告辞了。

===Original===
She closed the door on him, saw him stop in the middle of the path and pick up something from a border bed, and wondered, frowning, why this middle-aged man had picked up the horseshoe she had thrown through the window the night before. Into Mr. Reeder’s tail pocket went this piece of rusted steel and then he continued his thoughtful way to the nursery gardens, for he had a few questions to ask.

The men of Section 10 were parading for duty when Mr. Reeder came timidly into the charge room and produced his credentials to the inspector in charge.

“Oh, yes, Mr. Reeder,” said that officer affably. “We have had a note from the P.P.’s office, and I think I had the pleasure of working with you on that big slush1 case a few years ago. Now what can I do for you? … Burnett? Yes, he’s here.”

He called the man’s name and a young and good-looking officer stepped from the ranks.

===Chinese===
她关上门，看见他在小径中段停下，从花境里拾起一样东西，不由皱起眉，纳闷这个中年男人为什么拾走她前一晚从窗户扔出去的那只马蹄铁。这片锈铁进了里德先生的后裙袋，他又一路沉思着往苗圃去，因为他有几句话要问。

第 10 分队的警员正列队待勤，里德先生怯生生地走进值班室，向主管警长出示证件。

“哦，是里德先生。”那位警官和蔼地说，“检察总长公署的条子我们收到了。几年前那起伪钞（slush）大案，我想还有幸同您共过事。如今我能为您效什么劳？……伯内特？在，他在。”

他唤了那名字，一名年轻俊朗的警员从队列里跨出。

===Original===
“He’s the man who discovered the murder—he’s marked for promotion,” said the inspector. “Burnett, this gentleman is from the Public Prosecutor’s office and he wants a little talk with you. Better use my office, Mr. Reeder.”

The young policeman saluted and followed the shuffling figure into the privacy of the inspector’s office. He was a confident young man: already his name and portrait had appeared in the newspapers, the hint of promotion had become almost an accomplished fact, and before his eyes was the prospect of a supreme achievement.

“They tell me that you are something of a poet, officer,” said Mr. Reeder.

Burnett blushed.

“Why, yes, sir. I write a bit,” he confessed.

===Chinese===
“命案就是他发现的——他内定要升职了。”警长说，“伯内特，这位先生是检察总长公署来的，想同你谈几句。里德先生，就用我的办公室吧。”

年轻警察敬了个礼，跟着那拖着脚的身影进了警长办公室，掩上门密谈。他是个自信满满的年轻人：姓名和照片已经登上报纸，升职的暗示几乎已成定局，眼前更有一桩丰功伟绩的前景。

“听说您算得上一位诗人呢，警员。”里德先生说。

伯内特红了脸。

“嗐，是的长官。我写过一点。”他承认。

===Original===
“Love poems, yes?” asked the other gently. “One finds time in the night—er—for such fancies. And there is no inspiration like—er—love, officer.”

Burnett’s face was crimson.

“I’ve done a bit of writing in the night, sir,” he said, “though I’ve never neglected my duty.”

“Naturally,” murmured Mr. Reeder. “You have a poetical mind. It was a poetical thought to pluck flowers in the middle of the night—”

“The nurseryman told me I could take any flowers I wanted,” Burnett interrupted hastily. “I did nothing wrong.”

===Chinese===
“情诗，对吧？”对方温和地问，“夜里——呃——总有工夫琢磨这些情思。而世上没有哪种灵感比得上——呃——爱情，警员。”

伯内特的脸涨得通红。

“夜里我是写过一点，长官。”他说，“可我从没耽误过职守。”

“那自然。”里德先生喃喃道，“您有一副诗人的头脑。半夜里去摘花——这个念头颇有诗意——”

“苗圃工人跟我说过，想要什么花尽管拿。”伯内特急忙插话，“我没做错事。”

===Original===
Reeder inclined his head in agreement.

“That I know. You picked the flowers in the dark—by the way, you inadvertently included a Michaelmas daisy with your chrysanthemums—tied up your little poem to them and left them on the doorstep with—er—a horseshoe. I wondered what had become of that horseshoe.”

“I threw them up on to her—to the lady’s windowsill,” corrected the uncomfortable young man. “As a matter of fact, the idea didn’t occur to me until I had passed the house—”

Mr. Reeder’s face was thrust forward.

===Chinese===
里德先生颔首赞同。

“这我知道。您摸黑采了花——顺便说一句，您不小心把一朵紫菀混进了菊花里——把您那首小诗系上，连——呃——一只马蹄铁一起，留在了人家门口。我正纳闷那只马蹄铁下落如何呢。”

“我是把它们抛上她的——抛上那位小姐的窗台。”这浑身不自在的年轻人更正道，“其实，走过那房子之后我才想起这个主意——”

里德先生的脸探了过来。

===Original===
“This is what I want to confirm,” he said softly. “The idea of leaving the flowers did not occur to you until you had passed her house? The horseshoe suggested the thought? Then you went back, picked the flowers, tied them up with the little poem you had already written, and tossed them up to her window—we need not mention the lady’s name.”

Constable Burnett’s face was a study.

“I don’t know how you guessed that, but it is a fact. If I’ve done anything wrong—”

“It is never wrong to be in love,” said Mr. J. G. Reeder soberly. “Love is a very beautiful experience—I have frequently read about it.”

===Chinese===
“这正是我想确认的。”他轻声说，“走过她的房子之后，您才起意留下那些花？是那马蹄铁触发的灵感？然后您折回去，采了花，用您早已写好的小诗捆上，抛上了她的窗台——那位小姐的姓名，我们就不必提了。”

伯内特警员的脸成了一幅耐人寻味的画。

“我不知道您怎么猜着的，可事实如此。我要是做错了什么——”

“谈恋爱永远谈不上错。”约翰·G·里德先生一本正经地说，“爱情是一种非常美妙的体验——我时常读到。”

===Original===
Miss Magda Grayne had dressed to go out for the afternoon and was putting on her hat, when she saw the queer man who had called so early that morning, walking up the tessellated path. Behind him she recognised a detective engaged in the case. The servant was out; nobody could be admitted except by herself. She walked quickly behind the dressing-table into the bay of the window and glanced up and down the road. Yes, there was the taxicab which usually accompanies such visitations, and, standing by the driver, another man, obviously a “busy.”

She pulled up the overlay of her bed, took out the flat pad of banknotes that she found, and thrust them into her handbag, then, stepping on tiptoe, she went out to the landing, into the unfurnished back room, and, opening the window, dropped to the flat roof of the kitchen. In another minute she was in the garden and through the back gate. A narrow passage divided the two lines of villas that backed on one another. She was in High Street and had boarded a car before Mr. Reeder grew tired of knocking. To the best of his knowledge Mr. Reeder never saw her again.

---

===Chinese===
玛格达·格雷恩小姐已换好衣裳准备下午出门，正戴帽子，一眼看见清早来访的那个怪人正走上镶嵌花纹的小径。她还认出他身后跟着一个专办此案的侦探。女仆出去了；除了她自己，谁也进不了门。她快步绕过梳妆台躲进窗龛，朝马路两头张望。不错，那辆这类造访惯常随行的出租汽车正停着，司机身旁还站着一个人，一望便知是个“包打听”。

她掀起床褥，摸出藏在下面的一沓扁平钞票，塞进手提包；随后踮着脚尖出了房门到楼梯口，闪进那间没有家具的后屋，推开窗子，跳下厨房的平屋顶。不到一分钟，她已在花园里，穿出了后门。两条背靠背的小楼之间隔着一条窄巷。等里德先生敲得不耐烦时，她已到了大街，上了电车。就里德先生所知，此后他再没见过她。

---

===Original===
At the Public Prosecutor’s request, he called at his chief’s house after dinner and told his surprising story.

“Green, who had the unusual experience of being promoted to his position over the heads of his seniors, for special services he rendered during the war, was undoubtedly an ex-convict, and he spoke the truth when he said that he had received a letter from a man who had served a period of imprisonment with him. The name of this blackmailer is, or rather was, Arthur George Crater, whose other name was Malling!”

“Not the night watchman?” said the Public Prosecutor, in amazement.

Mr. Reeder nodded.

===Chinese===
应检察总长之邀，他晚饭后到上司家中登门拜访，讲出了那个惊人的故事。

“格林因大战期间的殊勋，越过众多资历更深的同僚被提拔到经理之位，这段际遇本属罕见；而他无疑是个刑满释放犯。他说收到过一个狱友的敲诈信，这话不假。这敲诈者的名字，现在——或者说从前——叫阿瑟·乔治·克雷特，他另有一个名字，叫马林！”

“莫非就是那个守夜人？”检察总长愕然道。

里德先生点点头。

===Original===
“Yes, sir, it was Arthur Malling. His daughter, Miss Magda Crater, was, as she very truly said, born at Walworth on the 17th of October, 1900. She said Wallington after, but Walworth first. One observes that when people adopt false family names, they seldom change their given names, and the ‘Magda’ was easy to identify.

“Evidently Malling had planned this robbery of the bank very carefully. He had brought his daughter, in a false name, to Ealing, and had managed to get her introduced to Mr. Green. Magda’s job was to worm her way into Green’s confidence and learn all that she could. Possibly it was part of her duty to secure casts of the keys. Whether Malling recognised in the manager an old prison acquaintance, or whether he obtained the facts from the girl, we shall never know. But when the information came to him, he saw, in all probability, an opportunity of robbing the bank and of throwing suspicion upon the manager.

“The girl’s role was that of a woman who was to be divorced, and I must confess this puzzled me until I realised that in no circumstances would Malling wish his daughter’s name to be associated with the bank manager.

“The night of the seventeenth was chosen for the raid. Malling’s plan to get rid of the manager had succeeded. He saw the letter on the table in Green’s private office, read it, secured the keys—although he had in all probability a duplicate set—and at a favourable moment cleared as much portable money from the bank vaults as he could carry, hurried them round to the house in Firling Avenue, where they were buried in the central bed of the front garden, under a rose bush—I rather imagined there was something interfering with the nutrition of that unfortunate bush the first time I saw it. I can only hope that the tree is not altogether dead, and I have given instructions that it shall be replanted and well fertilised.”

“Yes, yes,” said the Prosecutor, who was not at all interested in horticulture.

===Chinese===
“正是，先生，正是阿瑟·马林。他的女儿玛格达·克雷特小姐，正如她千真万确说过的那样，生于 1900 年 10 月 17 日，地点沃尔沃思。她后头改口说沃灵顿，可先说的是沃尔沃思。人们可以发现，凡冒用假姓的，很少连名也改掉，这个‘玛格达’一查便着。

“显然，马林为这桩银行劫案筹划已久。他把女儿以假名带到伊灵，设法让她结识了格林先生。玛格达的任务，是慢慢取得格林的信任，能打探多少打探多少。搞到钥匙的蜡模，或许也在她的差事之内。至于马林是认出了这位经理是当年的狱友，还是从女儿嘴里得了实情，我们永远不得而知了。但消息一到他手上，他十有八九便看到了机会：抢银行，又能把嫌疑栽到经理头上。

“姑娘扮演的是一个即将离婚的女人。我必须承认，这一点让我困惑了很久，直到我明白：无论如何，马林都不愿女儿的名字同这位银行经理扯上干系。

“动手定在十七日夜里。马林支开经理的计谋得逞了。他看见格林私人办公室桌上的信，读了，拿了钥匙——尽管他多半本有一套复制的——趁一个合适当口，把金库里搬得动的钱尽量搜刮一空，急急绕到费灵大道那座房子，把钱埋进前院中央的花坛，藏在一丛玫瑰底下——我头一回看见那丛不幸的玫瑰，就隐约觉出有什么东西妨碍着它的养分。但愿那株树还没有全死，我已吩咐人将它移栽，好生施肥。”

“是，是。”检察总长说，他对园艺毫无兴趣。

===Original===
“In planting the tree, as he did in some haste, Malling scratched his hand. Roses have thorns—I went to Ealing to find the rose bush that had scratched his hand. Hurrying back to the bank, he waited, knowing that Constable Burnett was due at a certain time. He had prepared the can of chloroform, the handcuffs and straps were waiting for him, and he stood at the corner of the street until he saw the flash of Burnett’s lamp; then, running into the bank and leaving the door ajar, he strapped himself, fastened the handcuffs and lay down, expecting that the policeman would arrive, find the open door and rescue him before much harm was done.

“But Constable Burnett had had some pleasant exchanges with the daughter. Doubtless she had received instructions from her father to be as pleasant to him as possible. Burnett was a poetical young man, knew it was her birthday, and as he walked along the street his foot struck an old horseshoe and the idea occurred to him that he should return, attach the horseshoe to some flowers, which the nurseryman had given him permission to pick, and leave his little bouquet, to so speak, at his lady’s feet—a poetical idea, and one worthy of the finest traditions of the Metropolitan Police Force. This he did, but it took some time; and all the while this young man was philandering—Arthur Crater was dying!

“In a few seconds after lying down he must have passed from consciousness… the chloroform still dripped, and when the policeman eventually reached the bank, ten minutes after he was due, the man was dead!”

===Chinese===
“重栽那株玫瑰时他手忙脚乱，手叫玫瑰划破了。玫瑰有刺——我专程到伊灵，去找那丛划破他手的玫瑰。埋完钱他匆匆折回银行，静候时机，因为他知道伯内特警员几点会到。氯仿那罐他早备好了，手铐和皮带也在等着他。他立在街角，直到望见伯内特灯光一闪；随即跑进银行，让门虚掩着，把自己的腿捆上，铐上手铐，躺下，只等警察来到，发现敞开的门，赶在他受重伤之前救他一命。

“可伯内特警员同那位女儿有过几番愉快的交谈。无疑她奉了父命，要对他尽量和颜悦色。伯内特是个诗人气质的年轻人，知道那天是她的生日；走在街上，脚踢到一只旧马蹄铁，灵机一动：该折回去，把马蹄铁拴在花上——苗圃工人准过他采花——把那束小花，可以说，敬献到小姐的脚下——一个富有诗意的念头，无愧于伦敦警察厅最优秀的传统。他照办了，可这费了些工夫；这位年轻人风花雪月的当口——阿瑟·克雷特正在咽气！

“躺下几秒钟后，他必定就失了知觉……氯仿还在滴；等警察终于走到银行——比预定时刻晚了十分钟——人已经死了！”

===Original===
The Public Prosecutor sat back in his padded chair and frowned at his new subordinate.

“How on earth did you piece together all this?” he asked in wonder.

Mr. Reeder shook his head sadly.

“I have that perversion,” he said. “It is a terrible misfortune, but it is true. I see evil in everything … in dying rose bushes, in horseshoes—in poetry even. I have the mind of a criminal. It is deplorable!”

===Chinese===
检察总长往软垫椅背上一靠，皱眉打量这位新下属。

“这一切你到底是怎么拼拢起来的？”他惊奇地问。

里德先生悲哀地摇了摇头。

“我有那毛病。”他说，“这是天大的不幸，却是实情。我看样样东西都见着恶……枯萎的玫瑰、马蹄铁——连诗里也有。我有一副罪犯的头脑。可悲可叹！”

## The Stealer of Marble / 大理石窃贼

===Original===
Margaret Belman’s chiefest claim to Mr. Reeder’s notice was that she lived in the Brockley Road, some few doors from his own establishment. He did not know her name, being wholly incurious about law-abiding folk, but he was aware that she was pretty, that her complexion was that pink and white which is seldom seen away from a magazine cover. She dressed well, and if there was one thing that he noted about her more than any other, it was that she walked and carried herself with a certain grace that was especially pleasing to a man of aesthetic predilections.

He had, on occasions, walked behind her and before her, and had ridden on the same street car with her to Westminster Bridge. She invariably descended at the corner of the Embankment, and was as invariably met by a good-looking young man and walked away with him. The presence of that young man was a source of passive satisfaction to Mr. Reeder, for no particular reason, unless it was that he had a tidy mind, and preferred a rose when it had a background of fern and grew uneasy at the sight of a saucerless cup.

It did not occur to him that he was an object of interest and curiosity to Miss Belman.

===Chinese===
玛格丽特·贝尔曼（Margaret Belman）之所以能入里德先生（Mr. J. G. Reeder）的法眼，最主要的原因不过是她住在布罗克利路，与他的寓所相隔几家门面。他并不知道她的名字——他对守法良民向来毫无好奇心——却知道她生得漂亮，那种白里透红的娇嫩肤色，离开了杂志封面便难得一见。她衣着讲究，而若说他对她有什么比旁的更留意的，那就是她走路的姿态、举手投足间自有一种风韵，格外能愉悦他这种审美趣味特殊的人。

他偶尔走在她身后，或与她迎面错过，也曾同乘一辆电车到威斯敏斯特桥。她总在泰晤士河堤的拐角下车，也总会被一个英俊的青年迎住，双双走远。那青年的存在让里德先生隐隐感到满意，说不出什么特别的缘故——除非是因为他这人讲究井井有条：喜欢一枝有蕨叶衬底的玫瑰，见到一只没有托碟的茶杯便会心下不安。

他却从未想到，自己在贝尔曼小姐眼里，也是个引人注目、令人好奇的人物。

===Original===
“That was Mr. Reeder—he has something to do with the police, I think,” she said.

“Mr. J. G. Reeder?”

Roy Master looked back with interest at the middle-aged man scampering fearfully across the road, his unusual hat on the back of his head, his umbrella over his shoulder like a cavalryman’s sword.

“Good Lord! I never dreamt he was like that.”

===Chinese===
“那就是里德先生——我想，他和警方有点关系。”她说。

“J. G. 里德先生？”

罗伊·马斯特（Roy Master）饶有兴致地回头望去：那中年男人正提心吊胆地小跑着横穿马路，古怪的帽子扣在后脑勺上，雨伞像骑兵的马刀一样扛在肩头。

“天哪！我做梦也想不到他是这么副模样。”

===Original===
“Who is he?” she asked, distracted from her own problem.

“Reeder? He’s in the Public Prosecutor’s Department, a sort of a detective—there was a case the other week where he gave evidence. He used to be with the Bank of England—”

Suddenly she stopped, and he looked at her in surprise.

“What’s the matter?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“他是干什么的？”她问道，一时忘了自己的心事。

“里德？他在检察总长公署当差，算是某种侦探——前几个星期有桩案子，他还出庭作证来着。他从前在英格兰银行干过——”

她突然收住话头，他诧异地看着她。

“怎么了？”他问。

===Original===
“I don’t want you to go any farther, Roy,” she said. “Mr. Telfer saw me with you yesterday, and he’s quite unpleasant about it.”

“Telfer?” said the young man indignantly. “That little worm! What did he say?”

“Nothing very much,” she replied, but from her tone he gathered that the “nothing very much” had been a little disturbing.

“I am leaving Telfers,” she said unexpectedly. “It is a good job, and I shall never get another like it—I mean, so far as the pay is concerned.”

===Chinese===
“罗伊，我不想让你再送了，”她说，“特尔弗先生昨天看见我和你在一起，他为这事很不痛快。”

“特尔弗？”青年愤愤地说，“那条小蠕虫！他说什么了？”

“也没什么，”她答道，但听她的口气，他明白那句“也没什么”其实相当烦人。

“我要离开特尔弗公司了，”她冷不丁地说，“这是份好差事，我再也找不着这样的了——我是说，就薪水而论。”

===Original===
Roy Master did not attempt to conceal his satisfaction.

“I’m jolly glad,” he said vigorously. “I can’t imagine how you’ve endured that boudoir atmosphere so long. What did he say?” he asked again, and, before she could answer: “Anyway, Telfers are shaky. There are all sorts of queer rumours about them in the City.”

“But I thought it was a very rich corporation!” she said in astonishment.

He shook his head.

===Chinese===
罗伊·马斯特毫不掩饰他的满意。

“我太高兴了，”他劲头十足地说，“真想不到你怎么忍受了那么久的闺房气氛。他说什么了？”他又问了一遍，不等她回答便接着说，“反正特尔弗公司根基不稳，伦敦城里关于他们的怪话传得沸沸扬扬。”

“可我一直以为那是家财大气粗的公司！”她惊讶地说。

他摇了摇头。

===Original===
“It was—but they have been doing lunatic things—what can you expect when a halfwitted weakling like Sidney Telfer is at the head of affairs? They underwrote three concerns last year that no brokerage business would have touched with a barge-pole, and they had to take up the shares. One was a lost treasure company to raise a Spanish galleon that sank three hundred years ago! But what really did happen yesterday morning?”

“I will tell you tonight,” she said, and made her hasty adieux.

Mr. Sidney Telfer had arrived when she went into a room which, in its luxurious appointments, its soft carpet and dainty etceteras, was not wholly undeserving of Roy Masters’ description.

===Chinese===
“从前是——可他们净干些疯事——让西德尼·特尔弗（Sidney Telfer）这么个低能的软骨头当家，还能指望什么？去年他们包销了三家公司，随便哪家经纪行都避之唯恐不及，结果只好自己把股票吃下来。其中一家是寻宝公司，要去打捞一艘三百年前沉没的西班牙大帆船！话说回来，昨天早上到底出什么事了？”

“晚上我再告诉你，”她说完，匆匆道别而去。

她走进那间屋子时，西德尼·特尔弗先生已经到了。那间屋子陈设奢华、地毯柔软、处处是精致的小玩意儿，倒也不全枉担罗伊·马斯特那句“闺房气氛”的讥评。

===Original===
The head of Telfers Consolidated seldom visited his main office on Threadneedle Street. The atmosphere of the place, he said, depressed him; it was all so horrid and sordid and rough. The founder of the firm, his grandfather, had died ten years before Sidney had been born, leaving the business to a son, a chronic invalid, who had died a few weeks after Sidney first saw the light. In the hands of trustees the business had flourished, despite the spasmodic interferences of his eccentric mother, whose peculiarities culminated in a will which relieved him of most of that restraint which is wisely laid upon a boy of sixteen.

The room, with its stained-glass windows and luxurious furnishing, fitted Mr. Telfer perfectly, for he was exquisitely arrayed. He was tall and so painfully thin that the abnormal smallness of his head was not at first apparent. As the girl came into the room he was sniffing delicately at a fine cambric handkerchief, and she thought that he was paler than she had ever seen him—and more repellent.

He followed her movements with a dull stare, and she had placed his letters on his table before he spoke.

===Chinese===
特尔弗联合公司的这位老板极少光顾针线街上的总行。他说那地方的氛围让他压抑——一切都那么粗鄙、污浊、硬邦邦。公司的创始人、他的祖父，在他出生前十年便去世了，把产业留给儿子——一个常年缠绵病榻的人，也在西德尼呱呱坠地几周后跟着去了。产业在受托人手里日渐兴旺，尽管他那位古怪的母亲时不时横加干涉；她的种种怪癖登峰造极之处，是一纸遗嘱解除了本该明智地加在一个十六岁少年身上的大部分管束。

这间屋子，配着彩色玻璃窗和奢华的家具，与特尔弗先生正相称，因为他自己也穿得考究之至。他个子很高，瘦得吓人，以至于他那颗小得出格的脑袋起初并不显眼。姑娘进屋时，他正对着一块细麻纱手帕轻轻嗅着；她觉得他比平日见到的更苍白——也更让她反感。

他目光呆滞地跟着她的动作转，直到她把信件放到他桌上，他才开口。

===Original===
“I say, Miss Belman, you won’t mention a word about what I said to you last night?”

“Mr. Telfer,” she answered quietly, “I am hardly likely to discuss such a matter.”

“I’d marry you and all that, only… clause in my mother’s will,” he said disjointedly. “That could be got over—in time.”

She stood by the table, her hands resting on the edge.

===Chinese===
“我说，贝尔曼小姐，昨晚我跟你说的话，你一个字也不会提吧？”

“特尔弗先生，”她平静地答道，“这种事我怎么可能拿去跟人讲。”

“我本来会娶你，等等之类的，只是……我母亲遗嘱里有条子，”他语无伦次地说，“那个……慢慢总能绕过去的。”

她站在桌边，双手搭在桌沿上。

===Original===
“I would not marry you, Mr. Telfer, even if there were no clause in your mother’s will; the suggestion that I should run away with you to America—”

“South America,” he corrected her gravely. “Not the United States; there was never any suggestion of the United States.”

She could have smiled, for she was not as angry with this rather vacant young man as his startling proposition entitled her to be.

“The point is,” he went on anxiously, “you’ll keep it to yourself? I’ve been worried dreadfully all night. I told you to send me a note saying what you thought of my idea—well, don’t!”

===Chinese===
“就算你母亲的遗嘱里没有那一条，我也不会嫁给你，特尔弗先生；至于要我跟你私奔到美洲——”

“南美洲，”他一本正经地纠正她，“不是合众国；从来没有人提过合众国。”

她差点笑出来——这个心不在焉的年轻人出的题目固然骇人，她对他的气恼却没有达到应有的程度。

“关键是，”他焦灼地往下说，“你会守口如瓶的吧？我担心了整整一夜。我原本要你写张条子，告诉我你对那个主意的看法——算了，别写了！”

===Original===
This time she did smile, but before she could answer him he went on, speaking rapidly in a high treble that sometimes rose to a falsetto squeak:

“You’re a perfectly beautiful girl, and I’m crazy about you, but… there’s a tragedy in my life… really. Perfectly ghastly tragedy. An’ everything’s at sixes an’ sevens. If I’d had any sense I’d have brought in a feller to look after things. I’m beginning to see that now.”

For the second time in twenty-four hours this young man, who had almost been tongue-tied and had never deigned to notice her, had poured forth a torrent of confidences, and in one had, with frantic insistence, set forth a plan which had amazed and shocked her. Abruptly he finished, wiped his weak eyes, and in his normal voice:

“Get Billingham on the phone; I want him.”

===Chinese===
这一回她真笑了，可不等她回答，他又滔滔不绝地说下去，尖细的嗓音说得飞快，时不时拔高成假嗓子的一串尖叫：

“你真是个绝色美人儿，我为你神魂颠倒，可是……我这一生有桩悲剧……真的。惨得不能再惨的悲剧。一切都乱七八糟。我要是有点脑子，早就该找个人来照管事务了。我现在总算明白过来了。”

二十四小时之内，这是第二回了：这个平日近乎木讷、从不屑于理会她的年轻人，忽然倾吐出一大篇体己话，其中有一次还发了疯似的死劝活劝，说出一个令她既惊讶又骇异的计划。他猛地打住，擦了擦那双水汪汪的眼睛，用平常的声调说：

“打电话找比林厄姆，我要见他。”

===Original===
She wondered, as her busy fingers flew over the keys of her typewriter, to what extent his agitation and wild eloquence was due to the rumoured “shakiness” of Telfers Consolidated.

Mr. Billingham came, a sober little man, bald and taciturn, and went in his secretive way into his employer’s room. There was no hint in his appearance or his manner that he contemplated a great crime. He was stout to a point of podginess; apart from his habitual frown, his round face, unlined by the years, was marked by an expression of benevolence.

Yet Mr. Stephen Billingham, managing director of the Telfer Consolidated Trust, went into the office of the London and Central Bank late that afternoon and, presenting a bearer cheque for one hundred and fifty thousand pounds, which was duly honoured, was driven to the Credit Lilloise. He had telephoned particulars of his errand, and there were waiting for him seventeen packets, each containing a million francs, and a smaller packet of a hundred and forty-six mille notes. The franc stood at 74.55 and he received the eighteen packages in exchange for a cheque on the Credit Lilloise for £80,000 and the 150 thousand-pound notes which he had drawn on the London and Central.

===Chinese===
她十指在打字机键盘上飞舞，心里嘀咕：他的失态和那番癫狂的宏论，有多少是拜特尔弗联合公司传闻中的“根基不稳”所赐。

比林厄姆先生来了——一个矮小稳重、秃顶寡言的人，蹑手蹑脚地走进雇主的房间。无论外貌还是举止，都看不出他正酝酿着一桩大罪。他胖得圆滚滚的；除了惯常皱着的眉头，那张没被岁月刻下皱纹的圆脸，一派慈祥之色。

然而就在那天傍晚，特尔弗联合信托公司的总经理斯蒂芬·比林厄姆（Stephen Billingham）先生走进伦敦中央银行，亮出一张十五万英镑的无记名支票——银行如数兑付——随后驱车前往里尔信贷银行（Credit Lilloise）。他已电话告知了此行的细节，那里等着他的是十七包现钞，每包一百万法郎，另有一小包十四万六千法郎的纸币。当时法郎汇价是 74.55，他交出一张里尔信贷银行 80,000 英镑的支票，外加从伦敦中央银行取出的那 150 张千镑大钞，换回了这十八包钱。

===Original===
Of Billingham’s movements thenceforth little was known. He was seen by an acquaintance driving through Cheapside in a taxicab which was traced as far as Charing Cross—and there he disappeared. Neither the airways nor the waterways had known him, the police theory being that he had left by an evening train that had carried an excursion party via Havre to Paris.

“This is the biggest steal we have had in years,” said the Assistant Director of Public Prosecutions. “If you can slip in sideways on the inquiry, Mr. Reeder, I should be glad. Don’t step on the toes of the City police—they are quite amiable people where murder is concerned, but a little touchy where money is in question. Go along and see Sidney Telfer.”

Fortunately, the prostrated Sidney was discoverable outside the City area. Mr. Reeder went into the outer office and saw a familiar face.

===Chinese===
此后比林厄姆的行踪便鲜有人知。有个熟人瞥见他的出租车驶过奇普赛德，车子一路追到查令十字——从此他就没了踪影。空中航线和水路都没有他的记录，警方的推测是：他搭上了一班晚间列车，混在一批观光客里，取道勒阿弗尔去了巴黎。

“这是多年来最大的一桩盗窃案，”副检察总长说，“里德先生，你若能侧身插进这个调查，我很乐意。别踩了伦敦城警察的脚——说到凶杀案，他们是相当和气的人，可一涉及钱就有点敏感。去见见西德尼·特尔弗吧。”

所幸瘫软无力的西德尼在伦敦城区之外还能找到。里德先生走进外间办公室，看见一张熟悉的面孔。

===Original===
“Pardon me, I think I know you, young lady,” he said, and she smiled as she opened the little wooden gate to admit him.

“You are Mr. Reeder—we live in the same road,” she said, and then quickly: “Have you come about Mr. Billingham?”

“Yes.” His voice was hushed, as though he were speaking of a dead friend. “I wanted to see Mr. Telfer, but perhaps you could give me a little information.”

The only news she had was that Sidney Telfer had been in the office since seven o’clock and was at the moment in such a state of collapse that she had sent for the doctor.

===Chinese===
“恕我冒昧，我想我认得您，小姐，”他说；她微笑着拉开那扇小木门，请他进去。

“您是里德先生——我们住在同一条街上，”她说，随即急急地问：“您是为比林厄姆先生的事来的？”

“是的。”他的声音压得很低，仿佛在谈及一位亡故的朋友。“我本想见特尔弗先生，不过也许您能给我提供一点情况。”

她所知的情况只有一样：西德尼·特尔弗从七点钟起就待在办公室里，此刻整个人垮得不成样子，她已经去请医生了。

===Original===
“I doubt if he is in a condition to see you,” she said.

“I will take all responsibility,” said Mr. Reeder soothingly. “Is Mr. Telfer—er—a friend of yours. Miss—?”

“Belman is my name.” He had seen the quick flush that came to her cheek: it could mean one of two things. “No, I am an employee, that is all.”

Her tone told him all he wanted to know. Mr. J. G. Reeder was something of an authority on office friendships.

===Chinese===
“我看他这副样子未必能见您。”她说。

“一切责任由我来担，”里德先生宽慰道，“特尔弗先生——呃——是您的一位朋友吗，小姐——？”

“我姓贝尔曼。”他看到她颊上飞快掠过一抹红晕：这红晕只可能意味着两件事中的一件。“不是，我是雇员，仅此而已。”

她的口气把想知道的都告诉了他。J. G. 里德先生算得上办公室情谊方面的权威。

===Original===
“Bothered you a little, has he?” he murmured, and she shot a suspicious look at him. What did he know, and what bearing had Mr. Telfer’s mad proposal on the present disaster? She was entirely in the dark as to the true state of affairs; it was, she felt, a moment for frankness.

“Wanted you to run away! Dear me!” Mr. Reeder was shocked. “He is married?”

“Oh, no—he’s not married,” said the girl shortly. “Poor man, I’m sorry for him now. I’m afraid that the loss is a very heavy one—who would suspect Mr. Billingham?”

“Ah! who indeed!” sighed the lugubrious Reeder, and took off his glasses to wipe them; almost she suspected tears. “I think I will go in now—that is the door?”

===Chinese===
“他纠缠过您一点，是吧？”他喃喃道；她狐疑地瞟了他一眼。他知道些什么？特尔弗先生那疯狂的提议，与眼前这场灾祸又有什么相干？她对事情真相一无所知；她觉得，此刻唯有坦率。

“竟要您跟他私奔！天哪！”里德先生大为震惊，“他成家了？”

“哦，没有——他没成家，”姑娘简短地说，“可怜的人，如今我倒替他难过。恐怕这次损失不小——谁会疑心到比林厄姆先生头上呢？”

“啊！可不是嘛！”忧郁的里德先生叹息着摘下眼镜擦拭；她几乎疑心他要掉泪。“我想我现在就进去——是这扇门吗？”

===Original===
Sidney jerked up his face and glared at the intruder. He had been sitting with his head on his arms for the greater part of an hour.

“I say… what do you want?” he asked feebly. “I say… I can’t see anybody… Public Prosecutor’s Department?” He almost screamed the words. “What’s the use of prosecuting him if you don’t get the money back?”

Mr. Reeder let him work down before he began to ply his very judicious questions.

“I don’t know much about it,” said the despondent young man. “I’m only a sort of figurehead. Billingham brought the cheques for me to sign and I signed ’em. I never gave him instructions; he got his orders. I don’t know very much about it. He told me, actually told me, that the business was in a bad way—half a million or something was wanted by next week… Oh, my God! And then he took the whole of our cash.”

===Chinese===
西德尼猛地抬起脸，怒视着这位不速之客。大半个钟头里，他一直把头埋在臂弯里坐着。

“我说……你想干什么？”他有气无力地问，“我说……我谁也不能见……检察总长公署？”他这几个字几乎是尖叫出来的。“要是钱追不回来，起诉他又有什么用？”

里德先生由着他慢慢平静下来，才开始提出他那些极有分寸的问题。

“这里头的情形我知道得不多，”沮丧的年轻人说，“我只是个挂名的傀儡。比林厄姆把支票拿来给我签，我就签了。我从不给他下指示；他的命令另有来处。这里头的事我真知道得不多。他跟我说过，真的跟我说过，生意撑不住了——下星期以前得凑出五十万还是多少……哦，老天！结果他把我们的现款席卷一空。”

===Original===
Sidney Telfer sobbed his woe into his sleeve like a child. Mr. Reeder waited before he asked a question in his gentlest manner.

“No, I wasn’t here: I went down to Brighton for the weekend. And the police dug me out of bed at four in the morning. We’re bankrupt. I’ll have to sell my car and resign from my club—one has to resign when one is bankrupt.”

There was little more to learn from the broken man, and Mr. Reeder returned to his chief with a report that added nothing to the sum of knowledge. In a week the theft of Mr. Billingham passed from scare lines to paragraphs in most of the papers—Billingham had made a perfect getaway.

In the bright lexicon of Mr. J. G. Reeder there was no such word as holiday. Even the Public Prosecutor’s office has its slack time, when juniors and sub-officials and even the Director himself can go away on vacation, leaving the office open and a subordinate in charge. But to Mr. J. G. Reeder the very idea of wasting time was repugnant, and it was his practice to brighten the dull patches of occupation by finding a seat in a magistrate’s court and listening, absorbed, to cases which bored even the court reporter.

===Chinese===
西德尼·特尔弗像个孩子似的把悲声哭进袖子里。里德先生等了一等，才以最温和的态度提出一个问题。

“不，我不在这儿：我到布莱顿过周末去了。凌晨四点，警察把我从床上刨了出来。我们破产了。我得卖掉汽车，退掉俱乐部——人一破产，就得退俱乐部。”

从这个垮掉的人身上再问不出什么，里德先生回到上司那里，交上了一份于案情毫无增益的报告。一个星期后，比林厄姆先生这桩窃案便从各报的骇人标题缩成了简讯——比林厄姆跑得干干净净。

在J. G. 里德先生那部光明的辞典里，找不出“休假”这个词。连检察总长公署也有淡季，那时小字辈的属员、甚至总长本人都可以外出度假，办公室照常开着门，交给一名下属坐镇。可对J. G. 里德先生来说，浪费光阴这个念头本身就令他憎恶；他的消遣之道，是在治安法庭里找个位子坐下来，如痴如醉地听那些连法庭记者都听腻的案子。

===Original===
John Smith, charged with being drunk and using insulting language to Police Officer Thomas Brown; Mary Jane Haggitt, charged with obstructing the police in the execution of their duty; Henry Robinson, arraigned for being a suspected person, having in his possession housebreaking tools, to wit, one cold chisel and a screwdriver; Arthur Moses, charged with driving a motorcar to the common danger—all these were fascinating figures of romance and legend to the lean man who sat between the press and railed dock, his square-crowned hat by his side, his umbrella gripped between his knees, and on his melancholy face an expression of startled wonder.

On one raw and foggy morning, Mr. Reeder, self-released from his duties, chose the Marylebone Police Court for his recreation. Two drunks, a shop theft and an embezzlement had claimed his rapt attention, when Mrs. Jackson was escorted to the dock and a rubicund policeman stepped to the witness stand, and, swearing by his Deity that he would tell the truth and nothing but the truth, related his peculiar story.

“P.C. Ferryman No. 9717 L. Division,” he introduced himself conventionally. “I was on duty in the Edgware Road early this morning at 2:30 a.m. when I saw the prisoner carrying a large suitcase. On seeing me she turned round and walked rapidly in the opposite direction. Her movements being suspicious, I followed and, overtaking her, asked her whose property she was carrying. She told me it was her own and that she was going to catch a train. She said that the case contained her clothes. As the case was a valuable one of crocodile leather I asked her to show me the inside. She refused. She also refused to give me her name and address and I asked her to accompany me to the station.”

===Chinese===
约翰·史密斯，被控酗酒并出言侮辱警察托马斯·布朗；玛丽·简·哈吉特，被控妨碍警察执行公务；亨利·罗宾逊，因系可疑人物被提起诉讼，身怀入室行窃的工具，即一把冷凿和一支螺丝刀；亚瑟·摩西，被控驾车危及公共安全——对坐在记者席与围栏被告席之间那个瘦削的男人来说，这些人个个都是传奇与野史里引人入胜的角色。他的方顶帽搁在手边，雨伞夹在两膝之间，忧郁的脸上挂着一副惊异出神的神情。

一个阴冷多雾的早晨，自行免了职务的里德先生挑中马里波恩治安法庭作他的消遣。两桩酗酒、一桩店铺偷窃和一桩挪用公款案已让他听得入神，这时杰克逊太太被押到被告席，一名红光满面的警察走上证人席，凭他的神明起誓只说真话、除真话外别无他言，然后讲出了他那桩离奇的故事。

“警察费里曼，编号 9717，L 分区，”他照惯例自报家门，“今日凌晨 2:30，我正在埃奇韦尔路值勤，看见被告提着一只大手提箱。她一见我便转过身，飞快朝相反方向走去。其行迹可疑，我尾随而上，赶上她后问她提的是谁家财物。她说是她自己的，还说她要去赶火车，箱子里装的是她的衣服。那箱子是名贵的鳄鱼皮，我便请她让我看看里面。她拒绝了。她又不肯说出姓名住址，我便请她随我到警察局走一趟。”

===Original===
There followed a detective sergeant.

“I saw the prisoner at the station and in her presence opened the case. It contained a considerable quantity of small stone chips—”

“Stone chips?” interrupted the incredulous magistrate. “You mean small pieces of stone—what kind of stone?”

“Marble, your worship. She said that she wanted to make a little path in her garden and that she had taken them from the yard of a monumental mason in the Euston Road. She made a frank statement to the effect that she had broken open a gate into the yard and filled the suitcase without the mason’s knowledge.”

===Chinese===
接着上来一名便衣警士。

“我在警察局见到被告，当着她的面开箱检查。箱内装有数量可观的小碎石片——”

“碎石片？”治安官将信将疑地打断他，“你是说小石块——什么石？”

“大理石，阁下。她说想在自家花园里铺条小路，石头是从尤斯顿路一位墓碑石匠的场院里拿的。她供认不讳：撬开了通往场院的门，趁石匠不知情装满了手提箱。”

===Original===
The magistrate leant back in his chair and scrutinised the charge sheet with a frown.

“There is no address against her name,” he said.

“She gave an address, but it was false, your worship—she refuses to offer any further information.”

Mr. J. G. Reeder had screwed round in his seat and was staring open-mouthed at the prisoner. She was tall, broad-shouldered and stoutly built. The hand that rested on the rail of the dock was twice the size of any woman’s hand he had ever seen. The face was modelled largely, but though there was something in her appearance which was almost repellent, she was handsome in her large way. Deep-set brown eyes, a nose that was large and masterful, a well-shaped mouth and two chins—these in profile were not attractive to one who had his views on beauty in women, but Mr. J. G. Reeder, being a fair man, admitted that she was a fine-looking woman. When she spoke it was in a voice as deep as a man’s, sonorous and powerful.

===Chinese===
治安官往椅背上一靠，皱着眉头细看控状。

“她名下没有住址。”他说。

“她报过一个住址，但是假的，阁下——她拒绝再提供任何情况。”

J. G. 里德先生已在座位上拧转身子，张着嘴直勾勾盯着被告。她身材高大，肩膀宽阔，骨架粗壮。搭在被告席栏杆上的那只手，比他见过的任何女人的手都大一倍。那张脸轮廓粗放，虽然相貌里透着一种近乎惹人厌的东西，她那种大骨架的俊朗却自有气派。深陷的棕色眼睛，一只大而专断的鼻子，一张形状姣好的嘴和两层下巴——这副侧影在一位对女性之美自有主张的人看来并不动人，但J. G. 里德先生处事公道，承认她是位仪表堂堂的女人。她一开口，声音低沉如男子，洪亮而有力。

===Original===
“I admit it was a fool thing to do. But the idea occurred to me just as I was going to bed and I acted on the impulse of the moment. I could well afford to buy the stone—I had over fifty pounds in my pocketbook when I was arrested.”

“Is that true?” and, when the officer answered, the magistrate turned his suspicious eyes to the woman. “You are giving us a lot of trouble because you will not tell your name and address. I can understand that you do not wish your friends to know of your stupid theft, but unless you give me the information, I shall be compelled to remand you in custody for a week.”

She was well, if plainly, dressed. On one large finger flashed a diamond which Mr. Reeder mentally priced in the region of two hundred pounds. “Mrs. Jackson” was shaking her head as he looked.

===Chinese===
“我承认这是件蠢事。可我正要上床时忽然起了这个念头，一时冲动就干了。那点石头我完全买得起——被捕时我皮夹里还有五十多镑呢。”

“属实吗？”等警察答完，治安官把狐疑的目光转向那女人。“就因为你死活不肯说出姓名住址，给我们添了这么多麻烦。我不难理解你不愿意让朋友知道你这桩愚蠢的偷窃，可你若不交代清楚，我只好将你还押候审一星期。”

她衣着虽素净，却很讲究。一根粗大的手指上钻戒闪闪，里德先生在心里估了个两百镑上下的价。他望着的时候，“杰克逊太太”正摇着头。

===Original===
“I can’t give you my address,” she said, and the magistrate nodded curtly.

“Remanded for inquiry,” he said, and added, as she walked out of the dock: “I should like a report from the prison doctor on the state of her mind.”

Mr. J. G. Reeder rose quickly from his chair and followed the woman and the officer in charge of the case through the little door that leads to the cells.

“Mrs. Jackson” had disappeared by the time he reached the corridor, but the detective-sergeant was stooping over the large and handsome suitcase that he had shown in court and was now laying on a form.

===Chinese===
“我的住址不能说。”她说；治安官生硬地点了点头。

“还押待查。”他说，又在她走出被告席时补了一句：“我要监狱医生出具一份关于她精神状态的报告。”

J. G. 里德先生霍地从椅子上起身，跟着那女人和办案警察穿过通往拘留室的小门。

他走到过道时，“杰克逊太太”已经不见了，倒是那位便衣警士正俯身摆弄那只他在法庭上出示过的大号漂亮手提箱，此刻正把它放到长凳上。

===Original===
Most of the outdoor men of the C.I.D. knew Mr. J. G. Reeder, and Sergeant Mills grinned a cheerful welcome.

“What do you think of that one, Mr. Reeder? It is certainly a new line on me! Never heard of a tombstone artist being burgled before.”

He opened the top of the case, and Mr. Reeder ran his fingers through the marble chips.

“The case and the loot weighs over a hundred pounds,” said the officer. “She must have the strength of a navvy to carry it. The poor officer who carried it to the station was hot and melting when he arrived.”

===Chinese===
刑事部（C.I.D.）外勤的人大多认得J. G. 里德先生，米尔斯警士咧嘴笑着跟他打招呼。

“这案子您怎么看，里德先生？我可是头一回开这个眼！从没听说过有墓碑匠被人偷的。”

他掀开箱盖，里德先生把手指插进大理石碎块里拨弄。

“箱子连赃物足有一百多磅重，”警官说，“扛得动它，她力气准得赛过壮工。可怜那位把它拎到局里的警察，到的时候已经热得快化掉了。”

===Original===
Mr. J. G. was inspecting the case. It was a handsome article, the hinges and locks being of oxidised silver. No maker’s name was visible on the inside, or owner’s initials on its glossy lid. The lining had once been of silk, but now hung in shreds and was white with marble dust.

“Yes,” said Mr. Reeder absently, “very interesting—most interesting. Is it permissible to ask whether, when she was searched, any—er—document?” The sergeant shook his head. “Or unusual possession?”

“Only these.”

By the side of the case was a pair of large gloves. These also were soiled, and their surfaces cut in a hundred places.

===Chinese===
里德先生察看着箱子。这是件讲究的物件，铰链和锁扣都是做旧的银器。里面看不到制造商的名号，光亮的箱盖上也没有物主的姓名首字母。衬里原先想必是丝绸，如今已成条条缕缕，蒙着大理石灰，白花花的。

“唔，”里德先生心不在焉地说，“很有意思——非常有意思。可否冒昧一问，搜身的时候，有没有——呃——什么文件？”警士摇了摇头。“或者不寻常的物件？”

“只有这些。”

箱子旁边放着一副大号手套。手套也脏了，表面上百处割痕。

===Original===
“These have been used frequently for the same purpose,” murmured Mr. J. G. “She evidently makes—er—a collection of marble shavings. Nothing in her pocketbook?”

“Only the banknotes: they have the stamp of the Central Bank on their backs. We should be able to trace ’em easily.”

Mr. Reeder returned to his office and, locking the door, produced a worn pack of cards from a drawer and played patience—which was his method of thinking intensively. Late in the afternoon his telephone bell rang, and he recognised the voice of Sergeant Mills.

“Can I come along and see you? Yes, it is about the banknotes.”

Ten minutes later the sergeant presented himself.

“The notes were issued three months ago to Mr. Telfer,” said the officer without preliminary, “and they were given by him to his housekeeper, Mrs. Welford.”

===Chinese===
“这副手套干这营生显然不是一回两回了，”J. G. 先生喃喃道，“她显然在——呃——收藏大理石刨花。她皮夹里没有别的东西？”

“只有那些钞票：背面都盖着中央银行的戳。要追查它们的来路不难。”

里德先生回到办公室，锁上门，从抽屉里摸出一副磨旧了的纸牌，玩起单人牌戏——那是他高强度思考的方式。傍晚时分，电话铃响了，他听出是米尔斯警士的声音。

“我能过去见您一面吗？对，是为了钞票的事。”

十分钟后，警士到了。

“那些钞票是三个月前发给特尔弗先生的，”警官开门见山地说，“是他交给他的管家韦尔福德太太的。”

===Original===
“Oh, indeed?” said Mr. Reeder softly, and added, after reflection: “Dear me!”

He pulled hard at his lip.

“And is ‘Mrs. Jackson’ that lady?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“哦，是吗？”里德先生轻声说，沉吟片刻又补上一句：“天哪！”

他使劲抻着自己的嘴唇。

“那么‘杰克逊太太’就是那位太太？”他问。

===Original===
“Yes. Telfer—poor little devil—nearly went mad when I told him she was under remand—dashed up to Holloway in a taxi to identify her. The magistrate has granted bail, and she’ll be bound over tomorrow. Telfer was bleating like a child—said she was mad. Gosh! that fellow is scared of her—when I took him into the waiting-room at Holloway Prison she gave him one look and he wilted. By the way, we have had a hint about Billingham that may interest you. Do you know that he and Telfer’s secretary were very good friends?”

“Really?” Mr. Reeder was indeed interested. “Very good friends? Well, well!”

“The Yard has put Miss Belman under general observation: there may be nothing to it, but in cases like Billingham’s it is very often a matter of cherchez la femme.”

===Chinese===
“正是。特尔弗——可怜的小鬼——听说她被还押，差点疯了——打的士一路冲到霍洛威去认人。治安官已经准了保释，明天具结开释完事。特尔弗哭哭啼啼像个孩子——说她疯了。天哪！那家伙怕她怕得要死——我领他进霍洛威监狱的等候室，她朝他扫了一眼，他立马就蔫了。对了，我们还听到一桩关于比林厄姆的传闻，您或许有兴趣。您可知道，他跟特尔弗的秘书交情很深？”

“当真？”里德先生确实来了兴致，“交情很深？啧啧！”

“苏格兰场已对贝尔曼小姐作了一般性监视：也许什么都没有，不过比林厄姆这类案子，十有八九得‘cherchez la femme’——找出那个女人。”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder had given his lip a rest and was now gently massaging his nose.

“Dear me!” he said. “That is a French expression, is it not?”

He was not in court when the marble stealer was sternly admonished by the magistrate and discharged. All that interested Mr. J. G. Reeder was to learn that the woman had paid the mason and had carried away her marble chips in triumph to the pretty little detached residence in the Outer Circle of Regent’s Park. He had spent the morning at Somerset House, examining copies of wills and the like; his afternoon he gave up to the tracing of Mrs. Rebecca Alamby Mary Welford.

===Chinese===
里德先生的嘴唇歇了工，这会儿正轻轻按摩着自己的鼻子。

“天哪！”他说，“那是句法国话，对吧？”

大理石窃贼被治安官严厉训诫、当庭开释时，他并没有到庭。J. G. 里德先生关心的只有一件事：那女人赔了石匠的钱，然后得意洋洋地把她的碎大理石运到摄政公园外环路一栋漂亮的小独立宅子里去了。他一上午都泡在萨默塞特府翻查遗嘱副本之类；一下午则用来追查丽贝卡·阿拉姆比·玛丽·韦尔福德太太的底细。

===Original===
She was the relict of Professor John Welford of the University of Edinburgh, and had been left a widow after two years of marriage. She had then entered the service of Mrs. Telfer, the mother of Sidney, and had sole charge of the boy from his fourth year. When Mrs. Telfer died she had made the woman sole guardian of her youthful charge. So that Rebecca Welford had been by turns nurse and guardian, and was now in control of the young man’s establishment.

The house occupied Mr. Reeder’s attention to a considerable degree. It was a redbrick modern dwelling consisting of two floors and having a frontage on the Circle and a side road. Behind and beside the house was a large garden which, at this season of the year, was bare of flowers. They were probably in snug quarters for the winter, for there was a long greenhouse behind the garden.

He was leaning over the wooden palings, eyeing the grounds through the screen of box hedge that overlapped the fence with a melancholy stare, when he saw a door open and the big woman come out. She was bare-armed and wore an apron. In one hand she carried a dust box, which she emptied into a concealed ash-bin, in the other was a long broom.

===Chinese===
她是爱丁堡大学约翰·韦尔福德教授的遗孀，婚后两年便守了寡。此后她到西德尼的母亲特尔弗太太府上帮佣，从男孩四岁起便独自照管他。特尔弗太太临终时，立这个女人为她年幼受监护人的唯一监护人。就这样，丽贝卡·韦尔福德先后做过保姆、监护人，如今则执掌着这个年轻人整个家宅的大权。

那栋房子在很大程度上攫住了里德先生的注意。那是一栋红砖新房，上下两层，正面朝环路，侧面临一条小街。屋后和屋旁是一座大花园，一年中的这个时节不见花影。花多半已进了舒适的暖房过冬，因为花园后面有一长列温室。

他正倚着木栅栏，隔着爬过篱笆的黄杨树篱，用忧郁的目光打量院子，恰见一扇门开了，那高大的女人走了出来。她光着胳膊，系着围裙。一只手提着垃圾箱，倒进一个隐蔽的垃圾桶里，另一只手握着一把长柄扫帚。

===Original===
Mr. Reeder moved swiftly out of sight. Presently the door slammed and he peeped again. There was no evidence of a marble path. All the walks were of rolled gravel.

He went to a neighbouring telephone booth, and called his office.

“I may be away all day,” he said.

There was no sign of Mr. Sidney Telfer, though the detective knew that he was in the house.

===Chinese===
里德先生敏捷地躲出视线。不多时门砰地关上，他又探头去看。园中不见大理石小径的踪影，所有步道铺的都是碾实的砾石。

他走到附近的电话亭，给办公室去了个电话。

“我可能一整天都不回去。”他说。

西德尼·特尔弗先生不见踪影，尽管侦探知道他就在屋里。

===Original===
Telfer’s Trust was in the hands of the liquidators, and the first meeting of creditors had been called. Sidney had, by all accounts, been confined to his bed, and from that safe refuge had written a note to his secretary asking that “all papers relating to my private affairs” should be burnt. He had scrawled a postscript: “Can I possibly see you on business before I go?” The word “go” had been scratched out and “retire” substituted. Mr. Reeder had seen that letter—indeed, all correspondence between Sidney and the office came to him by arrangement with the liquidators. And that was partly why Mr. J. G. Reeder was so interested in 904, The Circle.

It was dusk when a big car drew up at the gate of the house. Before the driver could descend from his seat, the door of 904 opened, and Sidney Telfer almost ran out. He carried a suitcase in each hand, and Mr. Reeder recognised that nearest him as the grip in which the housekeeper had carried the stolen marble.

Reaching over, the chauffeur opened the door of the machine and, flinging in the bags, Sidney followed hastily. The door closed, and the car went out of sight round the curve of the Circle.

===Chinese===
特尔弗信托已交到清算人手里，第一次债权人会议也已召集。据说西德尼一直卧床不起，从那处安全的避难所里写了张便条给他的秘书，要求把“所有涉及我私人事务的文件”付之一炬。他还潦草地添了句附言：“我走之前能不能见你一面，谈点正事？”“走”字被划掉，改成了“引退”。里德先生见过那封信——事实上，经与清算人协商，西德尼与公司之间的全部函件都送到他手上。这也是J. G. 里德先生对环路 904 号如此上心的部分原因。

暮色四合时，一辆大轿车驶到宅门前。司机还没来得及下车，904 号的门就开了，西德尼·特尔弗几乎是跑着出来的。他两手各提一只箱子，里德先生认出离自己近的那只，正是管家装偷来的大理石用的那只旅行袋。

司机探过身来推开的车门，行李扔进去，西德尼急忙跟着钻进车里。车门一关，汽车绕过环路的弯道，没了踪影。

===Original===
Mr. Reeder crossed the road and took up a position very near the front gate, waiting.

Dusk came and the veil of a Regent’s Park fog. The house was in darkness, no flash of light except a faint glimmer that burnt in the hall, no sound. The woman was still there—Mrs. Sidney Telfer, nurse, companion, guardian and wife. Mrs. Sidney Telfer, the hidden director of Telfers Consolidated, a masterful woman who, not content with marrying a weakling twenty years her junior, had applied her masterful but ill-equipped mind to the domination of a business she did not understand, and which she was destined to plunge into ruin. Mr. Reeder had made good use of his time at the Records Office: a copy of the marriage certificate was almost as easy to secure as a copy of the will.

He glanced round anxiously. The fog was clearing, which was exactly what he did not wish it to do, for he had certain acts to perform which required as thick a cloaking as possible.

===Chinese===
里德先生穿过马路，在离前门极近的地方站定，等着。

暮色渐浓，又罩上一层摄政公园的雾。房子黑沉沉的，除了门厅里燃着的一点微光，不见一丝灯亮，也不闻声响。那女人还在里面——西德尼·特尔弗太太，保姆、伴侣、监护人兼妻子。西德尼·特尔弗太太，特尔弗联合公司幕后的掌舵人，一个强悍的女人：嫁给一个比她小二十岁的软骨头尚不满足，又把她那强悍却装备贫乏的头脑，用去支配一桩她一窍不通、且注定要被她拖入毁灭的生意。里德先生在档案署的时间没有白费：弄一份结婚证副本，几乎跟弄一份遗嘱副本一样容易。

他焦灼地四下张望。雾正在消散——这恰恰不是他希望的，因为他有几件事要办，需要越浓的遮蔽越好。

===Original===
And then a surprising thing happened. A cab came slowly along the road and stopped at the gate.

“I think this is the place, miss,” said the cabman, and a girl stepped down to the pavement.

It was Miss Margaret Belman.

===Chinese===
就在这时，一件出人意料的事发生了。一辆马车沿街缓缓而来，停在大门口。

“我想就是这儿了，小姐，”车夫说；一个姑娘踏上人行道。

是玛格丽特·贝尔曼小姐。

===Original===
Reeder waited until she had paid the fare and the cab had gone, and then, as she walked towards the gate, he stepped from the shadow.

“Oh!—Mr. Reeder, how you frightened me!” she gasped. “I am going to see Mr. Telfer—he is dangerously ill—no, it was his housekeeper who wrote asking me to come at seven.”

“Did she now! Well, I will ring the bell for you.”

===Chinese===
里德等她付了车钱、马车走远，才在她走向大门时从阴影里踱出来。

“哎呀！——里德先生，您吓了我一跳！”她倒吸一口气，“我来看特尔弗先生——他病得很重——不，是他管家写信让我七点来的。”

“是嘛！来，我替您按门铃。”

===Original===
She told him that that was unnecessary—she had the key which had come with the note.

“She is alone in the house with Mr. Telfer, who refuses to allow a trained nurse near him,” said Margaret, “and—”

“Will you be good enough to lower your voice, young lady?” urged Mr. Reeder in an impressive whisper. “Forgive the impertinence, but if our friend is ill—”

===Chinese===
她告诉他不必了——钥匙是随便条一道送来的。

“偌大的宅子里只有她和特尔弗先生两个人，他又不许训练有素的护士靠近，”玛格丽特说，“而且——”

“劳驾您把声音放低些，小姐，好吗？”里德先生恳切地低声劝道，“恕我冒昧，倘若咱们这位朋友病着——”

===Original===
She was at first startled by his urgency.

“He couldn’t hear me,” she said, but spoke in a lower tone.

“He may—sick people are very sensitive to the human voice. Tell me, how did this letter come?”

===Chinese===
他这副急切样子先把她吓了一跳。

“他听不见的。”她说，声音却压低了。

“未必——病人对人声是很敏感的。告诉我，这封信是怎么送来的？”

===Original===
“From Mr. Telfer? By district messenger an hour ago.”

Nobody had been to the house or left it—except Sidney. And Sidney, in his blind fear, would carry out any instructions which his wife gave to him.

“And did it contain a passage like this?” Mr. Reeder considered a moment. “‘Bring this letter with you’?”

===Chinese===
“特尔弗先生的信？一个钟头前由区信差送来的。”

没有人来过这宅子，也没有人离开——除了西德尼。而西德尼，在他那盲目的恐惧里，妻子吩咐什么他就会照办什么。

“信里有没有这样一句话？”里德先生想了想，“‘把这封信随身带来’？”

===Original===
“No,” said the girl in surprise, “but Mrs. Welford telephoned just before the letter arrived and told me to wait for it. And she asked me to bring the letter with me because she didn’t wish Mr. Telfer’s private correspondence to be left lying around. But why do you ask me this, Mr. Reeder—is anything wrong?”

He did not answer immediately. Pushing open the gate, he walked noiselessly along the grass plot that ran parallel with the path.

“Open the door, I will come in with you,” he whispered and, when she hesitated: “Do as I tell you, please.”

===Chinese===
“没有，”姑娘惊讶地说，“不过信到之前韦尔福德太太刚来过电话，叫我等这封信。她还要我随身把信带来，说她不愿意特尔弗先生的私人信件四处乱放。可您问这些做什么，里德先生——出什么事了？”

他没有立刻回答。推开院门，他踩着与步道平行的那片草地，悄无声息地走过去。

“开门，我跟你一起进去，”他悄声道；见她犹豫，又说：“请照我说的做。”

===Original===
The hand that put the key into the lock trembled, but at last the key turned and the door swung open. A small night-light burnt on the table of the wide panelled hall. On the left, near the foot of the stairs, only the lower steps of which were visible, Reeder saw a narrow door which stood open, and, taking a step forward, saw that it was a tiny telephone-room.

And then a voice spoke from the upper landing, a deep, booming voice that he knew.

“Is that Miss Belman?”

===Chinese===
把钥匙插进锁孔的那只手抖着，但钥匙终于转动，门豁然开了。宽敞的镶板门厅里，桌上点着一盏小小的夜灯。左边近楼梯脚处——楼梯只看得见下面几级——里德看见一扇敞开的窄门，再上前一步，看清那是间小小的电话间。

接着，楼上的楼梯口传来一个说话声，一个他熟悉的、低沉如钟的声音。

“是贝尔曼小姐吗？”

===Original===
Margaret, her heart beating faster, went to the foot of the stairs and looked up.

“Yes, Mrs. Welford.”

“You brought the letter with you?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
玛格丽特心跳得更快了，她走到楼梯脚下，抬头望去。

“是我，韦尔福德太太。”

“信随身带来了？”

“带来了。”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder crept along the wall until he could have touched the girl.

“Good,” said the deep voice. “Will you call the doctor—Circle 743—and tell him that Mr. Telfer has had a relapse—you will find the booth in the hall: shut the door behind you, the bell worries him.”

Margaret looked at the detective and he nodded.

===Chinese===
里德先生贴着墙根挪过去，挪到一伸手就能碰到姑娘的地方。

“好，”那低沉的声音说，“麻烦你打给医生——环路 743——告诉他特尔弗先生病又重了——电话间就在门厅里：进去后把门关上，铃声吵得他难受。”

玛格丽特望向侦探，他点了点头。

===Original===
The woman upstairs wished to gain time for something—what?

The girl passed him: he heard the thud of the padded door close, and there was a click that made him spin round. The first thing he noticed was that there was no handle to the door, the second that the keyhole was covered by a steel disc, which he discovered later was felt-lined. He heard the girl speaking faintly, and put his ear to the keyhole.

“The instrument is disconnected—I can’t open the door.”

===Chinese===
楼上那女人要为某件事争取时间——什么事？

姑娘从他身边走过去：他听见软垫门砰的一声合上，接着咔哒一响，惊得他霍然回身。他注意到的头一件事是门上没有把手，第二件是锁眼上盖着一块钢片——他后来才发现钢片衬着毛毡。他听见姑娘在里面低声说话，便把耳朵贴上锁眼。

“电话线断了——门我打不开。”

===Original===
Without a second’s hesitation, he flew up the stairs, umbrella in hand, and as he reached the landing he heard a door close with a crash. Instantly he located the sound. It came from a room on the left immediately over the hall. The door was locked.

“Open this door,” he commanded, and there came to him the sound of a deep laugh.

Mr. Reeder tugged at the stout handle of his umbrella. There was a flicker of steel as he dropped the lower end, and in his hand appeared six inches of knife blade.

===Chinese===
他片刻也不迟疑，提着雨伞飞奔上楼，刚到楼梯口，就听见一扇门砰然关上。他立时辨明了声音的方位：来自左边一间正对门厅上方的屋子。门锁着。

“开门。”他喝道；回应他的是一声低沉的大笑。

里德先生拽了拽雨伞那粗壮的伞柄。伞尾一沉，钢光一闪，他手里赫然多出六英寸长的刀刃。

===Original===
The first stab at the panel sliced through the thin wood as though it were paper. In a second there was a jagged gap through which the black muzzle of an automatic was thrust.

“Put down that jug or I will blow your features into comparative chaos!” said Mr. Reeder pedantically.

The room was brightly lit, and he could see plainly. Mrs. Welford stood by the side of a big square funnel, the narrow end of which ran into the floor. In her hand was a huge enamelled iron jug, and ranged about her were six others. In one corner of the room was a wide circular tank, and beyond, at half its height, depended a large copper pipe.

===Chinese===
刀尖刺进门板，薄木片像纸一样豁开。转眼间门上现出一道锯齿状的口子，一支自动手枪乌黑的枪口从缺口里探了进去。

“放下那只罐子，否则我就把您的五官轰个七零八落！”里德先生咬文嚼字地说。

屋里灯火通明，他看得分明。韦尔福德太太站在一个方口大漏斗旁，漏斗的窄口直通地板。她手里提着一只大号搪瓷铁罐，周围还依次摆着六只。房间一角立着一只宽大的圆槽，槽后不及其高的一半处，垂下一根粗大的铜管。

===Original===
The woman’s face turned to him was blank, expressionless.

“He wanted to run away with her,” she said simply, “and after all I have done for him!”

“Open the door.”

===Chinese===
那女人转向他的脸一片空白，毫无表情。

“他要跟她私奔，”她平平地说，“我为他操了那么多心！”

“开门。”

===Original===
Mrs. Welford set down the jug and ran her huge hand across her forehead.

“Sidney is my own darling,” she said. “I’ve nursed him, and taught him, and there was a million—all in gold—in the ship. But they robbed him.”

She was talking of one of the ill-fated enterprises of Telfers Consolidated Trust—that sunken treasure ship to recover which the money of the company had been poured out like water. And she was mad. He had guessed the weakness of this domineering woman from the first.

===Chinese===
韦尔福德太太放下罐子，一只大手抹过前额。

“西德尼是我亲爱的心肝，”她说，“我奶大了他，教出了他，船里本来有一百万——全是金子——可他们坑了他。”

她说的是特尔弗联合信托公司那些命途多舛的营生之一——为了打捞那艘沉入海底的宝船，公司的钱像水一样泼了出去。她疯了。这个跋扈女人内里的脆弱，他从头一起就猜到了。

===Original===
“Open the door; we will talk it over. I’m perfectly sure that the treasure ship scheme was a sound one.”

“Are you?” she asked eagerly, and the next minute the door was open and Mr. J. G. Reeder was in that room of death.

===Chinese===
“开门；咱们好好谈谈。我完全可以肯定，宝船那桩营生原本是稳妥的。”

“真的？”她急切地问。下一刻门开了，J. G. 里德先生走进了那间死亡的屋子。

===Original===
“First of all, let me have the key of the telephone-room—you are quite wrong about that young lady: she is my wife.”

The woman stared at him blankly.

“Your wife?” A slow smile transfigured the face. “Why—I was silly. Here is the key.”

===Chinese===
“首先，把电话间的钥匙给我——你把那位小姐看岔了：她是我妻子。”

那女人怔怔地瞪着他。

“你的妻子？”一张脸慢慢绽开笑意，焕然一新。“哦——我可真傻。给你钥匙。”

===Original===
He persuaded her to come downstairs with him, and when the frightened girl was released, he whispered a few words to her, and she flew out of the house.

“Shall we go into the drawing-room?” he asked, and Mrs. Welford led the way.

“And now will you tell me how you knew—about the jugs?” he asked gently.

===Chinese===
他劝得她随自己下了楼。受惊的姑娘一被放出来，他对她耳语了几句，她便飞也似的奔出宅子。

“我们到客厅坐坐？”他问；韦尔福德太太在前头引路。

“现在能告诉我，您是怎么知道的——关于这些罐子？”他柔声问。

===Original===
She was sitting on the edge of a sofa, her hands clasped on her knees, her deep-set eyes staring at the carpet.

“John—that was my first husband—told me. He was a professor of chemistry and natural science, and also about the electric furnace. It is so easy to make if you have power—we use nothing but electricity in this house for heating and everything. And then I saw my poor darling being ruined through me, and I found how much money there was in the bank, and I told Billingham to draw it and bring it to me without Sidney knowing. He came here in the evening. I sent Sidney away—to Brighton, I think. I did everything—put the new lock on the telephone box and fixed the shaft from the roof to the little room—it was easy to disperse everything with all the doors open and an electric fan working on the floor—”

She was telling him about the improvised furnace in the greenhouse when the police arrived with the divisional surgeon, and she went away with them, weeping because there would be nobody to press Sidney’s ties or put out his shirts.

===Chinese===
她坐在沙发边上，双手交叠放在膝头，深陷的眼睛盯着地毯。

“约翰——那是我的前夫——告诉我的。他是化学和博物学教授，还讲过电炉的事。只要有电，做起来容易得很——这宅子取暖也罢、什么都罢，用的全是电。后来我眼看我可怜的心肝要毁在我手里，我打听到银行里有多少钱，就吩咐比林厄姆取出来给我送来，别叫西德尼知道。他是傍晚到的。我把西德尼打发走了——好像是去了布莱顿。样样都是我干的——给电话间装了新锁，把风道从屋顶通到那间小屋——门户大开，再在地板上架一台风扇，什么都散得干干净净——”

她正给他讲温室里那座土法制成的电炉，警察带着警区医生到了；她随他们走了，一路哭着，因为往后再没有人替西德尼熨领带、晾衬衫了。

===Original===
Mr. Reeder took the inspector up to the little room and showed him its contents.

“This funnel leads to the telephone box—” he began.

“But the jugs are empty,” interrupted the officer.

===Chinese===
里德先生领着巡官上了那间小屋，指给他看屋里的东西。

“这漏斗通到电话间——”他开口说。

“可这些罐子是空的呀，”警官打断他。

===Original===
Mr. J. G. Reeder struck a match and, waiting until it burnt freely, lowered it into the jug. Half an inch lower than the rim the light went out.

“Carbon monoxide,” he said, “which is made by steeping marble chips in hydrochloric acid—you will find the mixture in the tank. The gas is colourless and odourless—and heavy. You can pour it out of a jug like water. She could have bought the marble, but was afraid of arousing suspicion. Billingham was killed that way. She got him to go to the telephone box, probably closed the door on him herself, and then killed him painlessly.”

“What did she do with the body?” asked the horrified officer.

“Come out into the hothouse,” said Mr. Reeder, “and pray do not expect to see horrors: an electric furnace will dissolve a diamond to its original elements.”

===Chinese===
J. G. 里德先生划了根火柴，等火苗烧旺，把它探进罐里。离罐口半英寸的地方，火光熄了。

“一氧化碳，”他说，“大理石碎块泡在盐酸里就生这个——那槽子里就有混合液。这气体无色无味——而且比空气重。能像倒水一样从罐里倒出来。大理石她本可以买，又怕惹人起疑。比林厄姆就是这么被害的。她哄他进了电话间，多半还亲手替他掩上门，然后毫无痛楚地结果了他。”

“尸体她怎么处置的？”骇然的警官问。

“到暖房来吧，”里德先生说，“也请您别指望看见什么骇人的场面：电炉能把一颗钻石都分解回它本来的元素。”

---

===Original===
Mr. Reeder went home that night in a state of mental perturbation, and for an hour paced the floor of his large study in Brockley Road.

Over and over in his mind he turned one vital problem: did he owe an apology to Margaret Belman for saying that she was his wife?

===Chinese===
那天夜里，里德先生心神不宁地回了家，在布罗克利路他那间大书房里来回踱了一个钟头。

有个重大的问题在他心里翻来覆去：自己对玛格丽特·贝尔曼说她是自己的妻子，这笔账该不该赔个不是？

## The Strange Case / 离奇案件

===Original===
In the days of Mr. Reeder’s youth, which were also the days when hansom cabs plied for hire and no gentleman went abroad without a nosegay in the lapel of his coat, he had been sent, in company with another young officer from Scotland Yard, to arrest a youthful inventor of Nottingham who earned more than a competence by methods which were displeasing to Scotland Yard. Not machines nor ingenious contrivances for saving labour did this young man invent—but stories. And they were not stories in the accepted sense of the word, for they were misstatements designed to extract money from the pockets of simple-minded men and women. Mr. Eiter employed no fewer than twenty-five aliases and as many addresses in the broadcasting of his fiction, and he was on the way to amassing a considerable fortune when a square-toed Nemesis took him by the arm and led him to the seat of justice. An unsympathetic judge sent Mr. Eiter to seven years’ penal servitude, describing him as an unconscionable swindler and a menace to society—at which Willie Eiter smiled, for he had a skin beside which the elephant’s was gossamer silk.

Mr. Reeder remembered the case chiefly because the prosecuting attorney, commenting upon the various disguises and subterfuges which the prisoner had adopted, remarked upon a peculiarity which was revealed in every part which the convict had played—his inability to spell “able” which he invariably wrote as though he were naming the victim of Cain’s envy.

“There is this identity to be discovered in every criminal, however ingenious he may be,” the advocate had said. “Whatever his disguise, no matter how cleverly he dissociates one role or pose from another, there is a distinguishable weakness common to every character he affects, and especially is this observable in criminals who live by fraud and trickery.”

===Chinese===
在里德先生（Mr. Reeder）年轻的时候——那也是双轮马车还在街头揽客、绅士出门不襟上别一小束鲜花便不出门的年代——他曾奉命与苏格兰场的另一名年轻警官同去诺丁汉，逮捕一位年轻的发明家。此人靠苏格兰场甚为不悦的手段，挣下了一份不止小康的家业。这年轻人发明的既不是机器，也不是省力的精巧装置，而是故事。而且那些故事也名不副实——实是专为从头脑简单的男男女女口袋里掏钱而编造的谎言。艾特先生（Mr. Eiter）散布他的小说时，用过的化名不下二十五个，地址也同样繁多；眼看一笔可观的财产就要攒成，一位穿方头鞋的复仇女神挽住了他的手臂，把他领到了法庭跟前。一位毫无恻隐之心的法官判了艾特先生七年苦役，骂他是个丧尽良心的骗子、社会的一大祸害——威利·艾特听了只是微微一笑，他那张脸皮，拿大象皮比都是蝉翼绸。

里德先生之所以牢记这桩案子，主要是因为公诉律师在评论被告种种伪装与狡计时，指出了一个在他扮演过的每个角色里都露头的怪癖——他不会拼“able”（能够）这个词，回回都写成“abel”，仿佛在提名该隐因嫉妒而下手的那个受害者：亚伯（Abel）。

“每个罪犯身上都能找出这样一种印记，任他多么机巧。”那位律师当年说，“无论他怎样伪装，无论他把一个角色一副面目同另一个分得多么干净，他所假扮的每一个人格里，都共有一个可辨的弱点。靠诈骗为生的罪犯身上，这一点尤其看得出来。”

===Original===
This Mr. Reeder remembered throughout his useful life. Few people knew that he had ever been associated with Scotland Yard. He himself evaded any question that was put to him on the subject. It was his amiable trait to pretend that he was the veriest amateur and that his success in the detection of wrongdoing was to be traced to his own evil mind that saw wrong very often where no wrong was.

He saw wrong in so many apparently innocent acts of man that it was well for his reputation that those who were acquainted with and pitied him because of his seeming inadequacy and unattractive appearance did not know what dark thoughts filled his mind.

There was a very pretty girl who lived in Brockley Road at a boardinghouse. He did not like Miss Margaret Belman because she was pretty, but because she was sensible: two terms which are as a rule antagonistic. He liked her so well that he often travelled home on the cars with her, and they used to discuss the Prince of Wales, the Labour Government, the high cost of living, and other tender subjects with great animation. It was from Miss Belman that he learned about her fellow-boarder, Mrs. Carlin, and once he travelled back with her to Brockley—a frail, slim girl with experience in her face and the hint of tragedy in her fine eyes.

===Chinese===
这话里德先生受用终身。很少有人知道他曾在苏格兰场供过职。凡有人问起，他总是绕开话头。他有个可爱的做派：假装自己是个地道的外行，而他在侦破罪恶上的成功，全归功于他那颗邪恶的头脑——它常常在本来无恶的地方看见恶。

人类种种看似无辜的举动里，他都能看出恶来；幸而那些因他不济的外表、其貌不扬而结识他、怜惜他的人，并不知道他脑子里装着多少阴暗的念头——不然他的名声可就悬了。

有一位挺标致的姑娘，住在布罗克利路的一幢寄宿公寓里。里德先生喜欢玛格丽特·贝尔曼小姐（Miss Margaret Belman），倒不为她标致，而为她明白事理——这两个词向来是冤家。他喜欢她喜欢得紧，常与她同车回家，两人还常兴致勃勃地议论威尔士亲王、工党内阁、生活的高昂开销，以及其他种种温情脉脉的话题。贝尔曼小姐的同宿卡林太太，他就是从她嘴里听说的；他还曾陪卡林太太同回布罗克利一次——那是个纤弱苗条的姑娘，脸上带着历练，好看的眼睛里含着悲剧的影子。

===Original===
So it happened that he knew all about Mr. Harry Carlin long before Lord Sellington sent for him, for Mr. Reeder had the gift of evoking confidences by the suggestion rather than the expression of his sympathy.

She spoke of her husband without bitterness—but also without regret. She knew him rather well, despite the shortness of their married life. She hinted once, and inadvertently, that there was a rich relation to whose wealth her husband would be heir if he were a normal man. Her son would, in due course, be the possessor of a great title—and penniless. She was at such pains to rectify her statement that Mr. Reeder, suspicious of peerages that come to Brockley, was assured of her sincerity, however great might be her error. Later he learned that the title was that borne by the Right Honourable the Earl of Sellington and Manford.

There came a slack time for the Public Prosecutor’s office, when it seemed that sin had gone out of the world; and Mr. Reeder sat for a week on end in his little room, twiddling his thumbs or reading the advertisement columns of *The Times*, or drawing grotesque men upon his blotting-pad, varying these performances with the excursions he was in the habit of making to those parts of London which very few people choose for their recreation. He loved to poke about the slum areas which lie in the neighbourhood of the Great Surrey Docks; he was not averse from frequenting the north side of the river, again in the dock areas; but when his chief asked him whether he spent much time at Limehouse, Mr. Reeder replied with a pathetic smile.

===Chinese===
于是，早在塞林顿勋爵（Lord Sellington）派人请他之前，他就把哈里·卡林先生的底细摸了个透——因为里德先生有一种本事：他引发人家的倾诉，靠的不是把同情挂在外面，而是暗暗递上一份。

她说起丈夫，不带怨怼——也不带惋惜。尽管婚姻生活短促，她对他知之颇深。有一次她失口暗示，自家有一位阔亲戚，丈夫若是个正常人，早晚继承那笔家财。她的儿子到时候也会得到一个显赫的头衔——外加身无分文。她慌忙下大力气更正自己的话，反倒叫里德先生确信了她的诚恳，尽管她可能错得厉害——他素来怀疑爵位会落到布罗克利来。后来他得知，那头衔属于尊敬的塞林顿与曼福德伯爵阁下。

检察总长公署迎来了一段闲日子，仿佛罪恶已从这世界上绝了迹。里德先生一连一星期坐在他那间小屋里，或者转大拇指，或者读《泰晤士报》的广告栏，或者在吸墨垫上画些奇形怪状的小人，间或换换花样，到他惯常出没的伦敦角落去走走——那种地方，极少有人挑来消遣。他爱在大萨里船坞一带的贫民区里转悠；也不反感常去河北岸，同样在船坞区；可上司问他是否常去莱姆豪斯，里德先生只报以一个楚楚可怜的微笑。

===Original===
“No, sir,” he said gently, “I read about such places—I find them infinitely more interesting in the pages of a—er—novel. Yes, there are Chinese there, and I suppose Chinese are romantic, but even they do not add romance to Limehouse, which is the most respectable and law-abiding corner of the East End.”

One morning the Public Prosecutor sent for his chief detective, and Mr. Reeder obeyed the summons with a light step and a pleasant sense of anticipation.

“Go over to the Foreign Office and have a talk with Lord Sellington,” said the Prosecutor. “He is rather worried about a nephew of his, Harry Carlin. Do you know the name?”

Mr. Reeder shook his head; for the moment he did not associate the pale girl who typed for her living.

“He’s a pretty bad lot,” explained the Prosecutor, “and unfortunately he’s Sellington’s heir. I rather imagine the old gentleman wants you to confirm his view.”

“Dear me!” said Mr. Reeder, and stole forth.

===Chinese===
“不去，先生。”他温和地说，“这种地方我是从书上读的——在一部——呃——小说的书页里，它们有趣得多。不错，那儿有中国人，我想中国人自有他们的浪漫，可就连他们也没给莱姆豪斯添上半分罗曼蒂克；那可是东区最安分守法的一角。”

一天早晨，检察总长派人去请他的主任侦探，里德先生应召而去，脚步轻快，心里怀着愉快的期待。

“去外交部一趟，同塞林顿勋爵谈谈。”检察总长说，“他为一个侄子哈里·卡林相当发愁。你知道这个名字吗？”

里德先生摇了摇头；一时间没联想起那个靠打字谋生的苍白姑娘。

“他可不是个好东西。”检察总长解释道，“不幸的是，他又是塞林顿的继承人。我琢磨着，老先生是要你去证实他的看法。”

“哎呀！”里德先生说罢，蹑手蹑脚地走了出去。

===Original===
Lord Sellington, Undersecretary of State for Foreign Affairs, was a bachelor and an immensely rich man. He had been rich in 1912 when, in a panic due to certain legislation which he thought would affect him adversely as a great landowner, he sold his estates and invested the larger bulk of his fortune (against all expert advice) in American industrial stocks. The war had trebled his possessions. Heavy investments in oil lands had made him many times a millionaire. He was a philanthropist, gave liberally to institutions devoted to the care of young children; he was the founder of the Eastleigh Children’s Home, and subscribed liberally to other similar institutions. A thin, rather sour-faced man, he glared up under his shaggy eyebrows as Mr. Reeder sidled apologetically into his room.

“So you’re Reeder, eh?” he grumbled, and was evidently not very much impressed by his visitor. “Sit down, sit down,” he said testily, walked to the door as though he were not certain that Mr. Reeder had closed it, and came back and flopped into his chair on the other side of the table. “I have sent for you in preference to notifying the police,” he said. “Sir James speaks of you, Mr. Reeder, as a gentleman of discretion.”

Mr. Reeder bowed slightly, and there followed a long and awkward pause, which the Undersecretary ended in an abrupt, irritable way.

===Chinese===
外交部政务次官塞林顿勋爵是个单身汉，家资巨万。早在 1912 年他就富了：当年有几项立法，他以为会不利于他这样的大地主，一时恐慌，卖掉地产，把大部分家财（顶着一切行家的忠告）投进了美国的工业股票。大战使他的财产翻了三倍。在油田上的巨额投资，更使他成了数倍的百万富翁。他是位慈善家，向收容幼童的机构慷慨解囊；他是伊斯特利儿童之家的创办人，也给其他类似机构大量捐款。他是个瘦削、面相刻薄的男人；里德先生赔着小心侧身进屋时，他从浓眉底下抬眼怒视。

“你就是里德，嗯？”他咕哝道，显然没把来客怎么放在眼里。“坐，坐。”他不耐烦地说，走到门口，似乎拿不准里德先生关没关门，又走回来，一屁股跌坐在桌子另一侧的椅子里。“我叫你来，而没有去通知警方。”他说，“詹姆斯爵士提起过你，里德先生，说你是个嘴严的君子。”

里德先生微微欠身。接着是一段又长又尴尬的沉默，最后还是次官突兀暴躁地把它打破了。

===Original===
“I have a nephew—Harry Carlin. Do you know him?”

“I know of him,” said Mr. Reeder truthfully; in his walk to the Foreign Office he had remembered the deserted wife.

“Then you know nothing good of him!” exploded his lordship. “The man is a blackguard, a waster, a disgrace to the name he bears! If he were not my brother’s son I would have him under lock and key tonight—the scoundrel! I have four bills in my possession—”

He stopped himself, pulled open a drawer savagely, took out a letter and slammed it on the table.

===Chinese===
“我有个侄子——哈里·卡林。你认识吗？”

“听说过。”里德先生说得实实在在；来外交部的一路上，他已想起了那位被遗弃的妻子。

“那你听说的没一句好话！”勋爵炸了，“这人是恶棍，是败家子，是他那个姓氏的耻辱！若不是我兄弟的儿子，我今晚就叫他进班房——这无赖！我手里有四张票据——”

他把话咽了回去，狠狠拉开抽屉，取出一封信，砰地摔在桌上。

===Original===
“Read that,” he snapped.

Mr. Reeder pulled his glasses a little farther up his nose (he always held them very tight when he was really using them) and perused the message. It was headed “The Eastleigh Home for Children,” and was a brief request for five thousand pounds, which the writer said he would send for that evening, and was signed “Arthur Lassard.”

“You know Lassard, of course?” said his lordship. “He is the gentleman associated with me in my philanthropic work. Certain monies were due for land which we purchased adjoining the home. As you probably know, there are lawyers who never accept cheques for properties they sell on behalf of their clients, and I had the money ready and left it with my secretary, and one of Lassard’s people was calling for it. That it was called for, I need hardly tell you,” said his lordship grimly. “Whoever planned the coup planned it well. They knew I would be speaking in the House of Lords last night; they also knew that I had recently changed my secretary and had engaged a gentleman to whom most of my associates are strangers.
