A bearded man came for the money at half-past six, produced a note from Mr. Lassard, and that was the end of the money, except that we have discovered that it was changed this morning into American bills. Of course, both letters were forged: Lassard never signed either, and made no demand whatever for the money, which was not needed for another week.”

“Did anybody know about this transaction?” asked Mr. Reeder.

===Chinese===
“看这个。”他厉声道。

里德先生把眼镜往鼻梁上又推了推（真要用它时，他总夹得极紧），细读那封信。信头是“伊斯特利儿童之家”，内容简短，索要五千英镑，写信人说当晚即遣人来取，署名“阿瑟·拉萨德”（Arthur Lassard）。

“拉萨德您当然认识？”勋爵说，“就是同我一起办慈善的那位先生。有一笔地款要付，是为儿童之家购置邻地用的。您大概知道，有的律师代客户出售产业时从不收支票。我把钱备好了，交给我秘书，拉萨德先生那边派人来取。钱被人取走了——这我不用多说。”勋爵沉着脸说，“策划这一手的人筹划得周全。他们知道我昨晚要在上议院发言；也知道我刚换了秘书，新请的这位先生，我的熟人大多不认得。六点半，一个蓄须的男人来取钱，出示了拉萨德先生的条子，钱就这样没了——只查到它今天早晨被兑成了美国钞票。两封信当然都是伪造的：拉萨德两封都没签过，也压根没催讨过这笔款，这钱还有一礼拜才到期呢。”

“这笔交易，还有谁知道？”里德先生问。

===Original===
His lordship nodded slowly.

“My nephew knew. He came to my house two days ago to borrow money. He has a small income from his late mother’s estate, but insufficient to support him in his reckless extravagance. He admitted frankly to me that he had come back from Aix broke. How long he had been in London I am unable to tell you, but he was in my library when my secretary came in with the money which I had drawn from the bank in preparation for paying the bill when it became due. Very foolishly I explained why I had so much cash in the house and why I was unable to oblige him with the thousand pounds which he wanted to borrow,” he added dourly.

Mr. Reeder scratched his chin.

“What am I to do?” he asked.

===Chinese===
勋爵缓缓点头。

“我侄子知道。两天前他上我家借钱。他亡母的产业给他留了点小进项，不够支撑他的挥霍无度。他倒坦白，直说他从埃克斯回来时已经一文不名。他在伦敦待了多久，我说不上；可我秘书提着钱进书房时，他就在书房里——那笔钱是我从银行提出来的，备着票据到期时支付。我蠢得很，竟解释了家里为何放着这么多现款，又为何拿不出他要借的那一千镑。”他阴沉沉地补了一句。

里德先生挠了挠下巴。

“要我做什么呢？”他问。

===Original===
“I want you to find Carlin,” Lord Sellington almost snarled. “But most I want that money back—you understand, Reeder? You’re to tell him that unless he repays—”

Mr. Reeder was gazing steadily at the cornice moulding.

“It almost sounds as if I am being asked to compound a felony, my lord,” he said respectfully. “But I realise, in the peculiar circumstances, we must adopt peculiar methods. The black-bearded gentleman who called for the money would appear to have been”—he hesitated—“disguised?”

“Of course he was disguised,” said the other irritably.

“One reads of such things,” said Mr. Reeder with a sigh, “but so seldom does the bearded stranger appear in real life! Will you be good enough to tell me your nephew’s address?”

===Chinese===
“我要你找到卡林。”塞林顿勋爵几乎是咆哮，“但我最要紧的是把钱追回来——你明白吗，里德？你去告诉他，他若不还——”

里德先生目不转睛地盯着檐口的线脚。

“这听着几乎像是让我私了一桩重罪了，大人。”他恭恭敬敬地说，“不过我明白，情势特殊，手段也得特殊。来取钱的那位黑胡子先生，想必是”——他顿了顿——“化了装的？”

“自然是化了装的。”对方不耐烦地说。

“这种事书上常有。”里德先生叹道，“可蓄胡子的陌生人，现实里何其少见！可否请您赐告令侄的住址？”

===Original===
Lord Sellington took a card from his pocket and threw it across the table. It fell to the floor, but he did not apologise. He was that kind of man.

“Jermyn Mansions,” said Mr. Reeder as he rose. “I will see what can be done.”

Lord Sellington grunted something which might have been a tender farewell, but probably was not.

Jermyn Mansions is a very small, narrow-fronted building and, as Mr. Reeder knew—and he knew a great deal—was a block of residential flats, which were run by an ex-butler who was also the lessee of the establishment. By great good fortune, as he afterwards learned, Harry Carlin was at home, and in a few minutes the man from the Public Prosecutor’s office was ushered into a shabby drawing-room that overlooked Jermyn Street.

===Chinese===
塞林顿勋爵从口袋里掏出一张名片，朝桌对面掷来。名片落在地上，他也不道歉。他就是这种人。

“杰敏公寓。”里德先生起身道，“我看看能做点什么。”

塞林顿勋爵咕哝了一句什么，也许是一声温情的道别，多半不是。

杰敏公寓是栋门面很窄的小楼；里德先生知道——他知道的可多——那是一栋供人寄住的公寓楼，经营者是个从前当过管家的人，也是这产业的承租人。真是走运——他后来才知道——哈里·卡林恰好在家。不多时，检察总长公署来的这个人就被领进一间寒酸的客厅，窗子俯临着杰敏街。

===Original===
A tall young man stood by the window, looking disconsolately into that narrow and lively thoroughfare, and turned as Mr. Reeder was announced. Thin-faced, narrow-headed, small-eyed, if he possessed any of the family traits and failings, the most marked was perhaps his too ready irritation.

Mr. Reeder saw, through an open door, a very untidy bedroom, caught a glimpse of a battered trunk covered with Continental labels.

“Well, what the devil do you want?” demanded Mr. Carlin. Yet, in spite of his tone, there was an undercurrent of disquiet which Mr. Reeder detected.

“May I sit down?” said the detective and, without waiting for an invitation, pulled a chair from the wall and sat down gingerly, for he knew the quality of lodging-house chairs.

===Chinese===
一个高个子青年立在窗前，郁郁地望着下面那条狭窄热闹的街道；通报里德先生到时，他转过身来。瘦脸，窄脑门，小眼睛——如果说他承袭了家族的相貌或脾性，最显著的怕就是那一点就着的火气。

里德先生从敞开的门里望见一间凌乱不堪的卧室，瞥见一只贴满欧陆行李签的破箱子。

“喂，你到底想干什么？”卡林先生喝问。然而别看他语气硬，里德先生还是听出了底下那股不安的暗流。

“可以坐坐吗？”侦探说着，不等邀请便从墙边拖过一把椅子，小心翼翼地落了座——寄宿公寓的椅子是什么成色，他是有数的。

===Original===
His self-possession, the hint of authority he carried in his voice, increased Mr. Harry Carlin’s uneasiness; and when Mr. Reeder plunged straight into the object of his visit, he saw the man go pale.

“It is a difficult subject to open,” said Mr. Reeder, carefully smoothing his knees, “and when I find myself in that predicament I usually employ the plainest language.”

And plain language he employed with a vengeance. Halfway through Carlin sat down with a gasp.

“What—what!” he stammered. “Does that old brute dare—! I thought you came about the bills—I mean—”

“I mean,” said Mr. Reeder carefully, “that if you have had a little fun with your relative, I think that jest has gone far enough. Lord Sellington is prepared, on the money being refunded, to regard the whole thing as an over-elaborate practical joke on your part—”

===Chinese===
他的镇定，他话音里隐隐的官威，加重了哈里·卡林的不安；待里德先生单刀直入道明来意，他眼看这人白了脸。

“这是个不好开口的题目。”里德先生说，一边细心抚平膝头的裤线，“每回陷进这种窘境，我通常都用最直白的话。”

他这回把直白用到了家。话到一半，卡林倒吸一口气，坐了下去。

“什么——什么！”他结结巴巴，“那老畜生竟敢——！我还当你是为票据来的——我是说——”

“我是说，”里德先生不紧不慢，“您若是同令亲开了个小玩笑，我想这玩笑也开得够大了。只要款项退还，塞林顿勋爵愿意把整件事看作您一场过分用心的恶作剧——”

===Original===
“But I haven’t touched his beastly money!” the young man almost screamed. “I don’t want his money—”

“On the contrary, sir,” said Reeder gently, “you want it very badly. You left the Hotel Continental without paying your bill; you owe some six hundred pounds to various gentlemen from whom you borrowed that amount; there is a warrant out for you in France for passing cheques which are usually described by the vulgar as—er—‘dud.’ Indeed”—again Mr. Reeder scratched his chin and looked thoughtfully out of the window—“indeed I know no gentleman in Jermyn Street who is so badly in need of money as your good self.”

Carlin would have stopped him, but the middle-aged man went on remorselessly.

“I have been for an hour in the Record Department of Scotland Yard, where your name is not unknown, Mr. Carlin. You left London rather hurriedly to avoid—er—proceedings of an unpleasant character. ‘Bills,’ I think you said? You are known to have been the associate of people with whom the police are a little better acquainted than they are with Mr. Carlin. You were also associated with a racecourse fraud of a peculiarly unpleasant character. And amongst your minor delinquencies there is—er—a deserted young wife, at present engaged in a City office as typist, and a small boy for whom you have never provided.”

===Chinese===
“可他那臭钱我碰都没碰！”年轻人几乎是尖叫，“我不要他的钱——”

“恰恰相反，先生。”里德先生温和地说，“您缺钱缺得厉害呢。您欠着欧陆大酒店的账不付一走了之；您欠着好几位先生大约六百英镑，都是您亲手借下的；法国还给您开了逮捕证，罪名是开那种俗话称作——呃——‘空头’的支票。的确”——里德先生又挠了挠下巴，若有所思地望着窗外——“的确，整个杰敏街上，我还找不出一位比您更急需钱的先生。”

卡林想拦住他，可这位中年人毫不留情地讲了下去。

“我在苏格兰场的档案科待了一个钟头，您的大名在那儿并不陌生，卡林先生。您当年离开伦敦走得甚是仓促，为的是躲开——呃——性质不愉快的诉讼。您方才说的，是‘票据’？据查，您结交的那伙人，警方对他们的熟识程度，可比对卡林先生您略胜一筹。您还同一场性质特别恶劣的赛马场诈骗有牵连。而在您那些小过失里，还有——呃——一位被遗弃的年轻妻子，目前在本城一家写字楼当打字员，外加一个您从没抚养过的小男孩。”

===Original===
Carlin licked his dry lips.

“Is that all?” he asked, with an attempt at a sneer, though his voice shook and his trembling hands betrayed his agitation.

Reeder nodded.

“Well, I’ll tell you something. I want to do the right thing by my wife. I admit I haven’t played square with her, but I’ve never had the money to play square. That old devil has always been rolling in it, curse him! I’m the only relation he has, and what has he done? Left every bean to these damned children’s homes of his! If somebody has caught him for five thousand I’m glad! I shouldn’t have the nerve to do it myself, but I’m glad if they did—whoever they may be. Left every penny to a lot of squalling, sticky-faced brats, and not a bean to me!”

===Chinese===
卡林舔了舔干裂的嘴唇。

“就这些？”他想挤出一声冷笑，可声音发颤，抖索的双手泄了底。

里德点点头。

“行，我也告诉你一件事。我想对得起我老婆。我承认我没好好待她，可我从没那个本钱好好待她。那老东西向来在钱里打滚，该死的！我是他唯一的亲人，他干了什么？把每一个铜子儿都留给他那些该死的儿童之家！要是有人敲了他五千镑，我高兴！我自己没胆量干这个，可他们要是干了，我高兴——管他们是谁呢。每一个便士都留给一帮嚎哭的、满脸黏糊糊的小崽子，一个子儿也不给我！”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder let him rave on without interruption, until at last, almost exhausted by his effort, he dropped down into a deep chair and glared at his visitor.

“Tell him that,” he said breathlessly; “tell him that!”

Mr. Reeder made time to call at the little office in Portugal Street wherein was housed the headquarters of Lord Sellington’s various philanthropic enterprises. Mr. Arthur Lassard had evidently been in communication with his noble patron, for no sooner did Reeder give his name than he was ushered into the plainly furnished room where the superintendent sat.

It was not unnatural that Lord Sellington should have as his assistant in the good work so famous an organiser as Mr. Arthur Lassard. Mr. Lassard’s activities in the philanthropic world were many. A broad-shouldered man with a jolly red face and a bald head, he had survived all the attacks which come the way of men engaged in charitable work, and was not particularly impressed by a recent visit he had had from Harry Carlin.

===Chinese===
里德先生由他骂下去，一声不打断，直到他骂得筋疲力尽，一头跌进一张高背椅里，怒视着来客。

“把这话告诉他。”他喘着说，“告诉他！”

里德先生挤出时间，去葡萄牙街那间小办公室走了一趟——塞林顿勋爵名下各项慈善事业的总处就设在那里。阿瑟·拉萨德先生显然已同他高贵的赞助人通过消息；里德先生刚一通报姓名，便被领进那间陈设简朴的房间，主管正坐在里面。

塞林顿勋爵在这桩善举里用阿瑟·拉萨德先生这样一位大名鼎鼎的办差好手做助手，原也顺理成章。拉萨德先生在慈善界的活动为数不少。他肩膀宽阔，红光满面，头顶秃了；办慈善的人该挨的明枪暗箭他都挨过、也都挺过来了；对哈里·卡林前不久的来访，他并不怎么放在心上。

===Original===
“I don’t wish to be unkind,” he said, “but our friend called here on such a lame excuse that I can’t help feeling that his real object was to secure a sheet of my stationery. I did, in fact, leave him in the room for a few minutes, and he had the opportunity to purloin the paper if he desired.”

“What was his excuse?” asked Mr. Reeder, and the other shrugged.

“He wanted money. At first he was civil and asked me to persuade his uncle; then he grew abusive, said that I was conspiring to rob him—I and my ‘infernal charities’!”

He chuckled, but grew grave again.

===Chinese===
“我不想说话刻薄。”他说，“可咱们那位朋友上门的借口蹩脚得很，我不由得觉得，他真正的目的是弄一张我的信纸。事实上，我确实把他一个人留在房里几分钟，他若存心顺走信纸，是有机会的。”

“他的借口是什么？”里德先生问，对方耸了耸肩。

“他要钱。起先还算客气，求我去劝他叔叔；后来就骂开了，说我和我那‘害死人的慈善’合起伙来抢他！”

他呵呵一笑，随即又板起脸。

===Original===
“The situation is mysterious to me,” he said. “Evidently Carlin has committed some crime against his lordship, for he is terrified of him!”

“You think Mr. Carlin forged your name and secured the money?”

The superintendent spread out his arms in despair.

“Who else can I suspect?” he asked.

Mr. Reeder took the forged letter from his pocket and read it again.

===Chinese===
“这局面叫我摸不着头脑。”他说，“卡林分明是对勋爵犯了什么事，他怕他怕得要命！”

“您认为是卡林先生伪造了您的签名取走了钱？”

主管绝望地摊开双手。

“不疑他我又疑谁？”他问。

里德先生从口袋里取出那封伪造信，又读了一遍。

===Original===
“I’ve just been on the phone to his lordship,” Mr. Lassard went on. “He is waiting, of course, to hear your report, and if you have failed to make this young man confess his guilt, Lord Sellington intends seeing his nephew tonight and making an appeal to him. I can hardly believe that Mr. Carlin could have done this wicked thing, though the circumstances seem very suspicious. Have you seen him, Mr. Reeder?”

“I have seen him,” said Mr. Reeder shortly. “Oh, yes, I have seen him!”

Mr. Arthur Lassard was scrutinising his face as though he were trying to read the conclusion which the detective had reached, but Mr. Reeder’s face was notoriously expressionless.

He offered a limp hand and went back to the Undersecretary’s house. The interview was short and on the whole disagreeable.

===Chinese===
“我刚同勋爵通过电话。”拉萨德先生接着说，“他自然在等您的报告；您若没能让这年轻人认罪，塞林顿勋爵打算今晚去见他，向他恳求。虽说情形疑云重重，我简直不能相信卡林先生会干出这种坏事。您见着他了吗，里德先生？”

“见着了。”里德先生简短地说，“哦，见着了，见着了！”

阿瑟·拉萨德先生端详着他的脸，仿佛想读出侦探得出的结论；可里德先生的脸没有表情，这是出了名的。

他伸出一只绵软的手一握，便回次官府上去了。那番晤谈很短，总的说不愉快。

===Original===
“I never dreamt he would confess to you,” said Lord Sellington with ill-disguised contempt. “Harry needs somebody to frighten him, and, my God! I’m the man to do it! I’m seeing him tonight.”

A fit of coughing stopped him and he gulped savagely from a little medicine bottle that stood on his desk.

“I’ll see him tonight,” he gasped, “and I’ll tell him what I intend doing! I’ve spared him hitherto because of his relationship and because he inherits the title. But I’m through. Every cent I have goes to charity. I’m good for twenty years yet, but every penny—”

He stopped. He was a man who never disguised his emotion, and Mr. Reeder, who understood men, saw the struggle that was going on in Sellington’s mind.

“He says he hasn’t had a chance. I may have treated him unfairly—we shall see.” He waved the detective from his office as though he were dismissing a strange dog that had intruded upon his privacy, and Mr. Reeder went out reluctantly, for he had something to tell his lordship.

===Chinese===
“我做梦也没指望他会向你招认。”塞林顿勋爵说，轻蔑毫不掩饰，“哈里这号人得有人吓唬，而且，天哪！吓唬他的人就是我！今晚我就去见他。”

一阵咳嗽打断了他，他抓起桌上的一只小药瓶，狠狠灌了一口。

“今晚我就去见他。”他喘着气说，“把我的打算告诉他！我至今容他，只因他是自家骨肉，只因头衔要由他继承。可我受够了。我每一个子儿都归慈善。我这条命还能再撑二十年，可每一个便士——”

他停住了。他是个从不掩饰感情的人；里德先生懂得人，看得出塞林顿心里正翻腾着一场交战。

“他说是我没给过他机会。或许我真亏待了他——走着瞧吧。”他挥手把侦探打发走，像轰一条闯进私宅的野狗；里德先生迟迟疑疑地退了出去，因为他有一件事要禀告勋爵。

===Original===
It was peculiar to him that, in his more secretive moments, he sought the privacy of his old-fashioned study in Brockley Road. For two hours he sat at his desk calling a succession of numbers—and curiously enough, the gentlemen to whom he spoke were bookmakers. Most of them he knew. In the days when he was the greatest expert in the world on forged currency notes, he had been brought into contact with a class which is often the innocent medium by which the forger distributed his handicraft—and more often the instrument of his detection.

It was a Friday, a day on which most of the principals were in their offices till a late hour. At eight o’clock he finished, wrote a note and, phoning for a messenger, sent his letter on its fateful errand.

He spent the rest of the evening musing on past experiences and in refreshing his memory from the thin scrapbooks which filled two shelves in his study.

===Chinese===
他有个怪癖：越是办隐秘事的时刻，越要躲进布罗克利路那间老式书房里图个清静。他在写字台前坐了两个钟头，拨了一连串号码——说来奇怪，同他通话的先生们全是赌注登记人。这些人他大多认识。当年他是世上首屈一指的假钞鉴定权威，同这一类人打过不少交道——伪造者散出手艺，常常拿他们当无辜的媒介；而叫他落网，更多时候也靠他们做工具。

那天是星期五，多数登记行的老板这一晚都在行里待到很晚。八点钟他忙完了，写了一封短信，打电话叫来信差，把信送上它命中注定的差事。

那晚余下的时间，他都在回味往事，翻检书房里排满两层书架的薄薄剪贴簿，唤醒记忆。

===Original===
What happened elsewhere that evening can best be told in the plain language of the witness-box. Lord Sellington had gone home after his interview with Mr. Reeder suffering from a feverish cold, and was disposed, according to the evidence of his secretary, to put off the interview which he had arranged with his nephew. A telephone message had been sent through to Mr. Carlin’s hotel, but he was out. Until nine o’clock his lordship was busy with the affairs of his numerous charities, Mr. Lassard being in attendance. Lord Sellington was working in a small study which opened from his bedroom.

At a quarter-past nine Carlin arrived and was shown upstairs by the butler, who subsequently stated that he heard voices raised in anger. Mr. Carlin came downstairs and was shown out as the clock struck half-past nine, and a few minutes later the bell rang for Lord Sellington’s valet, who went up to assist his master to bed.

===Chinese===
那天晚上别处发生的事，最好用证人席上的平实话来讲。同里德先生晤谈后，塞林顿勋爵带着一身发热的重感冒回了家；据其秘书证言，他本有意推迟与侄子约定的那次会面。电话打到了卡林先生的旅馆，人却不在。到九点为止，勋爵一直在料理他那一摊子慈善事业的事务，拉萨德先生在旁伺候。塞林顿勋爵在一间小书房里办公，书房由他的卧室开出。

九点一刻，卡林到了，由管家引上楼去；这位管家后来证称，听见楼上有怒气冲冲的说话声。九点钟声敲响时，卡林先生下楼，被送出门；几分钟后，铃声响起来，唤塞林顿勋爵的贴身男仆上楼，伺候主人就寝。

===Original===
At half-past seven the next morning, the valet, who slept in an adjoining apartment, went into his master’s room to take him a cup of tea. He found his employer lying face downward on the floor; he was dead, and had been dead for some hours. There was no sign of wounds, and at first glance it looked as though this man of sixty had collapsed in the night. But there were circumstances which pointed to some unusual happening. In Lord Sellington’s bedroom was a small steel wall-safe, and the first thing the valet noticed was that this was open, papers were lying on the floor, and that in the grate was a heap of paper which, except for one corner, was entirely burnt.

The valet telephoned immediately for the doctor and for the police, and from that moment the case went out of Mr. Reeder’s able hands.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨七点半，睡在隔壁房间的男仆端着一杯茶进主人房去。他发现雇主脸朝下趴在地上；人已经死了，且已死去好几个钟头。身上不见伤痕，乍一看，仿佛这位六十岁的人是夜里突发急症倒下的。可有些情形指明此非寻常变故。塞林顿勋爵卧室里有一只小型钢制壁式保险柜，男仆头一件注意到的是：柜门开着，纸张散在地上，炉格里有一堆烧掉的纸，只剩一角没烧尽。

男仆立即打电话请医生、报警察；从那一刻起，这桩案子便脱离了里德先生那双干练的手。

===Original===
Later that morning he reported briefly to his superior the result of his inquiries.

“Murder, I am afraid,” he said sadly. “The Home Office pathologist is perfectly certain that it is a case of aconitine poisoning. The paper in the hearth has been photographed, and there is no doubt whatever that the burnt document is the will by which Lord Sellington left all his property to various charitable institutions.”

He paused here.

“Well?” asked his chief, “what does that mean?”

Mr. Reeder coughed.

“It means that if this will cannot be proved, and I doubt whether it can, his lordship died intestate. The property goes with the title—”

===Chinese===
当天上午晚些时候，他把查证结果向上司作了简报。

“只怕是谋杀。”他悲伤地说，“内政部的病理学家完全确定，这是乌头碱中毒。炉膛里的纸已拍了照，烧掉的那份文件毫无疑问是塞林顿勋爵的遗嘱——他凭它把全部财产遗赠给了各家慈善机构。”

说到这里他停了一停。

“那么？”上司问，“这意味着什么？”

里德先生咳了一声。

“这意味着，这份遗嘱若无法被认证——我怀疑认证得了——勋爵便是身后无嘱。财产随头衔一并传下去——”

===Original===
“To Carlin?” asked the startled Prosecutor.

Mr. Reeder nodded.

“There were other things burnt; four small oblong slips of paper, which had evidently been fastened together by a pin. These are quite indecipherable.” He sighed again. The Public Prosecutor looked up.

“You haven’t mentioned the letter that arrived by district messenger after Lord Sellington had retired for the night.”

Mr. Reeder rubbed his chin.

“No, I didn’t mention that,” he said reluctantly.

“Has it been found?”

Mr. Reeder hesitated.

===Chinese===
“传给卡林？”检察总长愕然问。

里德先生点点头。

“烧掉的还有别的东西；四张小小的长方形纸片，显见原先用一枚别针别在一起。这些已全然无法辨认。”他又叹了一声。检察总长抬起头来。

“您还没提那封信——塞林顿勋爵就寝之后，由本地信差送到的。”

里德先生揉了揉下巴。

“对，我没提。”他不情愿地说。

“找着了吗？”

里德先生迟疑了一下。

===Original===
“I don’t know. I rather think that it has not been,” he said.

“Would it throw any light upon the crime, do you think?”

Mr. Reeder scratched his chin with some sign of embarrassment.

“I should think it might,” he said. “Will you excuse me, sir? Inspector Salter is waiting for me.” And he was out of the room before the Prosecutor could frame any further inquiry.

Inspector Salter was striding impatiently up and down the little room when Mr. Reeder came back. They left the building together. The car that was waiting for them brought them to Jermyn Street in a few minutes. Outside the flat three plainclothes men were waiting, evidently for the arrival of their chief, and the Inspector passed into the building, followed closely by Mr. Reeder. They were halfway up the stairs when Reeder asked:

===Chinese===
“不知道。我倒觉得没有。”他说。

“您说，它对破案能有所启发吗？”

里德先生挠着下巴，颇有点窘。

“我想是能的。”他说，“失陪了，先生？索尔特探长还在等我。”不等检察总长组织出下一句盘问，他已出了房门。

里德先生回来时，索尔特探长（Inspector Salter）正在小屋里焦躁地踱来踱去。两人一同出了大楼。候着他们的汽车几分钟便把他们送到杰敏街。公寓门外候着三个便衣，显然在等他们头儿驾到；探长走进楼去，里德先生紧随在后。上楼到一半，里德问：

===Original===
“Does Carlin know you?”

“He ought to,” was the grim reply. “I did my best to get him penal servitude before he skipped from England.”

“Humph!” said Mr. Reeder. “I’m sorry he knows you.”

“Why?” The Inspector stopped on the stairs to ask the question.

“Because he saw us getting out of the cab. I caught sight of his face, and—”

He stopped suddenly. The sound of a shot thundered through the house, and in another second the Inspector was racing up the stairs two at a time and had burst into the suite which Carlin occupied.

===Chinese===
“卡林认得你吗？”

“应该认得。”回答冷冰冰的，“他逃出英国之前，我尽了力想送他去服苦役。”

“哼！”里德先生说，“他认得你，可惜了。”

“为什么？”探长在楼梯上停住脚问。

“因为他瞧见我们下车了。我瞥见了他的脸，而且——”

他猛地收了声。一声枪响震彻整栋楼；转眼间探长已两级一跨地冲上楼梯，撞进了卡林住的那套房间。

===Original===
A glimpse of the prostrate figure told them they were too late. The Inspector bent over the dead man.

“That has saved the country the cost of a murder trial,” he said.

“I think not,” said Mr. Reeder gently, and explained his reasons.

Half an hour later, as Mr. Lassard walked out of his office, a detective tapped him on the shoulder.

“Your name is Eiter,” he said, “and I want you for murder.”

---

===Chinese===
一眼看见地上趴着的人影，他们便知来迟了。探长俯身看着死人。

“这倒给国家省了一场谋杀案的庭审费。”他说。

“我看没有。”里德先生温和地说，随即讲明缘由。

半小时后，拉萨德先生走出办公室，一个侦探在他肩上拍了一下。

“你叫艾特。”他说，“你因谋杀被捕了。”

---

===Original===
“It was a very simple case really, sir,” explained Mr. Reeder to his chief. “Eiter, of course, was known to me personally, but I remembered especially that he could not spell the word ‘able,’ and I recognised this peculiarity in our friend the moment I saw the letter which he wrote to his patron asking for the money. It was Eiter himself who drew the five thousand pounds; of that I am convinced. The man is, and always has been, an inveterate gambler, and I did not have to make many inquiries before I discovered that he was owing a large sum of money and that one bookmaker had threatened to bring him before Tattersall’s Committee unless he paid. That would have meant the end of Mr. Lassard, the philanthropic custodian of children. Which, by the way, was always Eiter’s role. He ran bogus charitable societies—it is extraordinarily easy to find dupes who are willing to subscribe for philanthropic objects. Many years ago, when I was a young man, I was instrumental in getting him seven years. I’d lost sight of him since then until I saw the letter he sent to Lord Sellington. Unfortunately for him, one line ran: ‘I shall be glad if you are abel to let my messenger have the money’—and he spelt ‘able’ in the Eiter way. I called on him and made sure. And then I wrote to his lordship, who apparently did not open the letter till late that night.

“Eiter had called on him earlier in the evening and had had a long talk with him. I only surmise that Lord Sellington had expressed a doubt as to whether he ought to leave his nephew penniless, scoundrel though he was; and Eiter was terrified that his scheme for getting possession of the old man’s money was in danger of failing. Moreover, my appearance in the case had scared him. He decided to kill Lord Sellington that night, took aconitine with him to the house and introduced it into the medicine, a bottle of which always stood on Sellington’s desk. Whether the old man destroyed the will which disinherited his nephew before he discovered he had been poisoned, or whether he did it after, we shall never know. When I had satisfied myself that Lassard was Eiter, I sent a letter by special messenger to Stratford Place—”

===Chinese===
“这案子其实再简单不过，先生。”里德先生向上司解释道，“艾特这个人我本来就认识，尤其记得他不会拼‘able’这个词；他写信向赞助人要钱，那信我一看，就在咱们这位朋友身上认出了这个怪癖。取走那五千英镑的正是艾特本人；这一点我深信不疑。此人是个积习难改的赌棍，且一向如此。我没费几番查证就查明：他欠着一大笔赌账，有个赌注登记人放话，他再不还钱，就把他告到塔特索尔委员会。那便意味着拉萨德先生——儿童们那位慈善的监护人——的完蛋。顺便说一句，这本来就是艾特一贯的角色。他开的全是冒牌慈善会——要找乐意给慈善事业掏钱的冤大头，容易得出奇。许多年前我年轻的时候，就亲手送他吃过七年官司。打那以后我把他忘在了脑后，直到看见他寄给塞林顿勋爵的那封信。他不幸就倒霉在一行字上：‘您若 abel（能够）让来人取走此款，我将不胜感激’——他把‘able’拼成了艾特式的拼法。我登门拜访，验实了。随后我给勋爵大人写了一封信，而他显然直到深夜才拆看。

“那天傍晚早些时候，艾特拜访过他，同他长谈了一场。我只是揣测：塞林顿勋爵言辞间流露了犹豫，拿不准该不该让侄子一文不名，哪怕他是个恶棍；艾特便吓坏了，生怕他那套谋夺老家伙钱财的计策功亏一篑。再说，我插手此案也惊着了他。他决定当夜就杀死塞林顿勋爵，带着乌头碱进了宅子，把它掺进了药里——那瓶药长年立在塞林顿的书桌上。老人发觉中毒之前烧掉了那份剥夺侄子继承权的遗嘱，还是发觉之后才烧的，我们永远无从知晓了。待我确信拉萨德就是艾特，我便派专差送了一封信到斯特拉福德坊——”

===Original===
“That was the letter delivered by special messenger?”

Mr. Reeder nodded.

“It is possible that Sellington was already under the influence of the drug when he burnt the will, and burnt too the four bills which Carlin had forged and which the old man had held over his head as a threat. Carlin may have known his uncle was dead; he certainly recognised the Inspector when he stepped out of the cab, and, thinking he was to be arrested for forgery, shot himself.”

Mr. Reeder pursed his lips and his melancholy face grew longer.

“I wish I had never known Mrs. Carlin—my acquaintance with her introduces that element of coincidence which is permissible in stories but is so distressing in actual life. It shakes one’s confidence in the logic of things.”

===Chinese===
“就是那封专差送到的信？”

里德先生点点头。

“塞林顿烧遗嘱的时候，毒性可能已经发作了；他连那四张票据也一并烧了——那是卡林伪造的，一直被老人攥在手里，当威胁他的把柄。卡林也许已经知道伯父死了；我们下车的时候，他干真万确认出了探长，自以为伪造票据的逮捕临头，便开枪自尽了。”

里德先生抿起嘴唇，那张忧郁的脸拉得更长了。

“我但愿从不曾认识卡林太太——与她相识，给这案子添进了巧合的成分。巧合在小说里尚可容许，在现实生活里却令人苦恼。它动摇了一个人对世间万物皆有逻辑的信心。”

## The Treasure Hunt / 寻宝

===Original===
There is a tradition in criminal circles that even the humblest of detective officers is a man of wealth and substance, and that his secret hoard was secured by thieving, bribery and blackmail. It is the gossip of the fields, the quarries, the tailor’s shop, the laundry and the bakehouse of fifty county prisons and three convict establishments, that all highly placed detectives have by nefarious means laid up for themselves sufficient earthly treasures to make work a hobby and their official pittance the most inconsiderable portion of their incomes.

Since Mr. J. G. Reeder had for over twenty years dealt exclusively with bank robbers and forgers, who are the aristocrats and capitalists of the underworld, legend credited him with country houses and immense secret reserves. Not that he would have a great deal of money in the bank. It was admitted that he was too clever to risk discovery by the authorities. No, it was hidden somewhere: it was the pet dream of hundreds of unlawful men that they would some day discover the hoard and live happily ever after. The one satisfactory aspect of his affluence (they all agreed) was that, being an old man—he was over fifty—he couldn’t take his money with him, for gold melts at a certain temperature and gilt-edged stock is seldom printed on asbestos paper.

The Director of Public Prosecutions was lunching one Saturday at his club with a judge of the King’s Bench—Saturday being one of the two days in the week when a judge gets properly fed. And the conversation drifted to a certain Mr. J. G. Reeder, the chief of the Director’s sleuths.

===Chinese===
罪犯圈子里流传着一个老话：哪怕最不起眼的侦探官员，也是个有钱有产业的人物，他那笔秘密积蓄是靠偷窃、贿赂和讹诈攒下的。这是五十座郡立监狱和三处苦役监里的田野、采石场、裁缝铺、洗衣房和面包房共同的闲话题材：凡是地位高的侦探，都靠歪门邪道给自己攒下了足够的俗世财宝，差事不过是消遣，官家发的那点薪水在他们收入里是最不值一提的零头。

由于J. G. 里德先生二十多年来专跟抢银行的和造假钞的打交道——这号人是黑社会里的贵族和资本家——传说便给他派上了乡间宅邸和巨额的秘密储备。倒不是说他会在银行里存下大笔的钱。大家都承认，他精明得很，绝不会冒被当局察觉的风险。不，钱藏在某个地方：数以百计的不法之徒都做着同一个美梦，盼望有朝一日找到那笔藏金，从此幸福到老。他的富足只有一处令人宽慰（他们一致同意）：他已经是老头子了——年过五十——死了也带不走，金子到了一定温度会熔化，而金边股票很少印在石棉纸上。

一个星期六，检察总长在俱乐部里同一位王座法庭的法官共进午餐——星期六是一星期里法官能吃上像样饭食的两天之一。话头不知不觉转到了检察总长手下头号密探J. G. 里德先生身上。

===Original===
“He’s capable,” he confessed reluctantly, “but I hate his hat. It is the sort that So-and-so used to wear,” he mentioned by name an eminent politician; “and I loathe his black frock-coat, people who see him coming into the office think he’s a coroner’s officer, but he’s capable. His side-whiskers are an abomination, and I have a feeling that, if I talked rough to him, he would burst into tears—a gentle soul. Almost too gentle for my kind of work. He apologises to the messenger every time he rings for him!”

The judge, who knew something about humanity, answered with a frosty smile.

“He sounds rather like a potential murderer to me,” he said cynically.

===Chinese===
“他是有本事，”他老大不情愿地承认，“可我讨厌他的帽子。就是某某人从前戴的那种，”他点了一位显赫政客的名；“我还讨厌他那件黑礼服大衣，看见他进办公室的人都当他是验尸官的手下——可他确实有本事。他那副连鬓胡子更是造孽，而且我总觉得，我要是跟他说话粗一点，他准会当场哭出来——一个温柔的灵魂。干我这一行，温柔得几乎过了头。他每次按铃叫听差，都要跟人家道歉！”

那位对人性颇有些见识的法官报以冷冰冰的一笑。

“依我看，这人倒像个还没动手的杀人犯。”他讥诮地说。

===Original===
Milord, in his extravagance, he did Mr. J. G. Reeder an injustice, for Mr. Reeder was capable of breaking the law—quite. At the same time there were many people who formed an altogether wrong conception of J. G.’s harmlessness as an individual. And one of these was a certain Lew Kohl, who mixed banknote printing with elementary burglary.

Threatened men live long, a trite saying but, like most things trite, true. In a score of cases, when Mr. J. G. Reeder had descended from the witness stand, he had met the baleful eye of the man in the dock and had listened with mild interest to divers promises as to what would happen to him in the near or the remote future. For he was a great authority on forged banknotes and he had sent many men to penal servitude.

Mr. Reeder, that inoffensive man, had seen prisoners foaming at the mouth in their rage, he had seen them white and livid, he had heard their howling execrations and he had met these men after their release from prison and had found them amiable souls half ashamed and half amused at their nearly forgotten outbursts and horrific threats.

===Chinese===
法官大人这话固然说得铺张，可也确实冤枉了J. G. 里德先生，因为里德先生干得出犯法的事——完完全全干得出来。与此同时，也有许多人把J. G. 这个人的无害想得完全岔了。其中一个便是卢·科尔（Lew Kohl），此人一手印钞票，一手干些入门级的夜盗营生。

受人威胁的人活得长——一句老生常谈，但老生常谈多半是真话。不下二十桩案子里，J. G. 里德先生走下证人席时，都迎上过被告席上那人恶狠狠的目光，也带着浅浅的兴味听过各种各样的许诺：不远的将来或遥远的未来，会怎样收拾他。因为他是伪钞案的权威，送过许多人去服苦役。

里德先生，这个与人为善的人，见过囚犯气得口吐白沫，见过他们脸色煞白铁青，听过他们嚎叫着咒骂；也在这些人出狱后再见到他们，发现他们都是和气的性子，对自己差不多忘掉的暴怒和凶狠的威胁，一半害臊，一半好笑。

===Original===
But when, in the early part of 1914, Lew Kohl was sentenced for ten years, he neither screamed his imprecations nor registered a vow to tear Mr. Reeder’s heart, lungs and important organs from his frail body.

Lew just smiled and his eyes caught the detective’s for the space of a second—the forger’s eyes were pale blue and speculative, and they held neither hate nor fury. Instead, they said in so many words:

“At the first opportunity I will kill you.”

Mr. Reeder read the message and sighed heavily, for he disliked fuss of all kinds, and resented, in so far as he could resent anything, the injustice of being made personally responsible for the performance of a public duty.

===Chinese===
可是在 1914 年初，卢·科尔被判十年监禁时，他既没有尖声咒骂，也没有立誓要把里德先生的心、肺和几处要紧器官从那具单薄的身体里扯出来。

卢只是笑了笑，目光与侦探相接了一秒钟——伪造犯的眼睛是淡蓝色的，带着盘算，里面没有恨，也没有怒。那双眼睛等于明明白白地说：

“一有机会，我就宰了你。”

里德先生读懂了这个信号，重重叹了口气，因为他厌恶一切形式的折腾，而且就他这种人而言，已经算相当恼火了：履行公职，竟要他个人来担仇怨，未免不公。

===Original===
Many years had passed, and considerable changes had occurred in Mr. Reeder’s fortune. He had transferred from the specialised occupation of detecting the makers of forged banknotes to the more general practice of the Public Prosecutor’s bureau, but he never forgot Lew’s smile.

The work in Whitehall was not heavy and it was very interesting. To Mr. Reeder came most of the anonymous letters which the Director received in shoals. In the main they were self-explanatory, and it required no particular intelligence to discover their motive. Jealousy, malice, plain mischief-making, and occasionally a sordid desire to benefit financially by the information which was conveyed, were behind the majority. But occasionally:

===Chinese===
许多年过去了，里德先生的际遇也发生了不小的变化。他从侦缉伪钞制造者这个专门行当，调到了检察总长公署更宽泛的业务里，但他从未忘记卢的那一笑。

白厅的公务不算繁重，而且很有意思。检察总长成堆收到的匿名信，大多转到里德先生手上。这些信大都不言自明，用不着多聪明就能看出动机：多数背后是嫉妒、恶意、纯粹使坏，偶尔也夹着借告密捞一笔钱的龌龊心思。不过偶尔也有：

===Original===
> “Sir James is going to marry his cousin, and it’s not three months since his poor wife fell overboard from the Channel steamer crossing to Calais. There’s something very fishy about this business. Miss Margaret doesn’t like him, for she knows he’s after her money. Why was I sent away to London that night? He doesn’t like driving in the dark, either. It’s strange that he wanted to drive that night when it was raining like blazes.”

This particular letter was signed “A Friend.” Justice has many such friends.

“Sir James” was Sir James Tithermite, who had been a director of some new public department during the war and had received a baronetcy for his services.

===Chinese===
> “詹姆斯爵士要娶他的表妹了，而他那位可怜的妻子从渡轮上落水，如今还不满三个月——那趟船是横渡去加来的。这事透着蹊跷。玛格丽特小姐不喜欢他，因为她知道他盯的是她的钱。那天夜里为什么偏偏把我打发去伦敦？他又不喜欢摸黑开车。偏偏挑那样一个雨下得发疯的夜里开车，真是奇怪。”

这封信用的是“一位朋友”的落款。正义就有许许多多这样的朋友。

“詹姆斯爵士”便是詹姆斯·蒂瑟米特爵士（Sir James Tithermite），战时在某个新设的官署当过董事，因功受封从男爵。

===Original===
“Look it up,” said the Director when he saw the letter. “I seem to remember that Lady Tithermite was drowned at sea.”

“On the nineteenth of December last year,” said Mr. Reeder solemnly. “She and Sir James were going to Monte Carlo, breaking their journey in Paris. Sir James, who has a house near Maidstone, drove to Dover, garaging the car at the Lord Wilson Hotel. The night was stormy and the ship had a rough crossing—they were halfway across when Sir James came to the purser and said that he had missed his wife. Her baggage was in the cabin, her passport, rail ticket and hat, but the lady was not found, indeed was never seen again.”

The Director nodded.

===Chinese===
“查一查，”检察总长看到信时说，“我好像记得蒂瑟米特爵士夫人是在海上淹死的。”

“去年 12 月 19 日，”里德先生郑重地说，“她同詹姆斯爵士要去蒙特卡洛，中途在巴黎停留。詹姆斯爵士在梅德斯通附近有宅子，他把车开到多佛，寄存在威尔逊勋爵酒店。那晚风雨大作，船横渡时颠簸得厉害——船行到半路，詹姆斯爵士找到事务长，说妻子不见了。她的行李都在舱里，护照、火车票和帽子也在，可这位夫人没找到，事实上从此再没人见过她。”

检察总长点了点头。

===Original===
“I see, you’ve read up the case.”

“I remember it,” said Mr. Reeder. “The case is a favourite speculation of mine. Unfortunately I see evil in everything and I have often thought how easy—but I fear that I take a warped view of life. It is a horrible handicap to possess a criminal mind.”

The Director looked at him suspiciously. He was never quite sure whether Mr. Reeder was serious. At that moment, his sobriety was beyond challenge.

===Chinese===
“明白了，你把案卷研究过了。”

“我记得这案子，”里德先生说，“它是我最爱琢磨的一桩悬想。不幸的是，我看什么都看得见罪恶，我常常想，这事办起来多么容易——只怕是我看人生的角度已经歪了。天生一副犯罪头脑，实在是个可怕的累赘。”

检察总长狐疑地看着他。他从来拿不准里德先生是不是当真。至少此时此刻，他的严肃无可置疑。

===Original===
“A discharged chauffeur wrote that letter, of course,” he began.

“Thomas Dayford, of 179, Barrack Street, Maidstone,” concluded Mr. Reeder. “He is at present in the employ of the Kent Motorbus Company, and has three children, two of whom are twins and bonny little rascals.”

The Chief laughed helplessly.

===Chinese===
“这信当然是哪个被辞退的司机写的。”他开口道。

“梅德斯通兵营街 179 号的托马斯·戴福德，”里德先生接了下文，“他如今在肯特汽车公司做事，有三个孩子，其中两个是双胞胎，虎头虎脑的小捣蛋。”

总监无可奈何地笑了。

===Original===
“I’ll take it that you know!” he said. “See what there is behind the letter. Sir James is a big fellow in Kent, a Justice of the Peace, and he has powerful political influences. There is nothing in this letter, of course. Go warily, Reeder—if any kick comes back to this office, it goes on to you—intensified!”

Mr. Reeder’s idea of walking warily was peculiarly his own. He travelled down to Maidstone the next morning, and, finding a bus that passed the lodge gates of Elfreda Manor, he journeyed comfortably and economically, his umbrella between his knees. He passed through the lodge gates, up a long and winding avenue of poplars, and presently came within sight of the grey manor house.

In a deep chair on the lawn he saw a girl sitting, a book on her knees, and evidently she saw him, for she rose as he crossed the lawn and came towards him eagerly.

===Chinese===
“就算你知道了吧！”他说，“看看这信背后有什么。詹姆斯爵士在肯特是个有头脸的人物，治安法官，政界的关系盘根错节。这信当然什么都不是。放谨慎些，里德——这公署要是挨了回踢，一脚全踹回你身上——还得加码！”

里德先生对“放谨慎些”的理解，自有他独到的一套。第二天上午他去了梅德斯通，找到一辆经过埃尔弗雷达庄园（Elfreda Manor）门房的班车，一路坐得又舒适又省钱，雨伞夹在两膝之间。他穿过门房大门，沿一条长长的白杨夹道的弯路走上去，不多时便望见了那栋灰色的庄园宅邸。

草坪上一张深深的椅子里坐着一位姑娘，膝上摊着本书；显然她也瞧见了他，因为他一走上草坪她就起身，急切地迎上前来。

===Original===
“I’m Miss Margaret Letherby—are you from—?” She mentioned the name of a well-known firm of lawyers, and her face fell when Mr. Reeder regretfully disclaimed connection with those legal lights.

She was as pretty as a perfect complexion and a round, not too intellectual, face could, in combination, make her.

“I thought—do you wish to see Sir James? He is in the library. If you ring, one of the maids will take you to him.”

===Chinese===
“我是玛格丽特·莱瑟比（Margaret Letherby）小姐——您是……哪来的？”她说出一家知名律师事务所的名号，里德先生遗憾地表示自己与那些法学名流毫无干系，她的脸顿时垮了下来。

她的漂亮，是无瑕的肤色和一张圆圆的、不显聪明的脸蛋加在一起所能达到的极致。

“我还以为——您想见詹姆斯爵士？他在书房。您要是按铃，女仆会领您去。”

===Original===
Had Mr. Reeder been the sort of man who could be puzzled by anything, he would have been puzzled by the suggestion that any girl with money of her own should marry a man much older than herself against her own wishes. There was little mystery in the matter now. Miss Margaret would have married any strong-willed man who insisted.

“Even me,” said Mr. Reeder to himself, with a certain melancholy pleasure.

There was no need to ring the bell. A tall, broad man in a golfing suit stood in the doorway. His fair hair was long and hung over his forehead in a thick flat strand; a heavy tawny moustache hid his mouth and swept down over a chin that was long and powerful.

===Chinese===
倘若里德先生是那种会被任何事情难住的人，眼前这事准叫他纳闷：一位自有钱财的姑娘，怎么会违着自己的心愿，去嫁一个大她许多的男人。如今此事已无多少玄妙。只要是意志坚决、非要不可的男人，玛格丽特小姐都会嫁。

“连我都行。”里德先生对自己说，带着某种忧郁的快意。

铃是用不着按的。门口已经站着一个穿高尔夫球装的高大宽阔的男人。他的金发很长，厚厚的一条平平地垂在额前；浓密的黄胡子遮住嘴，一直扫到那长而有力的下巴上。

===Original===
“Well?” he asked aggressively.

“I’m from the Public Prosecutor’s office,” murmured Mr. Reeder. “I have had an anonymous letter.”

His pale eyes did not leave the face of the other man.

===Chinese===
“什么事？”他咄咄逼人地问。

“我来自检察总长公署，”里德先生低声道，“我收到了一封匿名信。”

他浅色的眼睛一眨不眨地盯着对方的脸。

===Original===
“Come in,” said Sir James gruffly. As he closed the door he glanced quickly first to the girl and then to the poplar avenue. “I’m expecting a fool of a lawyer,” he said, as he flung open the door of what was evidently the library.

His voice was steady; not by a flicker of eyelash had he betrayed the slightest degree of anxiety when Reeder had told his mission.

“Well—what about this anonymous letter? You don’t take much notice of that kind of trash, do you?”

===Chinese===
“进来。”詹姆斯爵士粗声说。关门时他飞快地先瞟了一眼姑娘，又瞟了一眼白杨夹道。“我在等一个蠢律师。”他推开一扇显然是书房的门，说道。

他的声音平稳；里德说明来意时，他连睫毛都没颤一下，没有露出丝毫慌乱。

“唔——那封匿名信怎么说？这种废物你们不会太当回事吧？”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder deposited his umbrella and flat-crowned hat on a chair before he took a document from his pocket and handed it to the baronet, who frowned as he read. Was it Mr. Reeder’s vivid imagination, or did the hard light in the eyes of Sir James soften as he read?

“This is a cock and bull story of somebody having seen my wife’s jewellery on sale in Paris,” he said. “There is nothing in it. I can account for every one of my poor wife’s trinkets. I brought back the jewel case after that awful night. I don’t recognise the handwriting: who is the lying scoundrel who wrote this?”

Mr. Reeder had never before been called a lying scoundrel, but he accepted the experience with admirable meekness.

===Chinese===
里德先生先把雨伞和平顶帽搁在椅子上，才从口袋里取出一页文书递给从男爵；后者读着，皱起了眉头。是里德先生想象力太活跃，还是詹姆斯爵士眼里的冷光在读信时当真柔和了一瞬？

“这是个荒诞故事，说有人在巴黎见到我妻子的珠宝出售，”他说，“纯属子虚乌有。我那可怜妻子的每一件小首饰，我都能说出去向。那可怕的夜晚过后，我把首饰匣带回来了。这笔迹我不认得：写这东西的撒谎恶棍是谁？”

里德先生生平头一遭被人骂作撒谎的恶棍，但他以令人叹服的温顺领受了这一体验。

===Original===
“I thought it untrue,” he said, shaking his head. “I followed the details of the case very thoroughly. You left here in the afternoon—”

“At night,” said the other brusquely. He was not inclined to discuss the matter, but Mr. Reeder’s appealing look was irresistible. “It is only eighty minutes’ run to Dover. We got to the pier at eleven o’clock, about the same time as the boat train, and we went on board at once. I got my cabin key from the purser and put her ladyship and her baggage inside.”

“Her ladyship was a good sailor?”

===Chinese===
“我也认为不实，”他摇着头说，“这案子的细节我追得极细。你们是下午离开这里的——”

“是夜里，”对方粗暴地说。他本不想谈这件事，可里德先生那恳切的目光叫人无法拒绝。“到多佛不过八十分钟的车程。我们十一点到码头，跟船务火车差不多同时，立刻就上了船。我从事务长那儿拿到舱位钥匙，把夫人和她的行李都安置进去。”

“夫人不晕船？”

===Original===
“Yes, a very good sailor; she was remarkably well that night. I left her in the cabin dozing, and went for a stroll on the deck—”

“Raining very heavily and a strong sea running,” nodded Reeder, as though in agreement with something the other man had said.

“Yes—I’m a pretty good sailor—anyway, that story about my poor wife’s jewels is utter nonsense. You can tell the Director that, with my compliments.”

===Chinese===
“不晕，从来不晕；那天夜里她精神好得出奇。我把她留在舱里打盹，自己到甲板上遛弯去了——”

“雨下得正大，浪头也高。”里德点头附和，仿佛对方方才正是这么说的。

“是啊——我也经得起风浪——总之，关于我可怜妻子的珠宝那些话全是胡说。请代我向检察总长转达此意，并附上我的敬意。”

===Original===
He opened the door for his visitor, and Mr. Reeder was some time replacing the letter and gathering his belongings.

“You have a beautiful place here. Sir James—a lovely place. An extensive estate?”

“Three thousand acres.” This time he did not attempt to disguise his impatience. “Good afternoon.”

===Chinese===
他替访客开了门，里德先生慢条斯理地收好信件、拾掇行头，颇费了一点工夫。

“您这儿真美，詹姆斯爵士——可爱的地方。产业很广吧？”

“三千英亩。”这一回他索性不再掩饰不耐烦，“午安。”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder went slowly down the drive, his remarkable memory at work.

He missed the bus which he could easily have caught, and pursued an apparently aimless way along the winding road which marched with the boundaries of the baronet’s property. A walk of a quarter of a mile brought him to a lane shooting off at right angles from the main road, and marking, he guessed, the southern boundary. At the corner stood an old stone lodge, on the inside of a forbidding iron gate. The lodge was in a pitiable state of neglect and disrepair. Tiles had been dislodged from the roof, the windows were grimy or broken, and the little garden was overrun with docks and thistles. Beyond the gate was a narrow, weed-covered drive that trailed out of sight into a distant plantation.

Hearing the clang of a letter-box closing, he turned to see a postman mounting his bicycle.

===Chinese===
里德先生沿着车道缓步而下，他那过人的记性开动了。

他错过了本来完全赶得上的班车，顺着与从男爵产业边界并行的那条蜿蜒大路，摆出一副漫无目的的样子走去。走了四分之一英里，一条小巷从大路直角岔出，他猜那便是南面的地界。巷口立着一座旧石砌门房，在一道冷森森的铁门内侧。门房久无人居、破败失修，景象凄凉：屋顶的瓦片翻落，窗玻璃不是积满污垢就是碎了，小园子里酸模和蓟草疯长。铁门之内是一条窄车道，杂草丛生，蜿蜒隐没进远处的树影里。

听见信箱合上的哐当声，他回头看见一个邮差正跨上自行车。

===Original===
“What place is this?” asked Mr. Reeder, arresting the postman’s departure.

“South Lodge—Sir James Tithermite’s property. It’s never used now. Hasn’t been used for years—I don’t know why; it’s a shortcut if they happen to be coming this way.”

Mr. Reeder walked with him towards the village, and he was a skilful pumper of wells, however dry; and the postman was not dry by any means.

===Chinese===
“这是什么地方？”里德先生拦住正要走的邮差。

“南门房——詹姆斯·蒂瑟米特爵士的产业。如今不用了，好些年没用过——不知为什么；他们要是打这边来，走这儿倒是个近道。”

里德先生陪他往村子方向走。不管井多深多干，他都是个套话的好手；何况这位邮差的井一点也不干。

===Original===
“Yes, poor lady! She was very frail—one of those sort of invalids that last out many a healthy man.”

Mr. Reeder put a question at random and scored most unexpectedly.

“Yes, her ladyship was a bad sailor. I know because every time she went abroad she used to get a bottle of that stuff people take for seasickness. I’ve delivered many a bottle till Raikes the chemist stocked it—‘Pickers’ Travellers’ Friend,’ that’s what it was called. Mr. Raikes was only saying to me the other day that he’d got half a dozen bottles on hand and he didn’t know what to do with them. Nobody in Climbury ever goes to sea.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，可怜的夫人！她身子骨弱得很——就是那种偏偏能熬倒多少健康人的病秧子。”

里德先生随口提了一个问题，却出人意料地大有所获。

“没错，夫人晕船晕得厉害。我知道，因为她每回出国，都要买一瓶人家治晕船吃的那种药水。药剂师雷克斯（Raikes）自己进货之前，一瓶瓶都是我送的——‘皮克斯牌旅行之友’，就叫这个名。雷克斯先生前两天还跟我说，他手里压了半打瓶子，不知怎么打发。克利姆伯里这地方，谁也不出海。”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder went on to the village and idled his precious time in most unlikely places. At the chemist’s, at the blacksmith’s shop, at the modest building yard. He caught the last bus back to Maidstone, and by great good luck the last train to London.

And, in his vague way, he answered the Director’s query the next day with:

“Yes, I saw Sir James: a very interesting man.”

===Chinese===
里德先生继续往村里去，把他宝贵的光阴消磨在顶不像样的一些地方：药房、铁匠铺，还有那家不起眼的建材场。他赶上末班汽车回到梅德斯通，又运气极好地赶上了回伦敦的末班火车。

第二天，他以他惯常那种含糊其辞的方式，这样回答检察总长的询问：

“见了，我见到詹姆斯爵士了：一个很有意思的人。”

===Original===
This was on the Friday. All day Saturday he was busy. The Sabbath brought him a new interest.

On this bright Sunday morning, Mr. Reeder, attired in a flowered dressing-gown, his feet encased in black velvet slippers, stood at the window of his house in Brockley Road and surveyed the deserted thoroughfare. The bell of a local church, which was accounted high, had rung for early Mass, and there was nothing living in sight except a black cat that lay asleep in a patch of sunlight on the top step of the house opposite. The hour was 7:30, and Mr. Reeder had been at his desk since six, working by artificial light, the month being March towards the close.

From the half-moon of the window bay he regarded a section of the Lewisham High Road and as much of Tanners Hill as can be seen before it dips past the railway bridge into sheer Deptford.

===Chinese===
这是星期五的事。星期六他忙了一整天。礼拜日给他带来了新的乐趣。

这个晴朗的礼拜日早晨，里德先生穿着印花晨衣，脚上套着黑丝绒拖鞋，站在布罗克利路自家的窗前，打量空荡荡的街面。本地一座教堂的钟素以音色高亢闻名，已经敲过早弥撒；放眼望去，只有对面房子最高一级台阶上，一只黑猫躺在阳光里酣睡。此时是 7:30，里德先生从六点起就伏在案头，靠灯光工作——时令已近三月底了。

从凸窗的半圆里，他望着刘易舍姆大街的一段，还有坦纳斯山能望见的那一截——过了铁路桥，它便一头扎进纯粹的德普特福德去了。

===Original===
Returning to his table, he opened a carton of the cheapest cigarettes and, lighting one, puffed in an amateurish fashion. He smoked cigarettes rather like a woman who detests them but feels that it is the correct thing to do.

“Dear me,” said Mr. Reeder feebly.

He was back at the window, and he had seen a man turn out of Lewisham High Road. He had crossed the road and was coming straight to Daffodil House—which frolicsome name appeared on the doorposts of Mr. Reeder’s residence. A tall, straight man, with a sombre brown face, he came to the front gate, passed through and beyond the watcher’s range of vision.

===Chinese===
回到桌前，他拆开一盒最廉价的纸烟，点上一支，外行似的喷云吐雾。他抽烟的样子，很像一个厌恶香烟、却觉得不抽有失体统的女人。

“天哪。”里德先生有气无力地说。

他又回到窗前，看见一个男人从刘易舍姆大街拐了进来。那人穿过马路，直奔水仙楼（Daffodil House）——这个俏皮的名字就刻在里德先生宅子的门柱上。来人高而笔挺，一张阴沉的棕脸，走到前门，进了院子，出了守望者的视野。

===Original===
“Dear me!” said Mr. Reeder, as he heard the tinkle of a bell.

A few minutes later his housekeeper tapped on the door.

“Will you see Mr. Kohl, sir?” she asked.

===Chinese===
“天哪！”里德先生听见门铃叮的一响。

几分钟后，管家敲了敲门。

“科尔先生您见吗，先生？”她问。

===Original===
Mr. J. G. Reeder nodded.

Lew Kohl walked into the room to find a middle-aged man in a flamboyant dressing-gown sitting at his desk, a pair of pince-nez set crookedly on his nose.

“Good morning, Kohl.”

===Chinese===
J. G. 里德先生点了点头。

卢·科尔走进屋来，只见一个中年人穿着件花哨的晨衣坐在案后，鼻梁上架着一副夹鼻眼镜，歪的。

“早上好，科尔。”

===Original===
Lew Kohl looked at the man who had sent him to seven and a half years of hell, and the corner of his thin lips curled.

“Morning, Mr. Reeder.” His eyes flashed across the almost bare surface of the writing-desk on which Reeder’s hands were lightly clasped. “You didn’t expect to see me, I guess?”

“Not so early,” said Reeder in his hushed voice, “but I should have remembered that early rising is one of the good habits which are inculcated by penal servitude.”

===Chinese===
卢·科尔望着这个送他下了七年半地狱的人，薄嘴唇的嘴角翘了起来。

“早，里德先生。”他的目光扫过几乎空空的书桌面——里德的两只手轻轻交叠在上面。“没想到会见到我吧，我猜？”

“没想到这么早，”里德用他那种压低的嗓音说，“不过我本该记得，早起是苦役监教给人的好习惯之一。”

===Original===
He said this in the manner of one bestowing praise for good conduct.

“I suppose you’ve got a pretty good idea of why I have come, eh? I’m a bad forgetter, Reeder, and a man in Dartmoor has time to think.”

The older man lifted his sandy eyebrows, the steel-rimmed glasses on his nose slipped further askew.

===Chinese===
他这话说得像是在表彰品行优良。

“我为什么来，你心里大概有数吧，嗯？我这个人不健忘，里德，蹲达特穆尔的人有的是工夫想事情。”

年长的那位扬起浅色的眉毛，鼻子上那副钢框眼镜滑得更歪了。

===Original===
“That phrase seems familiar,” he said, and the eyebrows lowered in a frown. “Now let me think—it was in a melodrama, of course, but was it *Souls in Harness* or *The Marriage Vow*?”

He appeared genuinely anxious for assistance in solving this problem.

“This is going to be a different kind of play,” said the long-faced Lew through his teeth. “I’m going to get you, Reeder—you can go along and tell your boss, the Public Prosecutor. But I’ll get you sweet! There will be no evidence to swing me. And I’ll get that nice little stocking of yours, Reeder!”

===Chinese===
“这话听着耳熟，”他说，眉毛皱着垂下来，“让我想想——当然是在一出情节剧里，可到底是《羁魂记》（Souls in Harness）还是《婚誓》（The Marriage Vow）？”

他看上去真心实意地盼着有人帮他解开这个难题。

“这回可要换一出戏了，”长脸的卢咬着牙说，“我要收拾你，里德——尽管去告诉你的上司检察总长好了。可我收拾你收拾得舒舒服服！到时候不会有半点证据吊得死我。我还要把你袜筒里那笔可爱的小积蓄弄到手，里德！”

===Original===
The legend of Reeder’s fortune was accepted even by so intelligent a man as Kohl.

“You’ll get my stocking! Dear me, I shall have to go barefooted,” said Mr. Reeder, with a faint show of humour.

“You know what I mean—think that over. Some hour and day you’ll go out, and all Scotland Yard won’t catch me for the killing! I’ve thought it out—”

===Chinese===
里德有笔横财的传说，连科尔这么精明的人也信了。

“你要拿走我的袜筒！天哪，那我只好打赤脚了。”里德先生说，勉强显出一点幽默。

“你知道我什么意思——好好想想吧。不定哪个时辰哪一天你出了门，就算苏格兰场倾巢出动也抓不到我杀人的证据！我都想好了——”

===Original===
“One has time to think in Dartmoor,” murmured Mr. J. G. Reeder encouragingly. “You’re becoming one of the world’s thinkers, Kohl. Do you know Rodin’s masterpiece—a beautiful statue throbbing with life—”

“That’s all.” Lew Kohl rose, the smile still trembling at the corner of his mouth. “Maybe you’ll turn this over in your mind, and in a day or two you won’t be feeling so gay.”

Reeder’s face was pathetic in its sadness. His untidy sandy-grey hair seemed to be standing on end; the large ears, that stood out at right angles to his face, gave the illusion of quivering movement.

===Chinese===
“在达特穆尔有的是工夫想，”J. G. 里德先生鼓励地喃喃道，“你都快成世界的思想家了，科尔。罗丹那件杰作你知道吗——一座栩栩如生、脉博跳动的美丽雕像——”

“就这些。”卢·科尔起身，嘴角那丝笑意还在颤动，“兴许你会翻来覆去想这件事，过一两天，你就乐不起来了。”

里德的脸悲伤得惹人怜悯。他那蓬乱的灰黄色头发根根乍起，两只与脸成直角支棱着的大耳朵，看上去仿佛在颤动。

===Original===
Lew Kohl’s hand was on the doorknob.

“*Womp!*”

It was the sound of a dull weight striking a board; something winged past his cheek, before his eyes a deep hole showed in the wall, and his face was stung by flying grains of plaster. He spun round with a whine of rage.

===Chinese===
卢·科尔的手已经搭上了门钮。

“噗！”

那是一个钝物击进木板的声音；有什么东西贴着他脸颊呼啸而过，他眼前的墙上现出一个深洞，飞溅的灰渣刺得脸生疼。他狂怒地呜咽一声，猛地转过身。

===Original===
Mr. Reeder had a long-barrelled Browning in his hand, with a barrel-shaped silencer over the muzzle, and he was staring at the weapon open-mouthed.

“Now how on earth did that happen?” he asked in wonder.

Lew Kohl stood trembling with rage and fear, his face yellow-white.

===Chinese===
里德先生手里握着一支长枪管的勃朗宁，枪口上套着圆筒状的消音器，他正张着嘴呆呆盯住这件武器。

“咦，这到底是怎么发生的？”他惊异地问。

卢·科尔站在那里，又怒又怕，浑身发抖，脸黄得发白。

===Original===
“You—you swine!” he breathed. “You tried to shoot me!”

Mr. Reeder stared at him over his glasses.

“Good gracious—you think that? Still thinking of killing me, Kohl?”

===Chinese===
“你——你这畜生！”他喘着说，“你想打死我！”

里德先生从眼镜上方盯着他。

“天哪——您这么想？还在琢磨杀我吗，科尔？”

===Original===
Kohl tried to speak but found no words, and, flinging open the door, he strode down the stairs and through the front entrance. His foot was on the first step when something came hurtling past him and crashed to fragments at his feet. It was a large stone vase that had decorated the windowsill of Mr. Reeder’s bedroom. Leaping over the debris of stone and flower mould, he glared up into the surprised face of Mr. J. G. Reeder.

“I’ll get you!” he spluttered.

“I hope you’re not hurt?” asked the man at the window in a tone of concern. “These things happen. Some day and some hour—”

===Chinese===
科尔想说话，却找不出词来，猛地拉开门，大步下楼，冲出正门。他一只脚刚踏上台阶，一件东西贴身呼啸而过，在他脚边摔得粉碎。那是摆在里德先生卧室窗台上的一只大石花瓶。他跳过石块和花土的碎堆，怒视着楼上J. G. 里德先生那张惊讶的脸。

“我饶不了你！”他唾沫四溅地吼道。

“没伤着您吧？”窗口那人关切地问，“这种事难免。不定哪个日子哪个时辰——”

===Original===
As Lew Kohl strode down the street, the detective was still talking.

Mr. Stan Bride was at his morning ablutions when his friend and sometime prison associate came into the little room that overlooked Fitzroy Square.

Stan Bride, who bore no resemblance to anything virginal, being a stout and stumpy man with a huge, red face and many chins, stopped in the act of drying himself and gazed over the edge of the towel.

===Chinese===
卢·科尔大步走上大街时，那侦探还在窗口絮絮说着。

斯坦·布赖德（Stan Bride）先生正在做晨间洗漱，他的朋友、昔日的狱友走进这间俯瞰菲茨罗伊广场的小屋。

斯坦·布赖德这个姓氏（Bride，意谓“新娘”）跟他本人没有半点相干——他是个矮墩墩的胖汉，大红脸，下巴叠了好几层——他正擦着身子，闻声停了手，从毛巾边上瞪过来。

===Original===
“What’s the matter with you?” he asked sharply. “You look as if you’d been chased by a busy. What did you go out so early for?”

Lew told him, and the jovial countenance of his roommate grew longer and longer—

“You poor fish!” he hissed. “To go after Reeder with that stuff! Don’t you think he was waiting for you? Do you suppose he didn’t know the very moment you left the Moor?”

===Chinese===
“你怎么了？”他尖声问，“瞧你这副样子，像叫条子追了一路。一大早出去干什么？”

卢说了，室友那张乐呵呵的脸越拉越长——

“你这可怜虫！”他嘶声道，“揣着那玩意儿去找里德！你以为他不是专候着你的吗？你以为你几时出的达特穆尔，他不清楚到分秒吗？”

===Original===
“I’ve scared him, anyway,” said the other, and Mr. Bride laughed.

“Good scout!” he sneered. “Scare that old person!” (He did not say “person.”) “If he’s as white as you, he is scared! But he’s not. Of course he shot past you—if he’d wanted to shoot you, you’d have been stiff by now. But he didn’t. Thinker, eh—he’s given you somep’n’ to think about.”

“Where that gun came from I don’t—”

===Chinese===
“反正我把他吓着了。”那一位说；布赖德先生笑了。

“好样的！”他讥笑道，“把那位老‘家伙’吓着了！”（他实际用的那个词可没这么斯文。）“他要是跟你一样软，那就真吓着了！可他才不呢。他当然是故意打偏的——真想打你，你这会儿早僵了。可他没有。思想家，嗯——他倒给了你点东西想想。”

“那把枪是打哪儿冒出来的，我——”

===Original===
There was a knock at the door and the two men exchanged glances.

“Who’s there?” asked Bride, and a familiar voice answered.

“It’s that busy from the Yard,” whispered Bride, and opened the door.

===Chinese===
有人敲门，两人交换了一下眼色。

“谁？”布赖德问；一个熟悉的声音答了话。

“是苏格兰场的条子。”布赖德低声说，开了门。

===Original===
The “busy” was Sergeant Allford, C.I.D., an affable and portly man and a detective of some promise.

“Morning, boys—not been to church, Stan?”

Stan grinned politely.

===Chinese===
这位“条子”是刑事部（C.I.D.）的阿尔福德警士，一个和气富态的人，也是前程可期的侦探。

“早啊，二位——没上教堂去，斯坦？”

斯坦客气地咧嘴一笑。

===Original===
“How’s trade, Lew?”

“Not so bad.” The forger was alert, suspicious.

“Come to see you about a gun—got an idea you’re carrying one, Lew—Colt automatic R.7/94318. That’s not right, Lew—guns don’t belong to this country.”

===Chinese===
“买卖如何，卢？”

“还凑合。”伪造犯警觉着，满腹狐疑。

“为一把枪来的——我琢磨着你身上带着一把呢，卢——柯尔特自动手枪，编号 R.7/94318。这可不对，卢——枪不属于这个国家。”

===Original===
“I’ve got no gun,” said Lew sullenly.

Bride had suddenly become an old man, for he also was a convict on licence, and the discovery might send him back to serve his unfinished sentence.

“Will you come a little walk to the station, or will you let me go over you?”

===Chinese===
“我没枪。”卢绷着脸说。

布赖德一下子老了好几岁，因为他也是个假释在外的犯人，真搜出点什么，他没服完的刑期就得接着坐。

“是劳驾跟我到局子里走一趟呢，还是让我搜一搜你？”

===Original===
“Go over me,” said Lew, and put out his arms stiffly whilst the detective rubbed him down.

“I’ll have a look round,” said the detective, and his “look round” was very thorough.

“Must have been mistaken,” said Sergeant Allford. And then, suddenly: “Was that what you chucked into the river as you were walking along the Embankment?”

===Chinese===
“搜吧。”卢说，僵直地张开两臂，由侦探上下摸了一遍。

“我再到四处瞧瞧。”侦探说；他这一“瞧瞧”彻头彻尾。

“准是我弄错了。”阿尔福德警士说。随即突然发问：“你沿着泰晤士河堤走的时候，扔进河里的就是那玩意儿吧？”

===Original===
Lew started. It was the first intimation he had received that he had been “tailed” that morning.

Bride waited till the detective was visible from the window crossing Fitzroy Square; then he turned in a fury on his companion.

“Clever, ain’t you! That old hound knew you had a gun—knew the number. And if Allford had found it you’d have been ‘dragged’ and me too!”

===Chinese===
卢一激灵。这是他头一回知道，那天早晨自己一直被人“盯梢”。

布赖德等到从窗口望得见那侦探穿过菲茨罗伊广场的背影，才转身朝同伴大发雷霆。

“能耐，真能耐！那条老猎犬早知道你有枪——连号码都知道。阿尔福德真搜出来，你就得‘吃官司’，我也得跟着陪绑！”

===Original===
“I threw it in the river,” said Lew sulkily.

“Brains—not many but some!” said Bride, breathing heavily. “You cut out Reeder—he’s hell and poison, and if you don’t know it you’re deaf! Scared him? You big stiff! He’d cut your throat and write a hymn about it.”

“I didn’t know they were tailing me,” growled Kohl; “but I’ll get him! And his money too.”

===Chinese===
“我把它扔河里了。”卢气呼呼地说。

“脑瓜子——不多，总算还有点！”布赖德喘着粗气说，“你离里德远点——他是地狱加砒霜，这都不知道你是聋子！吓着他了？你这个大笨熊！他割了你喉咙，还能就着写首赞美诗。”

“我不知道他们盯着我，”科尔咆哮道，“可我要收拾他！连他的钱一起。”

===Original===
“Get him from another lodging,” said Bride curtly. “A crook I don’t mind, being one; a murderer I don’t mind, but a talking jackass makes me sick. Get his stuff if you can—I’ll bet it’s all invested in real estate, and you can’t lift houses—but don’t talk about it. I like you, Lew, up to a point; you’re miles before the point and out of sight. I don’t like Reeder—I don’t like snakes, but I keep away from the Zoo.”

So Lew Kohl went into new diggings on the top floor of an Italian’s house in Dean Street, and here he had leisure and inclination to brood upon his grievances and to plan afresh the destruction of his enemy. And new plans were needed, for the schemes which had seemed so watertight in the quietude of a Devonshire cell showed daylight through many crevices.

Lew’s homicidal urge had undergone considerable modification. He had been experimented upon by a very clever psychologist—though he never regarded Mr. Reeder in this light, and, indeed, had the vaguest idea as to what the word meant. But there were other ways of hurting Reeder, and his mind fell constantly back to the dream of discovering this peccant detective’s hidden treasure.

===Chinese===
“要收拾他，先搬个地方再说。”布赖德粗声说，“贼我不嫌，我自己就是；杀人犯我也不嫌，可一张嘴的蠢驴叫我恶心。他的东西你搞得到手就搞——我打赌全置成了房产，房子你扛不走——可别到处嚷嚷。我喜欢你，卢，有个限度；你早过限好几英里，都没影了。我不喜欢里德——我也不喜欢蛇，可我从来不进动物园。”

就这样，卢·科尔搬进了迪安街一个意大利人家的顶层新窝，在这里，他有闲也有兴咀嚼自己的怨毒，重新谋划毁灭仇人。新谋划也是非有不可的：那些在德文郡牢房的寂静里显得天衣无缝的计划，如今处处透光。

卢那股杀人冲动已大打折扣。他被人拿一位很高明的心理学家做过的实验摆弄过——尽管他从不这样看里德先生，老实说，连这个词什么意思他都稀里糊涂。可除了杀人还有别的法子能让里德疼，他的心思便一而再、再而三地落回那个美梦上：挖出这个奸诈侦探的藏宝。

===Original===
It was nearly a week later that Mr. Reeder invited himself into the Director’s private sanctum, and that great official listened spellbound while his subordinate offered his outrageous theory about Sir James Tithermite and his dead wife. When Mr. Reeder had finished, the Director pushed back his chair from the table.

“My dear man,” he said, a little irritably, “I can’t possibly give a warrant on the strength of your surmises—not even a search warrant. The story is so fantastic, so incredible, that it would be more at home in the pages of a sensational story than in a Public Prosecutor’s report.”

“It was a wild night, and yet Lady Tithermite was not ill,” suggested the detective gently. “That is a fact to remember, sir.”

===Chinese===
差不多一个星期后，里德先生不请自入，进了检察总长的私人办公室；这位大人物听手下人抛出他那套关于詹姆斯·蒂瑟米特爵士和亡妻的骇人听闻的推论，听得入了迷。里德先生讲完，检察总长把椅子从桌边推开。

“我亲爱的先生，”他有点恼火地说，“凭你这些臆测我绝不能签逮捕令——连搜查令都不行。这故事太离奇、太骇人，登在惊险小说里还差不多，写进检察总长的公文里可不像话。”

“那晚风急浪高，蒂瑟米特爵士夫人却没有不适，”侦探轻声提示，“这是个该记住的事实，长官。”

===Original===
The Director shook his head.

“I can’t do it—not on the evidence,” he said. “I should raise a storm that’d swing me into Whitehall. Can’t you do anything—unofficially?”

Mr. Reeder shook his head.

===Chinese===
检察总长摇了摇头。

“我办不到——就凭这些证据，”他说，“那会掀起一场风暴，把我刮进白厅去。你不能想点办法——以非官方的身份？”

里德先生摇了摇头。

===Original===
“My presence in the neighbourhood has been remarked,” he said primly. “I think it would be impossible to—er—cover up my traces. And yet I have located the place, and could tell you within a few inches—”

Again the Director shook his head.

“No, Reeder,” he said quietly, “the whole thing is sheer deduction on your part. Oh, yes, I know you have a criminal mind—I think you have told me that before. And that is a good reason why I should not issue a warrant. You’re simply crediting this unfortunate man with your ingenuity. Nothing doing!”

===Chinese===
“我那一带露过面，已经被人记下了，”他一板一眼地说，“要想——呃——抹掉行迹，恐怕不可能了。话虽如此，地方我已经找准了，误差不出几英寸——”

检察总长又摇了摇头。

“不行，里德，”他平静地说，“这整件事纯属你的推演。哦，是的，我知道你有一副犯罪头脑——这话你好像跟我说过。而这恰恰是我不该签令状的理由：你不过是把自己的诡诈，记到了这个倒霉蛋的账上。没门儿！”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder sighed and went back to his bureau, not entirely despondent, for there had intruded a new element into his investigations.

Mr. Reeder had been to Maidstone several times during the week, and he had not gone alone; though seemingly unconscious of the fact that he had developed a shadow, for he had seen Lew Kohl on several occasions, and had spent an uncomfortable few minutes wondering whether his experiment had failed.

On the second occasion an idea had developed in the detective’s mind, and if he were a laughing man he would have chuckled aloud when he slipped out of Maidstone station one evening and, in the act of hiring a cab, had seen Lew Kohl negotiating for another.

===Chinese===
里德先生叹了口气，回到自己的科室，倒也不算全然泄气，因为调查里已经闯进了一个新变数。

这一星期里，里德先生去了梅德斯通好几趟，而且并非独来独往；尽管他似乎全然不觉自己身后长了条尾巴——他几次三番瞥见卢·科尔，也难受了好几分钟，拿不准自己那场实验是不是砸了。

第二次碰上时，侦探心里萌生了一个主意；倘若他是爱笑的人，那天傍晚溜出梅德斯通车站、正雇马车时瞥见卢·科尔在讲另一辆车的价钱，他准会笑出声来。

===Original===
Mr. Bride was engaged in the tedious but necessary practice of so cutting a pack of cards that the ace of diamonds remained at the bottom, when his former co-lodger burst in upon him, and there was a light of triumph in Lew’s cold eye which brought Mr. Bride’s heart to his boots.

“I’ve got him!” said Lew.

Bride put aside the cards and stood up.

===Chinese===
布赖德先生正埋头做那件乏味却必需的功课——把一副牌切得让方块A稳稳垫底——昔日同居一室的伙伴破门而入，卢那只冷淡的眼睛里闪着得胜的光，让布赖德先生的心一路沉到靴底。

“我逮着他了！”卢说。

布赖德把牌推到一边，站起身。

===Original===
“Got who?” he asked coldly. “And if it’s killing, you needn’t answer, but get out!”

“There’s no killing.”

Lew sat down squarely at the table, his hands in his pockets, a real smile on his face.

===Chinese===
“逮着谁了？”他冷冷地问，“要是杀人，不必回答，滚出去！”

“不杀人。”

卢端端正正在桌边坐下，两手插兜，脸上挂着真心实意的笑。

===Original===
“I’ve been trailing Reeder for a week, and that fellow wants some trailing!”

“Well?” asked the other, when he paused dramatically.

“I’ve found his stocking!”

===Chinese===
“我盯了里德一整个礼拜的梢——这主儿，可真不好盯！”

“然后呢？”见他卖关子似的顿住，那一位问。

“我找到他的袜筒了！”

===Original===
Bride scratched his chin, and was half convinced.

“You never have?”

Lew nodded.

===Chinese===
布赖德挠着下巴，信了一半。

“你可别是吹牛？”

卢点了点头。

===Original===
“He’s been going to Maidstone a lot lately, and driving to a little village about five miles out. There I always lost him. But the other night, when he came back to the station to catch the last train, he slipped into the waiting-room and I found a place where I could watch him. What do you think he did?”

Mr. Bride hazarded no suggestion.

“He opened his bag,” said Lew impressively, “and took out a wad of notes as thick as that! He’d been drawing on his bank! I trailed him up to London. There’s a restaurant on the station and he went in to get a cup of coffee, with me keeping well out of his sight. As he came out of the restaurant he took out his handkerchief and wiped his mouth. He didn’t see the little book that dropped, but I did. I was scared sick that somebody else would see it, or that he’d wait long enough to find it himself. But he went out of the station and I got that book before you could say ‘knife.’ Look!”

===Chinese===
“他近来老往梅德斯通跑，再开车去一个离城五英里左右的小村子。到了那儿我总把他跟丢。可前几天夜里，他回车站赶末班车，溜进了候车室，我找了个能盯着他的地方。你猜他干什么来着？”

布赖德先生没猜。

“他打开提包，”卢加重了语气，“掏出一沓票子，有这么厚！他是从银行取的钱！我一路跟到伦敦。车站里有家餐馆，他进去喝了杯咖啡，我躲得远远的不叫他看见。他从餐馆出来，掏手帕擦嘴。那本掉出来的小册子他没瞧见，我可见着了。我吓得魂都飞了，生怕叫旁人看见，又怕他磨蹭得久了自己回头找着。可他出了车站，说时迟那时快，我抢先把那本子捞到了手。瞧！”

===Original===
It was a well-worn little notebook, covered with faded red morocco. Bride put out his hand to take it.

“Wait a bit,” said Lew. “Are you in this with me fifty-fifty, because I want some help?”

Bride hesitated.

===Chinese===
那是一本用旧了的小笔记本，包着褪色的红色摩洛哥羊皮。布赖德伸手要拿。

“慢着，”卢说，“这票买卖你跟我对半分不分？我需要个帮手。”

布赖德犹豫了。

===Original===
“If it’s just plain thieving, I’m with you,” he said.

“Plain thieving—and sweet,” said Lew exultantly, and pushed the book across the table.

For the greater part of the night they sat together talking in low tones, discussing impartially the methodical bookkeeping of Mr. J. G. Reeder and his exceeding dishonesty.

===Chinese===
“只要是不沾血的光棍买卖，我就入伙。”他说。

“光棍买卖——甜得很。”卢得意洋洋地说，把本子推过桌面。

大半个夜里，两人凑在一处低声交谈，公允地讨论着J. G. 里德先生井井有条的记账法和他那登峰造极的欺诈。

===Original===
The Monday night was wet. A storm blew up from the southwest, and the air was filled with falling leaves as Lew and his companion footed the five miles which separated them from the village. Neither carried any impedimenta that was visible, yet under Lew’s waterproof coat was a kit of tools of singular ingenuity, and Mr. Bride’s coat pockets were weighted down with the sections of a powerful jemmy.

They met nobody in their walk, and the church bell was striking eleven when Lew gripped the bars of the South Lodge gates, pulled himself up to the top and dropped lightly on the other side. He was followed by Mr. Bride, who, in spite of his bulk, was a singularly agile man. The ruined lodge showed in the darkness, and they passed through the creaking gates to the door and Lew flashed his lantern upon the keyhole before he began manipulation with the implements which he had taken from his kit.

The door was opened in ten minutes and a few seconds later they stood in a low-roofed little room, the principal feature of which was a deep, grateless fireplace. Lew took off his mackintosh and stretched it over the window before he spread the light in his lamp, and, kneeling down, brushed the debris from the hearth, examining the joints of the big stone carefully.

===Chinese===
那个星期一晚上潮湿多雨。西南边暴起一场风雨，满天飞着落叶，卢和他的同伴徒步走完把他们同那村子隔开的五英里。两人身上都看不见什么行头，可卢的防水外套底下掖着一整套灵巧得邪门的工具，布赖德先生的外套口袋里，坠着几截威力强大的撬棍。

一路没遇见人。教堂钟敲十一点时，卢攀住南门房大门的栏杆，引体翻上顶端，轻轻落到另一侧。布赖德先生跟了上去——别看他块头大，身手利落得出奇。破败的门房在黑暗里显出轮廓，两人穿过吱呀作响的园门来到屋门前，卢先用提灯照了照锁孔，才从工具包里取出家伙开始摆弄。

十分钟后门开了，再过几秒钟，两人已站在一间低顶的小屋里，屋里最扎眼的是一座深深的、不带炉栅的壁炉。卢脱下胶布雨衣遮严窗户，才拧亮提灯，随即跪下来扫去炉床的碎屑，细细察看那块大石板的接缝。

===Original===
“This work’s been botched,” he said. “Anybody could see that.”

He put the claw of the jemmy into a crack and levered up the stone, and it moved slightly. Stopping only to dig a deeper crevice with a chisel and hammer he thrust the claw of the jemmy farther down. The stone came up above the edge of the floor and Bride slipped the chisel underneath.

“Now together,” grunted Lew.

===Chinese===
“这活儿是外行干的，”他说，“谁都看得出来。”

他把撬棍的爪尖插进一道裂缝，往上一撬，石板动了动。他只用凿子和锤子开出更深的口子，便把爪尖又往下插。石板翘出地面，布赖德把凿子塞了进去。

“一起来。”卢咕哝一声。

===Original===
They got their fingers beneath the hearthstone and with one heave hinged it up. Lew picked up the lamp and, kneeling down, flashed a light into the dark cavity. And then:

“Oh, my God!” he shrieked.

A second later two terrified men rushed from the house into the drive. And a miracle had happened, for the gates were open and a dark figure stood squarely before them.

===Chinese===
两人把手指抠进炉床石底下，一使劲，把它整个掀了起来。卢提起灯，跪下去，一道光照进黑洞洞的坑里。接着——

“哦，老天爷！”他失声惨叫。

一秒钟后，两个魂飞魄散的人从屋里冲上车道。奇迹出现了：大门敞开着，一个黑黢黢的身影端端正正立在门前。

===Original===
“Put up your hands, Kohl!” said a voice, and hateful as it was to Lew Kohl, he could have fallen on the neck of Mr. Reeder.

At twelve o’clock that night Sir James Tithermite was discussing matters with his bride-to-be: the stupidity of her lawyer, who wished to safeguard her fortune, and his own cleverness and foresight in securing complete freedom of action for the girl who was to be his wife.

“These blackguards think of nothing but their fees,” he began, when his footman came in unannounced, and behind him the Chief Constable of the county and a man he remembered seeing before.

===Chinese===
“举起手来，科尔！”一个声音说。这声音在卢·科尔听来可恶至极，他却恨不得扑进里德先生怀里，搂着他的脖子哭一场。

那天夜里十二点，詹姆斯·蒂瑟米特爵士正同他未过门的太太商量事情：数落她那位想保全她财产的律师多么愚蠢，夸耀自己多有先见之明，为即将成为他妻子的姑娘争得了完全的行动自由。

“这些恶棍满脑子只有他们的收费。”他刚开口，跟班没通报就走了进来，身后跟着郡警察局长，还有一个他记得见过的人。

===Original===
“Sir James Tithermite?” said the Chief Constable unnecessarily, for he knew Sir James very well.

“Yes, Colonel, what is it?” asked the baronet, his face twitching.

“I am taking you into custody on a charge of wilfully murdering your wife, Eleanor Mary Tithermite.”

===Chinese===
“詹姆斯·蒂瑟米特爵士？”警察局长这句问话纯属多余，他跟詹姆斯爵士熟得很。

“是我，上校，什么事？”从男爵问，脸上的肌肉抽动着。

“你因蓄意谋杀你的妻子埃莉诺·玛丽·蒂瑟米特，被逮捕了。”

---

===Original===
“The whole thing turned upon the question as to whether Lady Tithermite was a good or a bad sailor,” explained J. G. Reeder to his chief. “If she were a bad sailor, it was unlikely that she would be on the ship, even for five minutes, without calling for the stewardess. The stewardess did not see her ladyship, nor did anybody on board, for the simple reason that she was not on board! She was murdered within the grounds of the Manor; her body was buried beneath the hearthstone of the old lodge, and Sir James continued his journey by car to Dover, handing over his packages to a porter and telling him to take them to his cabin before he returned to put the car into the hotel garage. He had timed his arrival so that he passed on board with a crowd of passengers from the boat train, and nobody knew whether he was alone or whether he was accompanied, and, for the matter of that, nobody cared. The purser gave him his key, and he put the baggage, including his wife’s hat, into the cabin, paid the porter and dismissed him. Officially, Lady Tithermite was on board, for he surrendered her ticket to the collector and received her landing voucher. And then he discovered she had disappeared. The ship was searched, but of course the unfortunate lady was not found. As I remarked before—”

“You have a criminal mind,” said the Director good-humouredly. “Go on, Reeder.”

===Chinese===
“整桩案子的关键，就在蒂瑟米特爵士夫人到底晕不晕船这一点上，”J. G. 里德对上司解释道，“她若是晕船，就不可能在船上待哪怕五分钟而不叫女乘务员。女乘务员没见过夫人，船上任何人也没见过，道理简单得很——她根本不在船上！她是在庄园地界之内被害的；尸体埋在旧门房的炉床石下面，詹姆斯爵士则独自开车继续赶往多佛，把行李交给一个搬运工，让他先送进舱里，自己回头再把车存进酒店车库。他把到达的时间掐得恰到好处，跟船务火车下来的一大群旅客一齐登船，谁也不知道他是独自一人还是有伴，说实在的，也没人在乎。事务长把钥匙给了他，他把行李——包括他妻子的帽子——安顿进舱房，付了脚钱，打发走搬运工。在案卷上，蒂瑟米特爵士夫人是在船上的，因为他把她的船票交给了收票员，领了她的登岸证。然后他才发现她失踪了。全船都搜遍了，那位不幸的夫人当然没有找到。正如我从前说过的——”

“你天生一副犯罪头脑，”检察总长和颜悦色地说，“接着说，里德。”

===Original===
“Having this queer and objectionable trait, I saw how very simple a matter it was to give the illusion that the lady was on board, and I decided that, if the murder was committed, it must have been within a few miles of the house. And then the local builder told me that he had given Sir James a little lesson in the art of mixing mortar. And the local blacksmith told me that the gate had been damaged, presumably by Sir James’s car—I had seen the broken rods and all I wanted to know was when the repairs were effected. That she was beneath the hearth in the lodge I was certain. Without a search warrant it was impossible to prove or disprove my theory, and I myself could not conduct a private investigation without risking the reputation of our department—if I may say ‘our,’” he said apologetically.

The Director was thoughtful.

===Chinese===
“既然我有这么个古怪讨嫌的毛病，我一眼便看出，要制造夫人在船上的假象是何等轻巧的事；于是断定，凶案若已发生，必然在离宅子几英里之内。后来，本地的建材商告诉我，他给詹姆斯爵士上过一小课，教他拌砂浆的手艺。本地的铁匠告诉我，那扇大门坏过，多半是叫詹姆斯爵士的车撞的——断栏杆我早看见了，我只想知道修葺是在什么时候。她就在门房的炉床底下，这一点我有把握。没有搜查令，我的推论便无法证实也无法证伪；而我自己若去私下查探，又难免拿咱们公署的名声冒险——容我说一声‘咱们’。”他抱歉地说。

检察总长沉吟起来。

===Original===
“Of course, you induced this man Kohl to dig up the hearth by pretending you had money buried there. I presume you revealed that fact in your notebook? But why on earth did he imagine that you had a hidden treasure?”

Mr. Reeder smiled sadly.

“The criminal mind is a peculiar thing,” he said, with a sigh. “It harbours illusions and fairy stories. Fortunately, I understand that mind. As I have often said—”

===Chinese===
“这么说，是你冒充钱埋在那儿，哄这个科尔去掀的炉床。我料想，这层意思你在那个笔记本里透出来了？可他到底凭什么认定你藏着一笔宝？”

里德先生凄然一笑。

“犯罪头脑是种古怪的东西，”他叹口气说，“它豢养着幻觉和童话。所幸的是，我懂得那副头脑。正如我常说——”

## The Troupe / 剧团

===Original===
There was a quietude and sedateness about the Public Prosecutor’s office which completely harmonised with the tastes and inclinations of Mr. J. G. Reeder. For he was a gentleman who liked to work in an office where the ticking of a clock was audible and the turning of a paper produced a gentle disturbance.

He had before him one morning the typewritten catalogue of Messrs. Willoby, the eminent estate agents, and he was turning the leaves with a thoughtful expression. The catalogue was newly arrived, a messenger having only a few minutes before placed the portfolio on his desk.

Presently he smoothed down a leaf and read again the flattering description of a fairly unimportant property, and his scrutiny was patently a waste of time, for, scrawled on the margin of the sheet in red ink was the word “Let,” which meant that “Riverside Bower” was not available for hire. The ink was smudged, and “Let” had been obviously written that morning.

“Humph!” said Mr. Reeder.

===Chinese===
检察总长公署里有一种静谧与沉静，与J. G. 里德先生的趣味和脾性完全相合。因为他这位先生，就喜欢在一间听得见钟摆嘀嗒、翻动一页纸都会引起轻微骚动的办公室里做事。

一天上午，他面前摆着著名地产商威洛比公司（Messrs. Willoby）打印的房产目录，他正若有所思地翻着页。目录是新到的——几分钟前，听差才把文件夹放到他桌上。

不多时，他抚平一页，又读起一处相当不起眼的房产那段天花乱坠的描述来；他这般细看分明是白费工夫，因为纸页边缘有红笔潦草写下的“已租”二字——意思是“河畔小筑”（Riverside Bower）租不出去了。墨迹晕开，“已租”显然是那天早上刚写上的。

“哼！”里德先生说。

===Original===
He was interested for many reasons. In the heat of July riverside houses are at a premium: at the beginning of November they are somewhat of a drug on the market. And transatlantic visitors do not as a rule hire riverside cottages in a month which is chiefly distinguished by mists, rain and general discomfort.

> Two reception: two bedrooms: bath, large dry cellars, lawn to river, small skiff and punt. Gas and electric light. Three guineas weekly or would be let for six months at 2 guineas.

He pulled his table telephone towards him and gave the agents’ number.

“Let, is it—dear me! To an American gentleman? When will it be available?”

===Chinese===
他感兴趣，缘由不止一端。七月酷暑，河畔宅邸奇货可居；十一月初，这东西在市场上就有点滞销了。何况大西洋对岸来的客人，一般也不会挑一个以雾、雨和普遍的不适著称的月份，去租河边的小屋。

> 起居室两间：卧室两间：浴室，干燥大地下室，草坪直通河边，附小划子和方头平底船。煤气兼电灯。每周三几尼，若租半年，两几尼亦可。

他把桌上的电话拉到跟前，拨了地产公司的号码。

“租出去了，是吗——天哪！租给了一位美国先生？什么时候能空出来？”

===Original===
The new tenant had taken the house for a month. Mr. Reeder was even more intrigued, though his interest in the “American gentleman” was not quite as intensive as the American gentleman’s interest in Mr. Reeder.

When the great Art Lomer came on a business trip from Canada to London, a friend and admirer carried him off one day to see the principal sight of London.

“He generally comes out at lunch time,” said the friend, who was called “Cheep,” because his name was Sparrow.

===Chinese===
新房客租了一个月。里德先生愈发好奇了，不过他对那位“美国先生”的兴趣，还不及美国先生对里德先生的兴趣来得浓烈。

话说大名鼎鼎的亚特·洛默（Art Lomer）从加拿大来伦敦公干，一天，一位景仰他的朋友拉他去瞻仰伦敦头一号名胜。

“他一般午饭时候出来，”朋友说。此人外号“叽叽”（Cheep），因为他姓斯帕罗（Sparrow）——麻雀。

===Original===
Mr. Lomer looked up and down Whitehall disparagingly, for he had seen so many cities of the world that none seemed as good as the others.

“There he is!” whispered Cheep, though there was no need for mystery or confidence.

A middle-aged man had come out of one of the narrow doorways of a large grey building. On his head was a high, flat-crowned hat, his body was tightly encased in a black frock coat. A weakish man with yellowy-white side-whiskers and eyeglasses, that were nearer to the end than the beginning of his nose.

===Chinese===
洛默先生把白厅上下打量了一番，一脸不屑——他见过的世面城市太多，哪一座看着都不如传闻那么好。

“他在那儿！”叽叽耳语道，尽管实在犯不着这么神神秘秘。

一个中年男人刚从一栋灰色大楼的窄门洞里走出来。头上端着一顶高高的平顶帽，身子紧紧裹在黑色礼服大衣里。这是个有点孱弱的人，蓄着灰白色的连鬓胡子，鼻梁上的夹鼻眼镜快滑到鼻尖，离鼻根倒远。

===Original===
“Him?” demanded the amazed Art.

“Him,” said the other, incorrectly but with emphasis.

“Is that the kind of guy you’re scared about? You’re crazy. Why, that man couldn’t catch a cold! Now, back home in T’ronto—”

===Chinese===
“就他？”亚特惊得直问。

“就他。”对方答道，语法不通，语气却斩钉截铁。

“你们怕的就是这号人物？你们疯了。嘿，那人连感冒都传染不上！要搁我们老家多伦多——”

===Original===
Art was proud of his home town, and in that spirit of expansiveness which paints even the unpleasant features of One’s Own with the most attractive hues, he had even a good word to say about the Royal Canadian Police—a force which normally, and in a local atmosphere, he held in the greatest detestation.

Art “operated”—he never employed a baser word—from Toronto, which, by its proximity to Buffalo and the United States border, gave him certain advantages. He had once “operated” in Canada itself, but his line at that period being robbery of a kind which is necessarily accompanied by assault, he had found himself facing a Canadian magistrate, and a Canadian magistrate wields extraordinary powers. Art had been sent down for five years and, crowning horror, was ordered to receive twenty-five lashes with a whip which has nine tails, each one of which hurts. Thereafter he cut out violence and confined himself to the formation of his troupe—and Art Lomer’s troupe was famous from the Atlantic to the Pacific.

He had been plain Arthur Lomer when he was rescued from a London gutter and a career of crime and sent to Canada, the charitable authorities being under the impression that Canada was rather short on juvenile criminals. By dint of great artfulness, good stage management and a natural aptitude for acquiring easy money, he had gained for himself a bungalow on the islands, a flat in Church Street, a six-cylinder car and a New England accent which would pass muster in almost any place except New England.

===Chinese===
亚特以家乡为荣，怀着那种把“自家人”连缺点也涂得金光闪闪的豪兴，连加拿大皇家警察他都夸上了——这支部队平日里、在本地氛围中，本是他最最深恶痛绝的。

亚特的“营生”——再难听的词他从来不用——以多伦多为根据地，那地方挨着布法罗和美国边界，有种种便利。他有一回在加拿大本土“营生”，可他那阵子的路子是抢劫——难免动手伤人的那种——于是站到了加拿大治安法官面前，而加拿大治安法官的权力大得吓人。亚特被判了五年，更可怕的还判了二十五鞭，用一种带九条皮梢的鞭子，条条都咬肉。从此他戒了暴力，专心经营他的剧团——亚特·洛默的剧团，从大西洋到太平洋，无人不知。

想当年，他被从伦敦的阴沟和犯罪生涯里打捞出来、送去加拿大时，还只是个平平无奇的阿瑟·洛默（Arthur Lomer）——慈善当局以为加拿大正缺少年犯。凭着一身狡黠、出色的舞台调度和赚轻松钱的天赋，他给自己挣下了岛上的一栋平房、丘奇街的一套公寓、一辆六缸汽车，还有一口新英格兰口音——这口音到哪儿都蒙得过去，除了新英格兰。

===Original===
“I’ll tell the world you fellows want waking up! So that’s your Reeder? Well, if Canada and the United States was full of goats like him, I’d pack more dollars in one month than Hollywood pays Chaplin in ten years. Yes, sir. Listen, does that guy park a clock?”

His guide was a little dazed.

“Does he wear a watch? Sure!”

Mr. Art Lomer nodded.

===Chinese===
“我明说了吧，你们这帮家伙该醒醒了！这就是你们的里德？得了，要是加拿大和美国净是这种呆羊，我一个月搂的美元，比好莱坞十年付给卓别林的还多。没错，先生。听着，那家伙身上揣不揣表？”

向导有点发懵。

“他戴不戴表？当然戴！”

亚特·洛默先生点了点头。

===Original===
“Wait—I’ll bring it back to you in five minutes—I’m goin’ to show you sump’n’.”

It was the maddest fool thing he had ever done in his life; he was in London on business, and was jeopardising a million dollars for the sake of the cheap applause of a man for whose opinion he did not care a cent.

Mr. Reeder was standing nervously on the sidewalk, waiting for what he described as “the vehicular traffic” to pass, when a strange man bumped against him.

===Chinese===
“等着——五分钟我就把它给你拿回来——我要给你露一手。”

这是他此生干过的最疯最蠢的事：他在伦敦是来办正事的，却为了一个他半分也瞧不上眼的家伙的廉价喝彩，拿一百万美元去冒险。

里德先生正紧张兮兮地站在人行道边，等他口中所谓的“机动车辆”过完，一个陌生男人撞到了他身上。

===Original===
“Excuse me, sir,” said the stranger.

“Not at all,” murmured Mr. Reeder. “My watch is five minutes fast—you can see the correct time by Big Ben.”

Mr. Lomer felt a hand dip into his coat pocket, saw, like one hypnotised, the watch go back to J. G. Reeder’s pocket.

===Chinese===
“对不住，先生。”陌生人说。

“不客气，”里德先生喃喃道，“我的表快五分钟——正确时间可以看大本钟。”

洛默先生觉出一只手探进自己的外套口袋，随即像被催眠了一般，眼睁睁看着那块表回到了J. G. 里德的口袋里。

===Original===
“Over here for long?” asked Mr. Reeder pleasantly.

“Why—yes.”

“It’s a nice time of the year.” Mr. Reeder removed his eyeglasses, rubbed them feebly on his sleeve and replaced them crookedly. “But the country is not quite so beautiful as Canada in the fall. How is Leoni?”

===Chinese===
“这趟过来待得久吗？”里德先生和气地问。

“呃——挺久。”

“这个季节正好。”里德先生摘下夹鼻眼镜，用袖子有气无力地擦了擦，又歪着架了回去。“不过这地方到底不如秋天的加拿大美。莱奥尼（Leoni）近来可好？”

===Original===
Art Lomer did not faint; he swayed slightly and blinked hard, as if he were trying to wake up. Leoni was the proprietor of that little restaurant in Buffalo which was the advanced base of those operations so profitable to Art and his friends.

“Leoni? Say, mister—”

“And the troupe—are they performing in England or—er—resting? I think that is the word.”

===Chinese===
亚特·洛默没有晕倒；他晃了晃，使劲眨眼，像要把自己眨醒。莱奥尼是布法罗那家小餐馆的老板——那馆子，正是亚特和他一伙人那桩桩获利丰厚的营生的前哨基地。

“莱奥尼？我说，先生——”

“还有剧团——他们是在英国演出呢，还是——呃——歇着？我想是这个词。”

===Original===
Art gaped at the other. On Mr. Reeder’s face was an expression of solicitude and inquiry. It was as though the well-being of the troupe was an absorbing preoccupation.

“Say—listen—” began Art huskily.

Before he could collect his thoughts, Reeder was crossing the road with nervous glances left and right, his umbrella gripped tightly in his hand.

===Chinese===
亚特张着嘴瞪着他。里德先生脸上挂着一副关切探询的神情，仿佛剧团的安危冷暖是他心头放不下的头等大事。

“我说——听我说——”亚特哑着嗓子开口。

不等他理清思路，里德已经左顾右盼、提心吊胆地穿过马路去了，雨伞紧紧攥在手里。

===Original===
“I guess I’m crazy,” said Mr. Lomer, and walked back very slowly to where he had left his anxious cicerone.
